## Open questions

Each open decision has a proposal. The first three are needed before Phase 1 starts; the rest can wait until the phase that needs them.

| Question | Proposal | Needed by | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Which cluster hosts the Phase 1 test deployment? | A non-production cluster you choose, reached by port-forward; CI uses kind. | Phase 1 | Open |
| Where are images published? | GitHub Container Registry under ucesys, private until the repo goes public. | Phase 1 | Open |
| Which Kubernetes versions does CI test? | Every minor release still maintained upstream, today 1.35 to 1.37. | Phase 1 | Open |
| May the ServiceAccount read Secrets, for Secret keys and sizes and for Helm releases? | Yes, filtered on the server (P2-07), with a chart setting to turn it off. | Phase 2 | Settled at the Phase 1 review: yes, as proposed; `secrets.read` in the chart turns it off. |
| How is per-person access enforced? | Impersonation, so Kubernetes RBAC decides. 221B needs the right to impersonate on every cluster, and sign-in groups must match the groups RBAC uses. | Phase 3 | Built as proposed in Phase 3, with access reviews deciding what each person sees of the watched data and impersonation for live reads; `auth.kubernetes.userPrefix` and `groupPrefix` match the API server's. To confirm at the Phase 3 review. |
| Where do settings live (connections, fleets, Sherlock settings)? | Kubernetes objects in 221B's namespace: Secrets for credentials, ConfigMaps for the rest. No database. | Phase 3 | Built as proposed in Phase 3: one Secret per connection, a ConfigMap for the settings. To confirm at the Phase 3 review. |
| Do clusters behind firewalls need an agent that connects out to 221B? | Not in this plan; decide after Phase 3 from real installations. | After Phase 3 | Open |
| Which model does Sherlock use, and how long are conversations kept? | The admin picks the model. Conversations last for the session; the audit log keeps questions and tool calls. | Phase 5 | Built as proposed in Phase 5: Claude Opus 5.5 by default, any model in Settings; conversations kept on the server for the session (ADR-42). To confirm at the Phase 5 review. |
| Should Watson ever run kubectl apply, edit or exec, or helm install and upgrade? | Not in the first release; exec carries the most risk. | Phase 6 | Exec settled at the Toolbox design (October 25, 2026): shells, commands and forwards arrive as sessions through the Toolbox ([toolbox.md](toolbox.md)), each a Watson card; Watson's command line still refuses exec, apply and edit. Apply, edit and helm install and upgrade stay open. |
| How do outside contributors sign off on their work once the repo is public? | A Developer Certificate of Origin sign-off on each commit. | Phase 7 | Settled in Phase 7: a DCO sign-off (`git commit -s`) on every commit of a pull request from a fork, checked by CI's *Sign-off* job; the maintainers' own branches need none (CONTRIBUTING.md). |

## Phase 10.1: maintenance after 0.10.0

Asked for on October 28, 2026, after the 0.10.0 release; the patch release 0.10.1.

| ID | Requirement | Acceptance |
|---|---|---|
| M-01 | After a Helm change deploys, a drift check made before it is forgotten: the Helm table and the drawer show no stale *drifted* (HA-09 amended). | Upgrade a release through Watson; the drawer shows the fresh state without *Check again*. |
| M-02 | The Nodes table has no Problems column; Status sorts problems first and the drawer lists them (P1-13 amended). | The column is gone from the table, the Columns menu and the export. |
| M-03 | The release wrench says **Upgrade…**; in History each revision has a wrench whose menu offers **Diff…** and **Roll back to this revision…**, the latter handing `helm rollback NAME REV -n NS` to Watson as a card to approve; on the current revision it is disabled (*this revision runs now*), and where there is no Watson or no Admin role it says why. | An older revision's menu makes a card with that command; the current one's item is disabled with the reason. |
| M-04 | A pod, a node, a volume claim or a volume with an error or a warning offers **Troubleshoot…** first in its wrench menu (the drawer's, and the table's for pods and nodes), with the problem as its hint: Sherlock is asked for a troubleshooting report, shown above the drawer. Where Sherlock is off the item is disabled with the reason. A healthy object has no such item. | A failing pod's menu starts with Troubleshoot…; a healthy one's does not have it. |
| M-05 | A release whose deployed revision is not the source's last deploy is **behind the source**: the record's commit is not in the revision's description as the console writes it, or the chart versions differ, or the record has no commit yet (a rollback, a `helm upgrade` by hand, a source set on an existing release and not deployed). The header shows a warn pill, a callout says why and what to do, and the table's Drift column says *behind the source* (*drifted · behind the source* when both). | After a rollback the pill and the callout show; after Upgrade… from the source they go. |
| M-06 | The values file is edited in a YAML editor: line numbers, highlighting in the theme's colours, Tab indents two spaces, and the parser's errors underlined and named. Values that do not parse are not saved (the drawer says *Not saved: …*; the dialog's Install waits). | A stray tab or an unclosed quote is underlined and named; fixed, the save goes through. |
| M-07 | Typing a repository URL into the new-application form (and leaving the field) looks the repository over on the server: the charts in it (Chart.yaml within four directories, a chart's own charts/ left out; the README's chart first, else the shallowest) fill the chart path, the default branch fills the branch, and the README's `helm install` example fills the release name, the namespace, *create the namespace*, the `--set` parameters and the values file it names (or the first fenced YAML block whose keys the chart's values.yaml has). Only empty fields are filled; several charts offer a choice. A private repository's token is used for the look and kept nowhere. | github.com/helm/examples fills `charts/hello-world`; the fixture's README fills name, namespace, parameters and values. |

Round 2, asked for on October 29, 2026:

| ID | Requirement | Acceptance |
|---|---|---|
| R2-01 | The Namespaces tab has no footer about cluster-scoped objects. | The text is gone. |
| R2-02 | The Nodes tab has the fleets on its left: each one filters the table (`#/nodes?fleet=`, several at once, *none* for the nodes in no fleet), and an administrator makes, changes and deletes fleets there with the Fleets tab's editor. | Picking a fleet narrows the table to its nodes; New opens the editor; the pencil edits; the editor's Delete removes it. |
| R2-03 | The Fleets tab is optional: *Administration › Settings › General › Fleets page* shows or hides it for everyone (`fleetsPage`, default shown). Fleets are kept from the Nodes tab either way. | Hidden, the tab leaves the bar; the Nodes tab's fleet list stays. |
| R2-04 | **Standing approvals.** With *Administration › Settings › Watson › Standing approvals* above *nothing*, Watson's dock offers *Approve all for…* (5 minutes to 4 hours, up to the limit): for that long, every card the person could approve is approved as soon as its plan is ready: their own (the first approval) and, under the two-person rule, other people's (the second). Every other rule still applies (read-only, two people, node shells), the audit log has the grant, its end and each approval in the person's name, and *Stop* ends it. A grant stands only while its holder does: it is read again before each approval, and ends when they sign out or are disabled. | With the limit at 30 minutes, choosing 5 minutes and pressing *Approve all* runs a cordon card with no other click, approved by the person; choosing a length alone grants nothing; with the limit at nothing the control is absent and the API refuses. |
| R2-05 | **What Sherlock knows.** *Settings › Sherlock* has the administrators' notes about their infrastructure (up to 16 KB, for every cluster), added to Sherlock's instructions after the cluster's description and trusted over what it works out; *Show the instructions* shows the built-in block (read-only) and the cluster's block with the notes as typed; *Test* asks Sherlock one question with the notes as typed, saved or not, and shows the answer. | The notes appear in the cluster block; the test answers; a question after Save carries them. |
| R2-06 | The Source card has a **Save** button (and Enter saves): the status says *Not saved yet* until it is saved, so setting a source on an existing release is never in doubt, and the wrench's Upgrade… follows at once. | Typing a repository and a branch and pressing Save shows *Saved*; Upgrade… is enabled. |
| R2-07 | A token for a private repository is given on the Source card and in the new-application form, kept in the record's Secret and never shown again. A token belongs to its repository: moving an application to another one removes the saved token unless a new one comes with the change, and the page says so. | The card has the field; the record says a token is kept; a change of repository without a token leaves none, and says so. |
| R2-08 | **A second values file.** The repository's look (M-07) lists the chart's other YAML files (its directory and one down; templates, charts, crds and ci left out). The new-application form and the Source card offer them as a *second values file*, remembered with the record and read at the ref on every deploy; the Values tab still overrides it. | The fixture's `values-staging.yaml` is offered; chosen, its values apply under the Values tab's. |
| R2-09 | **Order and the chart's defaults.** Values apply lowest first: the chart's `values.yaml`, the second values file, the Values tab, then the parameters (`--set`). *Ignore the chart's values.yaml* leaves the first out. | Compose lays replicas from the file, the image from the Values tab and the parameter last; ignored, the chart's values are empty. |
| R2-10 | **Drift explained.** Each drifted object carries a unified diff of the fields the manifest sets against the cluster's (defaults and status left out; a Secret's values as sizes). The header's drift line has *what changed*, and the deployed revision's wrench in History has *What drifted…*; both open a dialog listing each changed or missing object with its fields and diff and what drift is. | A scaled Deployment shows `-  replicas: 1` / `+  replicas: 5`; a deleted object is *missing*. |
| R2-11 | **Fleets for deploys.** A fleet's editor takes a *deploy label* (`key=value`, offered from its own labels); the new-application form and the Setup card (called Source until October 30, 2026) offer *Deploy to the fleet* among the fleets that have one, and the deploy adds `nodeSelector.<key>=<value>` as the last parameter. The record keeps the fleet and the label at save time; the fleet's label follows on the next save. On the Setup card the select is on a row of its own, under a rule, and always shown: with no fleet labelled it is disabled and says *No fleet has a label configured*. | A fleet with `fleet=edge` deploys with that nodeSelector; a fleet without a label is not offered; with none labelled the select is disabled with the note. |

## Phase 10.2: maintenance after 0.10.1

Asked for on October 30, 2026, after the 0.10.1 release; the patch release 0.10.2.

- **P7-09, leader reads** (ADR-70): the flicker of CPU and memory in use on the Helm release page with two replicas, and every replica reading every cluster. Built: `replicas.warmStandby` in the chart (`WARM_STANDBY`, `-warm-standby`), `leader.Activity` driving each connection's watches and pollers (`kube.Cluster.RunWatches` and `RunPollers`, the store polling only while active), every `/api/v1/` route but the session and account ones forwarded to the leader, a cold standby ready once it knows the leader, `b221_leader` in the metrics, the role checks on the leader only, and a cold-standby pass in `hack/ha-kind.sh`.

Asked for later the same day, seven more, built on the same branch:

- **M2-01, Audit log page.** Administration gains **Audit log** (SuperAdmin only; offered with sign-in off): the newest events the console recorded, who did what and when, with a word search, an action filter and an outcome filter, a Refresh button and a JSON download of what is shown. The console keeps the newest 2,000 events (at most about 600 KiB) under `audit-log.json` in the settings ConfigMap, every replica adding what it recorded every couple of seconds over the version it read (ADR-71); the stream on standard output and the webhook stay the full record. A console without the ConfigMap keeps them in memory and says so on the page. | The page lists a settings change made a moment ago; a search for a word that is nowhere says *No event matches*; a Viewer gets 403 from `GET /api/v1/admin/audit`.
- **M2-02, Drop-downs look like drop-downs.** Every select in a field (`.rk-field`), the revision picker on the Images tab among them, shows a chevron. | The Images tab's *Revision* control has a chevron.
- **M2-03, Redeploy.** The Tools menu's *Upgrade…* is *Redeploy…*, and its dialog *Redeploy from the source*; the History tab's link and the Values tab's note say Redeploy too. The action, its test ids and the Watson line are unchanged. | The menu says Redeploy…; the dialog's title says Redeploy from the source.
- **M2-04, Sherlock recalls earlier questions.** In Sherlock's question box, ↑ on the first line brings back the question before the one shown, ↓ on the last line the one after, and past the newest the draft being typed; elsewhere in a multi-line draft the keys move the caret. The browser keeps the last fifty questions asked from it. | Unit tests of the recall and the history; a question asked, then ↑, shows it again.
- **M2-05, Diff of any two.** In the diff dialog either side is a revision, the source at its deployed branch or tag, or the source at any branch or tag of the repository (read in the dialog when the panel has not): two revisions, a revision against a ref, or two refs compare. `GET …/diff` takes `from=source&fromRef=&fromRefKind=` beside `to=source&ref=&refKind=`. | The From and To selects both list the revisions and the branches and tags; a branch against a tag renders a diff.
- **M2-06, Backup and restore.** (Since 0.12.0, asked November 4, 2026: the file is gzipped, `221b-backup/2`; each secret's place holds the placeholder `<secret: not in the backup>`; a restore takes gzipped or plain JSON of either format, brings missing connections back with their credentials' placeholders, failed until the credential is pasted again, and never puts a placeholder over a secret the console has; a secret in the file where a placeholder belongs is refused.) Administration gains **Backup** (SuperAdmin only): *Download backup* saves one JSON file (`221b-backup/1`) with the settings (refresh, time format, fleets, acknowledged alerts, Sherlock's and Watson's settings, grace, banners), the local users made in the console (no password hash), the cluster connections without their credentials, the Helm applications (source, values and parameters as typed, commands, what was deployed from where; no repository token), the Toolbox's session records, Sherlock's spend ledger and the audit log, with a `leftOut` list naming what is never in it: no credential of any kind, no sign-in session, not Watson's cards or Sherlock's conversations (in memory), not the Toolbox's recordings (in the transcript store). *Restore from a backup* reads a file, shows what it holds with a checkbox per part, asks to confirm, applies the parts (settings replace; users are added without a password or updated; applications are added or replaced; session records and audit events are merged by id; the spend replaces) and reports what was done and what is left to do by hand: connections to add again with their credentials, tokens to paste, passwords to set. Both are recorded in the audit log. `GET /api/v1/admin/backup`, `POST /api/v1/admin/restore`. | Go test: the file holds no token, hash or password, and a restore into a second console brings the settings, the application and the records back, twice without doubling; e2e: a download, a restore of the settings, a file that is not a backup refused.
- **M2-08, Setup.** The release panel's first tab and its card are *Setup*, not *Source*: they hold the repository and ref, but also the token, the second values file, the chart's defaults, the fleet, the commit deployed and the drift check. *Deploy to the fleet* is on a row of its own under a rule, always shown (R2-11 amended). | The tab says Setup; the fleet row is there with no fleet labelled, disabled, with the note.
- **M2-07, Commands of an application.** A release with a source gets *New command…* in its Tools menu: a dialog takes a name (unique within the application, case-insensitively), a note (48 characters at most, so it fits the menu) and parameters (one `key=value` per line, as `helm --set`; at least one; no quotes, `$` or backquotes). Saved, the command is an item of the Tools menu with its note; picked, a dialog shows its parameters with **Run** (a Watson card: `helm upgrade … --app --set 'key=value'…` over the saved values and parameters, as the person, after their approval), **Edit** (rename, note, parameters) and **Delete…** (two presses). Commands are kept in the application's record (`commands`, at most twenty) and so in the backup; saving and deleting are audited (`app.command`), a run is the card's record. `PUT`/`DELETE /api/v1/apps/{ns}/{name}/commands/{command}`, `POST …/commands/{command}/run[?install=1]`. | Go test: save, refuse bad parameters and a duplicate name, rename, run (the line carries the --set flags), delete, a Viewer refused; e2e: the menu, the dialog, the run, the rename and the delete.

## Phase 11: SSH to nodes

Asked for on October 30, 2026: a way onto a node that does not go through the kubelet, so it works when the node is NotReady, which is when a node shell (TB-31) cannot start. Scope now: the cluster's own nodes (hosts outside the cluster later, if at all); the login user must exist on the node already. The approach is ADR-72: one SSH certificate authority in each console, pairing a node adds the authority and the console's principal to shared files through a node command, and every session runs with a short-lived certificate the console signs.

| ID | Requirement | Done when |
| --- | --- | --- |
| SSH-01 | **The authority.** The console holds one ed25519 SSH certificate authority, made on first use and kept in the Secret `<release>-ssh-ca` beside the connections, written only if no replica got there first (memory on a laptop, with a warning). Its fingerprint shows in the pairing dialog and is recorded with every pairing; a node paired with another authority is *stale* until paired again. | Go test: the key is made once and kept, and two replicas end up with one; a certificate it signs passes a checker that trusts it, with the console's principal, and fails for another principal or after it expires. |
| SSH-02 | **Pairing** is *Pair for SSH…* in a node's Tools menu (SuperAdmin only; `toolbox.ssh.enabled` and `toolbox.nodeShell.enabled`): the dialog takes the login user (default `toolbox.ssh.loginUser`, `root`) and the port (default `toolbox.ssh.port`, 22), says what it writes, and carries the root callout. It runs as a node command does (a short-lived pod on the node, the node's root at `/host`, TB-31): refuses when the login user does not exist (exit 3), when the node has no sshd or host keys, or when sshd already trusts another authority (exit 5), changing nothing; takes the node's lock, since consoles share these files; replaces this console's lines and leaves the others': its key in `/etc/ssh/221b/ca.pub`, one line per console tagged `221b-console=<name>` (`<name>` is `<namespace>.<release>`), and its principal `221b-superadmins-<name>` in `/etc/ssh/221b/principals-<user>`, taken out of every other user's file, and a file left empty is deleted; writes the drop-in `/etc/ssh/sshd_config.d/221b.conf` with `TrustedUserCAKeys` and `AuthorizedPrincipalsFile`, adding an `Include` at the top of `sshd_config` when it exists and has none; runs `sshd -t` and checks with `sshd -T` that sshd uses the drop-in and that port (exit 6); any failure puts the files back as they were; reloads sshd through systemd (`sshd` or `ssh`) or a HUP to its pid, and with none running (socket activation) reloads nothing; prints the node's host key and `221B-PAIRED <user>`. The console records the pairing from that output: user, port, host key, who, when, the authority's fingerprint. The session shows the command's output; it is audited and in Sessions like any node command; the pod is deleted after. The pairing dialog is the approval (TB-37); on production a reason is required and the two-person rule applies; `nodeShell.approval: always` applies. | Go test: the script carries the key, the principal and the quoted user, and its output parses to the host key and the user; the script run under `sh`: two consoles pair one node with different users, each keeps working and neither's certificates admit the other's user; a failed pairing leaves the earlier ones whole; a Viewer and an Admin get 403. |
| SSH-03 | **Unpairing** is *Unpair from SSH…* in the menu of a paired node: a node command that removes this console's key line and principal lines, and the drop-in and `/etc/ssh/221b` only when no console's key is left; it reloads sshd either way and prints `221B-UNPAIRED`; the console then forgets the pairing. | Go test: one of two consoles unpairs and the other keeps working; the last removes both; after it the node is not listed. |
| SSH-04 | **A session** is *SSH…* in the menu of a paired node (hint: *as ops*): a terminal over SSH to the node's InternalIP (else its external address) on the pairing's port, as the pairing's login user, with a certificate the console signs for this session alone (key ID `<person> via 221B session <id>`, the principal the pairing recorded, valid five minutes, used once to connect) and the node's host key checked against the pinned one. The pane's first line names the node, the address, the user and the certificate; host keys and their fingerprints stay on the server, in its log (asked October 30, 2026). It is a Toolbox session: idle and hard limits, recording where transcripts are configured, Administration › Sessions, the Dock. | Go test with an sshd stand-in: an unpaired node is not dialled; a paired one runs a shell whose input and output pass; a node showing another host key is refused before any login. |
| SSH-05 | **A changed host key** (the node reinstalled, or something between) refuses the session and says to pair again once it is known which. A node whose pairing names another authority, or a record with no host keys or no principal, is refused with *pair it again*. | Go test for the host key and such a record; the dialog's hint for a stale pairing. |
| SSH-06 | **Who.** SSH, pairing and unpairing are SuperAdmin-only, like a node shell (TB-32), and a read-only SuperAdmin definition turns them off with it, since Kubernetes never sees an SSH session; the certificate's principal is one name for all of a console's SuperAdmins, so taking a person off the console takes their access away without touching a node. Nothing personal and nothing long-lived goes on a node. | A non-SuperAdmin sees no SSH items; `POST /api/v1/watson/sessions` refuses the tools with 403. |
| SSH-07 | **Chart values** `toolbox.ssh.enabled` (false: SSH is opt-in), `toolbox.ssh.port` (22), `toolbox.ssh.loginUser` (`root`), rendered into the toolbox config; `GET /api/v1/toolbox` says `ssh: {enabled, port, loginUser, principal, caFingerprint, pairings}`, `principal` being what this console's new pairings admit, with each paired node's user, port, principal, when, by whom and whether it is stale. The pairings live under `ssh-pairings.json` in the settings ConfigMap, so every replica sees them, and in a backup. | `hack/check-chart.sh` renders the toolbox config with `ssh` and with it off. |
| SSH-09 | **Network.** The chart's NetworkPolicy allows egress to `toolbox.ssh.port` while SSH is on, to any address or to `networkPolicy.nodeCIDRs` when listed (the first session on 221b timed out without it). | `hack/check-chart.sh`: the rule is there by default, gone with SSH off, narrowed with CIDRs. |
| SSH-10 | **Picked nodes.** The Nodes page has the Fleets tab's picking: a box beside each node's name (`fleet-pick-node`, a 24px target) for a SuperAdmin, the one person with something to do with them; the picks are one list per cluster, shared by both pages and kept across a tab switch. With any picked, the same bar (`fleet-nodebar`) says how many and which, and offers *New fleet…*, *Add to a fleet…* and *Remove from a fleet…* (the Fleets tab's dialog: a list changes in the console, a fleet of labels and taints through Watson) and *Cancel*. The Nodes page adds a box in the Node column's heading that picks every node in view or, when all are, unpicks them (half ticked when some are; it replaced *pick all in view* in 0.12.0), *Collect facts* (the nodes paired for SSH and in a fleet; a toast counts those that answered and those skipped), and *Pair for SSH…* with Watson on: one dialog, one login user and port, one Watson card naming every node (one approval), then one pairing session per node, the first shown (`Request.nodes`; the plan dry-runs the first and lists the rest). On the Nodes page the bar sits above the table, in the flow (it floated briefly; reversed the same day). *Node shell* reads *K8S node shell* beside *SSH*. A fleet's drawer has the wrench too, with *Pair for SSH…* and *Collect facts* for the fleet's saved nodes. | e2e: picking two nodes, the picks kept across a tab switch and on the Fleets tab, the fleet dialog naming both, the pairing dialog naming both, Cancel; the heading's box, Collect facts, the fleet drawer's Tools. |
| SSH-08 | **Out of scope now:** per-role login users, an SSH column on the Nodes table, host certificates. Hosts outside the cluster, with pairing through a password or key and sudo and creating the login user, came in Phase 12 ([automation.md](automation.md#hosts-outside-kubernetes-phase-12)). | — |


## Phase 11, part 2: Automation

Asked for on October 31, 2026: run AI-written, human-approved scripts on fleets of nodes over the SSH of part 1, approving code once rather than every run. [automation.md](automation.md) has the approach, the script header, the mockups and the open questions; ADR-73 has the decisions. This first step is the Auto-1 core; the design doc "221B Automation Design" phases the rest (facts, Sherlock, Kubernetes targets, node hooks, schedules).

| ID | Requirement |
| --- | --- |
| AU-01 | An **Automation** tab after Fleets, with Scripts and Executions, for SuperAdmins when `automation.enabled` and `toolbox.ssh.enabled` are on. |
| AU-02 | Scripts with names, groups and versions; a changed save is a new draft; 32 KiB at most. |
| AU-03 | The `# 221b:` header: describe, param, privilege, dry-run, facts. |
| AU-04 | Static checks at every save; a version with errors cannot be submitted. |
| AU-05 | Versions are Draft, In review, Approved or Superseded; a two-person cluster runs only versions with two approvals, one not the author's. |
| AU-06 | Fleet and node parameters, resolved per node: run, node, fleet. |
| AU-23 | A node's own values and reboot time are the node's, kept once and shown under All nodes (0.13.0). A node's own value of a name that is sensitive on its cluster is sensitive too, and kept in the Secret (DB-71). |
| AU-07 | The Run dialog: fleet, nodes and their pairing, parameters and their source, batches, failure limit, timeout, dry run. |
| AU-08 | A node's part: through its pairing, the script on standard input, `runuser -u nobody` for privilege none and dry runs, exit code, result line, output. |
| AU-09 | Reboot and wait when a result asks for it. |
| AU-10 | Batches, the failure limit and cancel. |
| AU-11 | Executions: runs newest first, a drawer with each node's state and output. |
| AU-12 | A ConfigMap of its own: scripts, parameters and the newest 200 runs; output in memory or beside the transcripts; interrupted runs marked. |
| AU-13 | SuperAdmin-only, on the leader, audited as `automation.script`, `automation.approve`, `automation.params` and `automation.run`. |
| AU-14 | Chart: `automation.enabled`, the ConfigMap and its rule, `AUTOMATION_CONFIGMAP`. |

## Phase 12: AI agents

Asked for on November 5, 2026: instead of one Sherlock on one provider, a catalog of agents, each with one purpose, its own model from any of several providers, only its purpose's tools and its own budget; Sherlock at the front desk hands parts of a question to the others. Their instructions come in layers, and an edit goes from a draft through tests and a pilot to live with a second SuperAdmin. ADR-74 to ADR-79 have the decisions; the mockups are Main, Agent, Providers and Chat in the Phase 12 design.

| ID | Requirement |
| --- | --- |
| AI-01 | **Providers**, any number: an id, a name, a kind (Anthropic, OpenAI-compatible, Amazon Bedrock, Google Vertex AI), an endpoint, the credential's header and prefix, extra headers and query, region and project, a private CA, a default model and prices per million tokens. Each credential is in a Secret of its own (`<release>-ai-<id>`; the default provider's is `<release>-sherlock`, as before), never sent to a browser, an export, a backup or a log. A provider the chart fixes (`sherlock.lockProvider`) shows as locked. Each has a Test that sends it one short request. |
| AI-02 | **Agents**, a catalog: Sherlock (front desk, routes, the only one that changes the view), Troubleshooter, Change planner (its answer ends in a Watson card's commands), Auditor, Host doctor, Automation author (its drafts always go to the Script reviewer), Script reviewer, Mycroft (with `ask_knowledge`, into its wiki, KN-11; every agent may ask it) and Summarizer (off by default). Each has on or off, a provider, a model (empty is the provider's default), a daily and a monthly cap, the clusters it works on, and its tools, a subset of its purpose's, never more. |
| AI-03 | The Script reviewer is refused on the Automation author's provider unless a SuperAdmin confirms the console has one provider only. |
| AI-04 | **One ledger.** Every call is charged to the agent that made it; an agent's spend counts against its own caps and, with every other agent's, against the overall daily and monthly limits, Sherlock's caps of before. |
| AI-05 | **Hand-over.** Every agent's prompt lists the agents that are on and work on the cluster, one line each. `hand_over(agent, task, context)` asks one and returns its answer to the caller. The receiver works with its own instructions and tools, as the person asking. A chain is at most 3 deep, never asks one agent twice, charges each call to the receiver's budget, and stops when any budget in it is spent. |
| AI-06 | The answer shows each hand-over as a block, "Sherlock asked the Troubleshooter: …", that opens to its steps, what came back, its model and, for an administrator, its cost; hand-overs inside hand-overs nest. |
| AI-07 | The audit log records each agent call (`ai.agent`): the agent, its model, who started it and who asked, the hand-overs it made, the tokens and the cost; a question's record names its hand-overs. |
| AI-08 | **Prompt layers**, in this order: the fixed core of each agent (in code, one file per agent: text from the cluster is data, no secret leaves the console, changes go only through Watson cards, the hand-over rules), the house instructions (a SuperAdmin's, per agent), then, in the cluster's block, the notes for every cluster and for that cluster. Each editable text is at most 2,000 words; a line that looks like a fact about the infrastructure warns and is kept. |
| AI-09 | Sherlock's instructions and notes from before Phase 12 become its house instructions and the notes for every cluster, once, at the first start. |
| AI-10 | **Baseline and overrides.** `agents.<agent>.instructions` in the chart's values is the baseline of the house instructions. An edit in the console overrides it. The states: In sync, Edited in app, Baseline changed (an upgrade brought a new baseline while an edit is in force: shown side by side, kept, taken or merged by editing, never replaced silently). An edit equal to the deployed baseline is dropped. The drawer shows the values key and copies the text as values. |
| AI-11 | **Versions.** Every version that went live is kept (the latest fifty since AI-32, the latest ten before), and one restores in one click as a draft that may go live again without a new test. |
| AI-12 | **Draft → Test → Pilot → Live.** A test runs the recorded test cases (a question with recorded tool results; checks: the tools called, the agents asked, a refusal, no credential in the answer, points graded by a second model call) and replays the latest real answers with their tool results, side by side, proposing nothing; its cost is estimated first. A pilot serves the draft to chosen people or clusters for a set time and logs both versions' answers, to compare (no longer rated since AI-29). Live needs a second SuperAdmin's approval (Approvals, in Cases › Inbox since CS-06) where there is one. |
| AI-13 | Base test cases ship with each agent; any answer of one's own conversation can be saved as a test case, its recorded results scrubbed again. Tests run against the fake Claude API in CI, never a real model. |
| AI-14 | At a start with a changed baseline, the leader tests it once in the background and flags failures on the agent; nothing waits for it. |
| AI-15 | **Storage**: a ConfigMap of their own, `<release>-ai` (providers, agents, instructions and versions, test cases, the pilots' log), within the console's limits; `ai.BackupKeys` lists its keys for a backup, with a placeholder where a provider has a credential. |
| AI-16 | **Administration › AI**: Providers (each with its credential, Test, and a drawer to add or change one), Agents (a table: on or off, provider and model, cap, clusters, where its instructions stand; a drawer: model, instructions with their state, values key, Copy, Compare with baseline and the Draft, Test, Pilot, Live strip; tools and hand-overs; On its own, which leads to Cases › Jobs; budget and clusters) and Knowledge (Mycroft's sources, a later step). They replace the Sherlock section; its address leads to Agents. |
| AI-17 | An upgrade from 0.11 needs nothing: Sherlock's provider is the default provider with its key where it was, its model the default model, every agent starts on it, and its caps are the overall limits. Sherlock's settings endpoints keep working over the same data. |
| AI-18 | Automation's model calls are its agents': drafting is the Automation author's, a review the Script reviewer's, weighing a variable change the Change planner's. |
| AI-19 | **What Watson runs, from Watson's list** (review S-1, P-1). The sentence that tells an agent which kubectl and helm commands Watson runs is built from Watson's own list of commands (`watson.Commands`) when the prompt is assembled: in Sherlock's built-in instructions, its compact form, and after the rules every agent keeps for every other agent. Since PM-D7 the sentence goes only to Sherlock when the commands are its own work (no Change planner in its list, or no agents) and to its compact form; the Troubleshooter and the Change planner check each command with `check_command`. The Troubleshooter has `check_command`, as the Change planner has, and is told to check each command before giving it. Go `TestPromptsNameEveryCommandWatsonRuns`, `TestCommandsNamesEveryCommand`. |
| AI-20 | **Sherlock can open the Policies section it read** (review S-5). The Policies tab's path takes the section ids `list_policies` takes (`netpol`, `pdb`, `drain`, `quota`, `limits`, `psa`, `webhooks`, `priority`), and `prio` as before: `drain` opens Disruption budgets, `limits` Quotas and limits, `priority` Priority classes. vitest `the Policies tab’s sections (S-5)`. |
| AI-21 | **The steps under an answer read as words** (review S-6, S-7): a hand-over is "Asked the Change planner" (or "Asked Mycroft"), by the agent's name, and a tool dialog is "Opened a tool dialog". The number of view changes the prompt asks Sherlock to keep to for one question (4) comes from a constant below the hard cap of ten view actions an answer may make. Go `TestStepsNameToolsAndAgentsInWords`, `TestThePromptsViewChangesComeFromTheConstant`. |
| AI-22 | **Any provider can be removed, and any made the default** (known issue KI-2, November 10, 2026; amends AI-01, AI-17). In Administration › AI › Providers each provider's dialog has *Remove*, always shown and enabled, the default's too, and every provider but the default has *Make default*. Remove takes a provider away with its credential only when no agent uses it, on or off: otherwise nothing is removed and the dialog says why, naming the agents ("Anthropic cannot be removed: Sherlock, Troubleshooter and Summarizer (off) use it. Move them to another provider first."); the default is removed only once another is the default; a provider the chart fixes (`sherlock.lockProvider`) or whose key the chart sets stays, and says so. The server refuses the same. *Make default* makes it the provider of every agent that names none (they move to it with their models and caps; an agent put on another provider by name stays there; choosing the default in an agent's drawer follows the default); it is refused while the chart locks the default provider, and when it would put the Automation author and the Script reviewer on one provider without the single-provider confirmation. Removing the provider Sherlock had (`default`) deletes its key in `<release>-sherlock`; its settings stay in the settings ConfigMap. Audited as `ai.settings`. | Go `TestMakeDefaultAndRemove` (ai), `TestProvidersAndAgents`; vitest `ProvidersSection.test.tsx` *lets Remove be pressed on a provider in use…*, *makes another provider the default*. |
| AI-23 | **Make live untested** (Phase 12.1, asked November 17, 2026). While the draft is not tested at its revision (and restores no version and is not in pilot), the Live step offers *Make live untested* beside *Make live*, a SuperAdmin's as Make live is. Only a test run that ran every case it planned to the end tests a draft (AI-25): a run stopped, or failed at a cap or a case that could not be set up, is kept and shown, but the draft stays untested, for its pilot as for Make live ("test the draft first: its last test did not run every case to the end"), and the drawer says so beside the last test. It asks for a reason, required, at most 300 characters, then takes the same way to live: at once where no other SuperAdmin could approve it, else as a request on Approvals that names it "go live untested (reason: …)", approved as any other. The version it makes says "made live untested: <reason>" in the Versions list, and the audit records (`ai.prompt`, `twoperson`) say *untested* with the reason. Without the flag an untested draft is still refused ("test the draft first"); a flag without a reason is refused. Go `TestADraftGoesLiveUntestedWithAReason`, `TestAIMakeLiveUntestedAPI`; vitest *makes an untested draft live only with a reason…*. |
| AI-24 | **The test dialog** (Phase 12.1, asked November 17, 2026). *Run the test* starts the run and opens a dialog at once; it runs on the leader, so its progress (`GET /api/v1/ai/agents/{id}/tests/run`, a SuperAdmin's, forwarded to the leader from any replica) reads the same from every replica: the run's state (running, done, stopped, failed), each case's state (waiting, running, passed, failed) after each case and replay, how many of how many, the cost so far against the estimate (when costs show). *Stop* (`POST …/tests/stop`, audited `ai.test`) ends the run as stopped at the case it was on, kept with the draft so. Each result keeps the case as it ran: its question (clipped), what it expected with the planted secrets only counted, and a check per expected line, met or not (finishes, calls each tool, hands over to each agent, refuses, makes each point, does not reveal the planted secrets, no credential-looking text); a planted secret is never copied into a result, even from an answer that carried it. The dialog lists the cases, the failed first and open, each with its question, its expected lines ✓ or ✗, the answer (said when shortened), the tools called, the hand-overs and why it failed; a second tab shows each real question replayed, the earlier answer beside the draft's, the same or different. States read in words, not by colour alone; the progress is announced politely; Escape closes it without stopping the run. While the run goes on the drawer says "Tests running: n of M cases…" with *Show tests*; after it, the last test's line has the failures counted and *Show results*, which opens the same dialog read-only, and says when the run tested an earlier draft. With no draft, Draft, Test and Pilot read as not started, not done. Go `TestATestRunShowsItsProgressAndStops`, `TestEveryExpectationIsACheck`, `TestAITestRunProgressAndStopAPI`; vitest `TestDialog.test.tsx`, *shows the steps before live as not started…*; e2e `sherlock.spec.ts` (R2-05). |
| AI-25 | **Test cases run in parallel** (Phase 12.1, asked November 17, 2026; amends AI-24). A test run runs its cases four at once (`TestParallel`), each case's state shown as it changes, so several may read *running* together, and the dialog's progress says how many ("3 of 12 cases · 4 running"); the results are kept in the order of the cases, those finished so far, each matched to its case. The spending caps are checked before each case starts: a case stopped at a cap fails with the cap's words, no case starts after it, and the run ends failed at that case with the cap's words, the cases after it still waiting. *Stop* cancels every case in flight; the run ends stopped at the first case cut short ("the cases running then were cut short, and those still waiting did not run"). The replays still run after the cases, one at a time. Each case and replay reads the caps in force when it starts, and grading a case's points checks them first, so a cap lowered during a run holds at once. A run that stopped or failed does not test the draft (AI-23). A run is the leader's: a replica that loses the lead stops its runs, and a run ends with the console's process, whatever request started it. Go `TestACapLoweredDuringARunHolds`, `TestLosingTheLeadStopsTestRuns`, `TestATestRunEndsWithTheProcess`, `TestAStoppedRunDoesNotTestTheDraft`, `TestTestCasesRunInParallel` (a fake API that holds each request until four are in flight), `TestACapReachedStopsNewCases`, `TestATestRunShowsItsProgressAndStops`, run with `-race`; vitest *match each finished case to its result while several run at once*. |
| AI-26 | **A new draft discards the earlier ones** (Phase 12.1, asked November 17, 2026). An agent has one draft: saving a new one discards the one before, with its test, which no longer counts for anything, and its pilot, which ends. Every go-live request on Approvals still waiting for an earlier draft of that agent is withdrawn (kept, rejected and marked `withdrawn`, by the person who saved, with the reason "Withdrawn: draft N was saved, which discards the drafts before it."), so the new draft can be sent for go-live without "a request for it already waits"; a withdrawn request can no longer be approved. Discarding the draft, *Back to draft* and restoring a version withdraw the requests of the drafts they replace the same way. The live version and the versions once live are untouched. The save answers with what it discarded, and the drawer and a toast say it: "Earlier draft 3 was discarded; the pilot of draft 3 ended; request 1 was withdrawn." Audited: `ai.prompt` ("draft 4 saved …; earlier drafts discarded [3], the pilot of draft 3 ended, requests withdrawn [1]") and `twoperson` for each request withdrawn. Go `TestANewDraftDiscardsTheEarlier`, `TestAStaleRequestIsWithdrawn` (twoperson), `TestAINewDraftDiscardsTheEarlierAPI`; vitest *says which earlier drafts a new draft discarded…*. |

A hand-over, a test case and a job's run mark their newest message beside the tools' and the system blocks' breakpoints. No breakpoint is put on an effort message or a thinking block. The fake API refuses a fifth breakpoint and one on a thinking block or an empty text block, as the API does. | Go `TestTheHistoryIsCachedAI50`, `TestTheFakeHoldsTheCacheRulesAI50`, `TestWithHistoryCacheKeepsToFour` (sherlock). |
| AI-51 | **The answer written before a view call stays the answer** (the measurement of November 23, 2026, F6: a case's answer read only "The view couldn't be opened because no console window is available…", and Sherlock's diagnosis, written before its `view_open` call, was dropped as a note). Text a round writes before calls that read or hand over is the model thinking aloud and stays a note, as before; text a round writes before view calls alone (`view_open`, `view_filter` and the other view tools) is kept as the answer's lead. When the answer ends, a last round's text shorter than the lead follows it (a line about the view after the answer); one as long or longer stands alone (the answer after a line such as "Opening the nodes."). A later round that reads the cluster drops the lead. | Go `TestTheAnswerBeforeAViewCallIsKeptAI51` (sherlock; the fake API's `[[say {"text":…}]]` writes a round's text before its call). |
| AI-52 | **A test case's tool may be called by the agent asked** (the measurement of November 23, 2026, F2: `base-sherlock-1` expects `get_pod`, and Sherlock handed the crash loop to the Troubleshooter, which called it, in 5 of 6 runs: the case failed at every effort). A test run's tools (`RunResult.Tools`, a result's *Tools*) are the agent's own calls, then those of the agents it handed work to, in the order of the hand-overs, each one's before those of the agents it asked; a check *calls get_pod* holds when any of them called it. The base cases keep their expectations: a case that wants the hand-over itself says so with `handOvers`. | Go `TestAToolCalledByTheAgentAskedCountsAI52` (ai; the fake API's `[[chain troubleshooter]] [[get_pod {…}]]` has the last agent of the chain call the tools the question scripts). |
| AI-53 | **A test run's cost counts its hand-overs** (the measurement of November 23, 2026, F3: Sherlock's run said $0.356 where the console spent $0.471, the Troubleshooter's $0.134 against $0.249). A case's and a replay's cost (`costUSD`), and so the run's, are the agent's own calls, its grading and those of every agent it handed work to, down the chain (`RunResult.TotalCostUSD`); each agent's spend is still charged to its own budget, and each agent call's audit record says its own cost. The estimate counts, for an agent that may hand work over, one hand-over a case and a replay to the dearest agent on its roster, reading the same recorded results. | Go `TestATestRunCountsItsHandOversAI53` (ai: the run's cost is what the ledger counted; the estimate with agents to hand work to is at least half again the one without). |
| AI-54 | **Testing an agent that is off is a conflict; a live test says the instructions in force** (the measurement of November 23, 2026, F4 and F7). `POST /api/v1/ai/agents/{id}/test` (and its estimate) for an agent that is off answers 409 with the sentence ("the Summarizer is off"), not 500, and logs no warning; so does any AI request for an agent off or on a cluster it does not work on (`ai.ErrUnavailable`), and one for an agent whose provider cannot answer, gone or without its credential (`ai.ErrUnusable`, with the sentence: *The provider GW has no credential…*; the review of November 24, 2026, finding 8: the test panel answered 500 and logged a warning on every open). A job's run still tells the two apart: only an agent turned off is *off* (JB-25). An agent id that is not one answers 404. Automation's *Draft with Sherlock* and *Review* (`POST /api/v1/automation/sherlock/draft`, `…/versions/{n}/review`) answer 409 too when the Automation author or the Script reviewer is off or its provider cannot answer (finding 7: 502 and a warning), and 502 only when the provider fails. Each agent call of a test run of the instructions in force (`live`) says *instructions in force* in its `ai.agent` audit record; a test of the draft says *instructions draft*, as before. | Go `TestTestingAnAgentThatIsOffIsAConflictAI54`, `TestATestOfAnAgentWithAnUnusableProviderReview8`, `TestAScriptWithTheAuthorOffIsAConflictReview7` (api), `TestALiveTestSaysInstructionsInForceAI54` (ai). |
| AI-55 | **Each request to a model is logged with its effort and betas** (the measurement of November 23, 2026, F8: nothing showed what a request was sent with). The console logs each request to a provider once, at Debug (`logLevel: debug`), as *a request to the model's API* with the agent, the provider and its kind, the model, the effort as sent (`low (top level)`, `medium (message)` for Auto's change mid-conversation, `enable_thinking true` to an OpenAI-compatible server, or `none`) and the `anthropic-beta` header; never the credential, the prompt or the answer. | Go `TestEachRequestLogsItsEffortAndBetasAI55` (sherlock). |
| AI-56 | **A test's estimate follows the effort** (the measurement of November 23, 2026, F9: the estimate was the same at every effort, and the Automation author's run cost 1.3 to 1.7 times it at `low`, 2.8 times at `medium` and 3.4 to 4.1 times at `high`). The estimate of a test (`POST /api/v1/ai/agents/{id}/test` with `estimate`, the test dialog) counts what the model writes in each round by the effort the test runs at: the effort asked, else the agent's own fixed effort; the model's default and Auto (whose default is `medium`) count as `medium`. About 1,200 tokens a round at `low`, 2,400 at `medium`, 3,600 at `high`, 5,000 at `xhigh` and 7,000 at `max` (`ai.OutputPerRound`), in place of 600 at every effort; the agents it may hand work to are counted at their own effort. A case's grading is priced at the agent's own model, which grades it, also for a run on another model (the review of November 24, 2026, finding 13: it was priced at the model tested on). | Go `TestTheEstimateFollowsTheEffortF9`, `TestTheEstimatePricesGradingOnTheAgentsModelReview13` (ai). |
| AI-57 | **The models a provider offers its agents, their tiers for Auto, and one Model dropdown** (phase-13.1-ai, A9; the owner, November 23, 2026: "a new model is used once it is offered and tiered, with no release"). A provider's details (Administration › AI › Providers › *Edit…*) have *Models offered to the agents*: the models its endpoint lists (`GET /api/v1/ai/providers/{id}/models`, as the default model's field reads them), each with a checkbox, a model typed by hand added for a server that lists none, and its default model, always offered. Each offered model has a tier, *Light*, *Standard*, *Strong* or *No tier* (Auto does not pick it), and a place: the first of a tier is the one Auto prefers (↑ moves a model up). Saved with the provider (`offered`, `offeredChosen`; `ai_provider_models` and `ai_providers.is_models_chosen`, step `0054`, DB-99) and audited (*offers gpt-5 (standard), …*); a tier other than the three, a model twice or a name that is not one is refused; at most 50. Its card says what it offers. A provider whose offered models no SuperAdmin chose (every provider after the upgrade) offers every model it lists, as before, and Auto picks on a provider of kind Anthropic among Claude Haiku 5.5 (light), Sonnet 5.5 (standard) and Opus 5.5 (strong), on any other among none; its drawer starts from those three, the ones its endpoint lists, and any change there chooses. **Auto picks by tier** (AI-44, AI-45) among the agent's provider's offered models with a tier, on any kind of provider: low is light, medium standard, high and above strong; a tier with no model takes the next tier up that has one, else the next down; the defaults (no classifier) are medium on the standard model. Auto for the model is offered, and accepted, only on a provider one of whose offered models has a tier; an agent whose provider's models lost their tiers answers on the provider's default model, its drawer says so and the agents' table says *no tier: on the default model* under its model (`autoNoTier`); its model stays Auto and its other settings may still be saved, its On/Off switch too (the review of November 24, 2026, finding 2: such a save answered 400), while Auto newly set there, or on another provider, is still refused. The provider's save that takes the last tier is not refused. **An agent's Model is one dropdown**, in place of the checkboxes *Use the default model instead* and *Auto: let the console pick the model among …*: *Default (<model> on <provider>)*, *Auto* (when the provider has a tiered model), then the models the provider offers with their tiers (while none are chosen, those its endpoint lists, fetched as the drawer opens, and typed only when they cannot be listed, FU-01); a fixed model has its context window field as before. Saving an agent with a model its provider does not offer is refused (*GW does not offer qwen3.8-235b to the agents*), as is a floor that is not one of its tiered models. **A model taken off the list** stays set: the agent keeps answering on it until a SuperAdmin chooses another (its other settings may still be saved), and its drawer says *GW no longer offers qwen-old: the Troubleshooter answers on it until you choose another model* (`notOffered` in the agents' view). Auto across an agent's several providers is not decided (the owner). | Go `TestAnUpgradeOffersWhatItDidA9`, `TestAutoOnAnOpenAICompatibleProviderWithTiersA9`, `TestANewModelIsUsedOnceTieredA9`, `TestAModelRemovedFromTheOfferedListA9` (ai, both stores), `TestAutoWithNoTierLeftCanBeSavedReview2` (ai), `TestAutoPicksByTierAmongTheOfferedModelsA9` (auto), `TestProvidersInTheDatabase` (ai), `TestAProvidersOfferedModelsAPIA9` (api); vitest `AgentsSection.test.tsx` *offers Default, Auto and the provider’s offered models, says a model is no longer offered…*, *says Auto cannot pick the model…*, *says in the table when Auto has no tiered model to pick…*, `ProvidersSection.test.tsx` *starts an upgraded Anthropic provider from Claude’s three…*, *starts from what the server says…*. |

## Phase 12: Watson cards

Asked for on November 5, 2026: every change an agent wants, asked in a chat or on its own at night, becomes a durable Watson card that waits for people, explains itself, is checked again when approved, and outlives a restart; Watson's console actions; standing approvals for narrow classes outside production; and notifications. ADR-99 to ADR-102 have the decisions; the mockups are Approvals and Cases (the agent's proposal in the Inbox) in the Phase 12 design.

| ID | Requirement | Done when |
| --- | --- | --- |
| WC-01 | **Durable proposals.** A proposal (agent, what started it with who set it, cluster, title, reasoning, evidence with dates, actions with impact and risk, condition, dedup key, expiry) is stored as a card, in the database since 0.13 (`changes`, DB-45, DB-79; until then the ConfigMap `<release>-cases`, `cards.json`, 640 KiB), with Watson's reading of each action when it was proposed: a summary, the targets, the impact and a fingerprint. It survives a restart. `cards.Store.Propose` is what the jobs and Mycroft call. | Go test: a card stored, read back by a new store on the same records. |
| WC-02 | **States.** queued → approved, rejected, expired or replaced; approved → applied or failed. A card keeps its approvals and rejection (who, when, comment), its re-check, the result of each action and a history of what happened, at most 30 lines; decided cards are kept, the newest 500 that fit. | Go tests of each state. |
| WC-03 | **Replaced.** A newer proposal with the same dedup key replaces the older one that waits; the older names the newer, and is audited (`cards.replace`). | Go test. |
| WC-04 | **Expires.** After 24 hours at most (a later expiry is cut to 24 hours), or when the alert in its condition resolves (it recovers or leaves the Alerts tab); checked every 30 seconds on the leader, and audited (`cards.expire`). A card left approved by a console that stopped is failed with the reason. | Go test: 23 hours waits, 24 expires; an alert resolving expires two cards; the audit records. |
| WC-05 | **Never a secret.** A card never carries a credential or a host key: arguments named like one (password, private key, token, host key, fingerprint…) or holding a key or a PEM block are refused, free text is scrubbed (rule X-01), and only the fields each action knows are accepted. | Go test: a password, a host key and a key in a label refused; a token in the reasoning and evidence scrubbed. |
| WC-06 | **Approval permission.** Approving needs the approval permission: a SuperAdmin or a role in `cases.approverRoles` (SuperAdmin and Admin by default), not admin rights; a SuperViewer sees proposals. A card whose actions change the console's own records (hosts, fleets, Automation parameters) needs a SuperAdmin, as those records do today; kubectl and helm run as the approver, so Kubernetes still decides. | API test: a Viewer refused, an Admin approves a kubectl card and not a host card. |
| WC-07 | **An agent is not a person.** Where changes need a second person (Watson's two-person rule, on production), an agent's own card needs two different people; a card a person asked for in a chat counts them as one, and they may not approve it themselves; with sign-in off such a card cannot be approved. | Go and API tests: 1 of 2, the same person refused, the second runs it, as the second. |
| WC-08 | **Re-checked at approval.** Each approval first checks the condition (the alert still fires; the object is at the version read) and that every action's target fingerprint is unchanged; if not, the approval is refused (409) and the card says why, keeping the result. A target Watson could not read at the proposal is never approved. *Check again* runs the same check on request (`cards.recheck`). | Go tests: a changed target, a resolved alert, an unread target; API test of the 409 with the card. |
| WC-09 | **Applied through Watson.** Once approved, the actions run in order, stopping at the first that fails: kubectl and helm through Watson's runner as the last approver, shown in their Watson panel, read-only and locked clusters refusing as before; console actions as below. The card records each result, never the output, and the audit log records the outcome with the agent, its model, what started it and who set that, and every approver (`cards.approve`, `cards.apply`). | Go test of Watson's run as the approver; API test end to end on a simulated cluster. |
| WC-10 | **Shown.** The card lists in Cases › Inbox (`InboxProposals`: kind, what, cluster, status "Waiting · 0 of 2", requested by "Troubleshooter, on an alert", requested on, Review; the decided ones below with who and when), in the cluster's Watson panel, and opens as a drawer: what started it and who set the trigger, the agent and its model, why, the evidence with dates, the exact commands or console changes with the agent's impact and risk and Watson's targets and impact, Production: 2 people, when it expires, what is checked again and its last result, results and history; Approve and Reject… (a reason required; it was Refuse…, toast "Rejected."). A notification's link `#/cases/inbox?proposal=<id>` opens it. *Approve superseded by CF-62: the foot's Run in Watson brings the proposal's card to the drawer's Watson, which asks for the approval.* | vitest. |
| WC-11 | **Chat proposals durable too.** A change Sherlock proposed in a chat and the person sent to Watson is kept as a card as well (source chat, the person as requester), shown once while its Watson card is in the panel: either approval runs it once (the other is withdrawn); its Watson card run, discarded or lapsed applies, withdraws or leaves the card waiting. | API test; Go test of each ending. |
| WC-12 | **Console actions** (Watson runs, ADR-101), each with validation, a description of what it changes now, a fingerprint, apply and audit: `host.add` (pending until a person pairs it in Add host with one credential and one approval of every key), `host.label`, `host.remove` (with the cleanup command in its result), `fleet.create` and `fleet.update` (either kind; a list's nodes added and removed), `automation.params` (values and per-node values only: never the privilege ceiling or what is sensitive), and `record`, the pattern for other console records, each type registered with its own card shape. | Go test of each kind, a stale host label, and a record type. |
| WC-13 | **Standing approvals.** A SuperAdmin sets a narrow class: clusters (never production, refused when set and when a cluster becomes production), an agent or any, the actions (a console action or a kubectl or helm command of at least two words), namespaces, a condition, and at most 1 to 60 an hour. A matching card is approved by it as it arrives, after the same re-check, its changes run as the SuperAdmin who set it; beyond the rate it waits for a person. Set, changed, removed and every use are audited (`cards.standing`, `cards.approve` "by a standing approval"). Administration › Settings › Watson. | Go tests: three an hour, the fourth waits, room again an hour on; production and other commands not covered; API test: a SuperAdmin's, never on production. |
| WC-14 | **Announced.** The gear in the top bar flags proposals that came in for the person to approve since they last looked, and then opens Cases › Inbox. | vitest of the count. |
| WC-15 | **Notification channels.** A generic webhook (JSON with event, text, link and the proposal's summary), Slack (text with a link) and Microsoft Teams (an Adaptive Card with Open in 221B) incoming webhooks, set by a SuperAdmin with the console's address for links, each on or off, with Test. A message carries the title, cluster, agent, what started it, the approvals needed and the expiry, never the reasoning, evidence, commands, output or a secret. A failure shows on the channel and in the audit log (`cards.notify.send`) with the answer's status, never the address. Email is not offered. | Go test: three channels get a message with none of those; a failing test names 403. |
| WC-16 | **Channel addresses are secrets**, kept in the Secret `<release>-cases` (in memory with `connections.store=memory`), written and never read back: the page shows set or not set; the audit log says "address set". With the network policy on, `sherlock.egress` covers Slack and Teams; other webhooks go under `networkPolicy.outboundCIDRs`. | Go and API tests: the address in no answer, view or audit record. |
| WC-17 | **API.** `GET /api/v1/cases/proposals` (`/api/v1/aiops/…` since CF-104) (`state`, `cluster`, `agent`, `mine=1`; with how many wait for the person), `GET …/proposals/{id}`, `POST …/proposals/{id}/approve` (comment), `…/reject` (reason), `…/recheck`; `GET`, `POST /api/v1/cases/standing`, `PUT`, `DELETE …/standing/{id}` (SuperAdmin); `GET`, `PUT /api/v1/cases/notifications`, `POST …/notifications/{id}/test` (SuperAdmin). Writes go to the leader like every data request. | API tests. |
| WC-18 | **Backup.** The part `cases` (order 240): every card with its decisions and history, the standing approvals, and the notification settings with each address as the placeholder; a restore adds the cards missing, adds or replaces standing approvals, replaces the settings keeping this console's addresses, notes each channel that needs its address again, and refuses an address in clear. | Go test of the export and import. |
| WC-19 | **Chart.** The ConfigMap `<release>-cases` (`<release>-proposals` and `PROPOSALS_CONFIGMAP` since CF-104), created empty, in the Role's names, `CASES_CONFIGMAP` and `CASES_APPROVER_ROLES` (`-cases-configmap`, `-cases-approver-roles`), and `cases.approverRoles` checked to name the four roles. | `hack/check-chart.sh`. |

## Phase 12: Backups

Asked for on November 5, 2026: a backup that holds everything the console keeps, whichever package keeps it, and backups that leave on a schedule for S3 without anyone downloading them. ADR-80 to ADR-82 have the decisions.

| ID | Requirement | Done when |
| --- | --- | --- |
| BK-01 | **Parts registered.** A backup is made of parts each package registers with `backup.Register` (name, label, order, export, restore, optional check); the console's own parts (settings, users, connections, applications, Sherlock's key and the SSH authority, sealed since BK-15, session records, Sherlock's spend, hosts, pairings, audit log, Automation) are registered the same way. The format stays `221b-backup/2`; a restore reads `/1` and `/2`, applies the parts picked in order, and names a part it does not know. | Go test: a registered part's export and restore through the registry; the console's existing backup tests unchanged in what they check. |
| BK-02 | **Secrets stay out.** Every part writes the placeholder `<secret: not in the backup>` for a secret (`backup.Secret`), and its restore keeps the console's secret where the file has a placeholder and refuses a secret where a placeholder belongs (`backup.Keep`). (Changed on November 9, 2026, BK-15: with a backup key the secrets are carried sealed instead; the placeholder stays for an install without a key and for older backups, which restore as before.) | Go test: a part's tokens leave as placeholders, a restore keeps a token here, brings a missing record back without one, and refuses one in clear. |
| BK-03 | **Optional parts.** A part may be optional (chat conversations): exported only when asked, `?optional=1` on the download, *Include …* on the page when the server has one, and a setting of the scheduled backups. | Go test; vitest: the box shows with an optional part. |
| BK-04 | **Destination.** Administration › Backup › *Scheduled backups to S3*: bucket, prefix, region, an endpoint for an S3-compatible store, path-style addresses, how the bucket encrypts what it stores (*Encryption in the bucket (server-side)*: the bucket's default, SSE-S3 or SSE-KMS with its KMS key, BK-09; in the Destination row since 0.14.0, where it was in the Encryption row), a private CA. Saved under `backup-s3.json` in the settings ConfigMap (16 KiB at most). | Go test of the checks; vitest of the row (the server-side encryption in it, not under Encryption). |
| BK-05 | **Credentials.** An access key ID and secret kept in the Secret `<release>-backup` (in memory with `connections.store=memory`), never sent back to a page: the card says whether one is saved and offers *Replace…*; or the pod's own AWS identity: keys in its environment, IRSA's web identity (STS AssumeRoleWithWebIdentity) or EKS Pod Identity. | Go tests: the keys are not in the settings, the API's answers or the audit log; the web identity is exchanged once and kept; vitest: keys are sent only after *Replace…*. |
| BK-06 | **Schedule.** Every day at HH:MM, weekdays, weekly on some days, every N hours from a time, or a five-field cron expression, in an IANA time zone, read back in plain words ("Every day at 03:00, Europe/Berlin") with the next three times. Package `schedule`, which the jobs reuse. A time the clocks skip runs when they change; a time they pass twice runs once. | Go tests of every shape, cron's day rule, the clocks changing in Berlin and New York, and the refusals; `POST /api/v1/admin/backup/s3/schedule`. |
| BK-07 | **On the leader, once.** The schedule runs only on the leading replica (ADR-70); each time is recorded as handled before it runs, so a new leader does not run it again. A time missed while the console was down runs once if it is within the hour, and is let go, noted and audited, if older. A time that comes while a backup is running is let go and noted. | Go tests: a follower never runs; a second look and another replica run nothing more; 50 minutes late runs, two and a half hours late does not. |
| BK-08 | **Retention.** After each backup: keep the newest N, or those N days old, or leave it to the bucket's lifecycle rules. Only the console's own files under the prefix are deleted, never the one just written. | Go test of each mode with other objects in the bucket. |
| BK-09 | **Encryption.** SSE-S3 or SSE-KMS (a key ID, or the account's default), chosen in the Destination row (BK-04), and optionally the file itself encrypted on the console (the Encryption row): AES-256-GCM under a key derived with scrypt from a passphrase (12 characters or more) kept in the Secret; the file ends in `.enc` and starts with a line naming how, which is authenticated too. A restore, from the bucket or an uploaded file, opens it with the saved passphrase. | Go tests: the round trip, another passphrase and a changed byte or first line refused; the fake bucket sees the SSE headers. |
| BK-10 | **Status.** The card shows whether the last backup worked, the newest runs (time, by whom or on schedule, the file, its size, old ones removed) and a failure in full; every run and every time let go is in the audit log (`backup.s3.run`), as are saves (`backup.s3.settings`, naming what changed and which keys were replaced, never a value), tests (`backup.s3.test`) and reads for a restore (`backup.s3.read`). Notifications come later. | Go tests of the status and the audit records; vitest of the failure line. |
| BK-11 | **Test connection and Run now.** *Test connection* tries the settings on the card, saved or not: credentials, a small object put, read back (HEAD) and deleted, and a listing of the prefix, stopping at the first that fails. *Run now* takes a backup at once and is refused while one runs. | Go tests against the fake S3, a wrong secret failing at the put; vitest of the steps. |
| BK-12 | **Restore from the bucket.** *List the backups in the bucket* shows the console's files under the prefix, newest first; *Restore…* reads one (opening it when encrypted) into the same picking of parts and the same restore as a file chosen from the computer, the audit record naming `s3://bucket/key`. | Go test through the API: run, list, read, restore a registered part; vitest: the pick reaches the restore card with the other package's part. |
| BK-13 | **The S3 client is the console's own.** SigV4 (package `awsv4`, shared with Sherlock's Bedrock calls) and put, get, head, delete and list (package `s3`), path-style or virtual-host, TLS with a private CA; no AWS SDK in the image. | Go tests: AWS's SigV4 test-suite vectors and S3's documented examples; the client against a fake S3 that checks every signature as S3 does. |
| BK-14 | **Network.** The chart value `backup.s3.egressCIDRs` (empty) with `backup.s3.egressPorts` (`[443]`) opens the network policy to the S3 endpoint; empty adds no rule, and values without `backup` render as before. | `hack/check-chart.sh`: the rule with CIDRs and ports, none by default or with `backup=null`. |
| BK-15 | **Secrets travel sealed with the install's key** (asked November 9, 2026, ADR-137; amends X-01, SE-03, BK-02). **Key:** at start the console reads 256 random bits, 64 hexadecimal characters under `key`, from the Secret `<release>-backup-key` in its namespace, and makes it when there is none; two replicas starting at once end with one key (a conditional create or update: one wins, the other reads it). A key there is never changed or deleted, even one that cannot be read; the Secret is labelled and annotated `helm.sh/resource-policy: keep`, is not the chart's, and outlives `helm uninstall`. The Role names it for `get` (its create is the namespace's Secrets rule). With `connections.store=memory` there is no key and backups hold placeholders. The console never shows, logs or sends the key: a SuperAdmin keeps a copy with `kubectl -n <namespace> get secret <release>-backup-key -o jsonpath='{.data.key}' \| base64 -d`; a lost key means the backups' secrets cannot be recovered. **Sealing:** every secret a part carries (connections' credentials, the OIDC client secret among them; applications' repository tokens, and since 0.13 each application's values file, parameters and commands' parameters (X-01, item 27 of the Phase 13 handover: a file from before holds them in clear and restores as it did; an application whose sealed values this console cannot open, another key's or the placeholder of an install without one, is not restored half: one here is left as it is, one missing is not added, and the report names it and lists *the values and parameters of the application NS/NAME* to set again); Helm repositories' passwords and tokens; Sherlock's key and the AI providers' credentials; the knowledge sources', wiki store's, webhook's and Confluence tokens; notification channels' addresses; local users' password hashes; the SSH authority's private key) is AES-256-GCM with a random nonce per value, written `<sealed:<fingerprint>:<base64>>`, the fingerprint (the first 16 bytes of a SHA-256 of the key) authenticated with it; the file's header names the key by fingerprint (`secrets: {cipher, key}`), never the key; the format stays `221b-backup/2`. **Restore:** with the same key each secret is taken back where the console has none (a secret here stays); with another key or none, the page says so and asks *Restore the rest without the secrets?* (the server answers 409 `backup-key` until `withoutSecrets` is sent); on yes everything else is restored and the report lists every secret not brought back, with its part, its name and where it is set (*Clusters › Prod › Edit connection*), as for a placeholder backup, which restores as before. A secret in clear where a sealed value or placeholder belongs is refused. `GET /api/v1/admin/backup/parts` names the key's fingerprint and Secret. The audit log names the fingerprint only. | Go `TestASealedSecretOpensWithItsKeyOnly`, `TestTheKeyIsMadeOnceByTwoStarters`, `TestSecretsComeBackWithTheSameKeyOnly` (backup), `TestTheBackupKeySecretIsMadeOnce`, `TestHelmRepositoriesBackupWithTheKey` (main), `TestSecretsComeBackWithTheSameBackupKey`, `TestAUserComesBackWithTheirPassword` (api: nothing secret in the file, the logs or the audit log), `TestTheAuthorityComesBackFromABackup` (nodessh), `TestAnApplicationsValuesTravelSealed` (api: a marker in the values, a parameter and a command's parameter is in no file in clear, comes back into the application's Secret and no row with the same key, and the application is not restored with another; `TestABackupHoldsNoSecretsAndRestores` restores a file of before with them in clear); `hack/check-chart.sh` *the backups' key Secret is named in the Role and only read*; vitest *asks whether to restore without the secrets…*, *lists every secret to set again…* in `AdminBackup.test.tsx`; e2e `admin-extra.spec.ts` *a backup sealed with another key…*. |

## Phase 12: Cases

Since wave 5, Cases has three tabs, Inbox, Jobs and Mycroft; Reports is gone, a report being a case in the Inbox, triaged in the one case drawer (CU-01, CU-09 to CU-11), and Known issues is under Mycroft.

Asked for on November 5, 2026: a third area beside Kubernetes and Automation for everything agents and people raise that needs following up, across clusters, so that Administration keeps only configuration; and ways from anywhere in 221B into the report page of the issue service (docs/issues.md). ADR-88 and ADR-89 have the decisions; the mockups are Cases, Approvals, Chat and Report in the Phase 12 design. Runs, Jobs and Knowledge, and the Inbox's proposals, findings and knowledge drafts, are their own packages' (each a file of its own under `web/src/components/cases/`); this section is the area, the Inbox, Reports and Known issues.

| ID | Requirement | Done when |

It is not a SuperAdmin's: it shows to anyone with something in it (ADR-88). A person who holds only the Reporter role still never reaches the console (IS-20). | vitest: the router's routes; a Viewer who may report gets the switch with Kubernetes and Cases. |
| CS-02 | **Its tabs by role:** Inbox for approvers, operators and the console-wide roles (SuperAdmin, SuperViewer); Reports and Known issues for anyone holding the Report permission, operators included; Runs, Jobs and Knowledge for the console-wide roles. A tab not offered opens the first that is; someone with nothing gets a page that says so. Every part asks the server, which decides again. | vitest: the tabs per role, the refusal, and nothing chosen before the issue service answered. |
| CS-03 | **Inbox**: everything waiting for a person, across clusters, as one table since wave 5 (IN-01 to IN-06; in groups IB-01 in wave 4): approvals, proposed changes, cases, findings, reports to triage, known-issue drafts and knowledge drafts. A pane on the left lists the groups with their counts; a group, and Mine and Unassigned, are kept in the address (`?kind=`, `?who=`); New Case and On behalf of are on its bar (CW-02). | vitest: the counts in the pane, one group shown, Mine and Unassigned; e2e: the groups change the address. |
| CS-04 | **The count** on the switch's Cases (until CF-88, which took it off) and on the Inbox tab is what waits for the signed-in person (since wave 5, everything pending in the Inbox for them, IN-07): the approvals they may decide and, since 0.13.0, the ones they asked for themselves that someone else decides (IN-11: the Inbox counts everything in it), for an operator the open reports nobody took or they own that are not waiting on the reporter, and the known issues an agent proposed, plus the proposed changes, cases, findings and knowledge drafts their owners count (IB-05). The lists are read once for the top bar and the Inbox together, every 30 seconds while shown. While a list cannot be read because the database is away the count is not known, and the tab shows **?** in place of a number (DB-15). | vitest of the counting; e2e: the count shows. |

| CS-06 | **Approvals moved here** from Administration (rows of the Inbox's table since wave 5, each with its drawer, IN-06), the same rules: approving takes the approval permission (a SuperAdmin) as before, nothing is approved on the list itself, and a console change is decided on its row. Mine keeps what the person asked for, Unassigned what is not decided yet. Administration loses its Approvals page; `#/admin/approvals` opens the Inbox on approvals; the gear's yellow *i* says what waits (IN-07). | vitest of the moved list and its filters; e2e: the old link, the gear's flag. |

Each row opens its drawer at `#/cases/reports/<n>`, filters kept. | e2e: a report found by its known issue, its drawer at its address. |

The operator who filed it still writes on it as an operator (replies, internal notes); only on a report of their own problem do they write as its reporter. | e2e: both tabs open, the second saying who it is for; Go test: support's note and reply on a report they filed. |
