## Phase 12: Cases (work items)

Since wave 5 a case and a report are one object (CU-01 to CU-14): a case's statuses are the issue service's, its priorities P1 to P5, what it is about any words, and Cases › Cases is gone, every case being in the Inbox; CU- requirements win where they differ.

Asked for on November 6, 2026: *New report* in Cases becomes *New Case*, and a case is a new object of its own, apart from problem reports, which people still file through `/report`, the account menu and Sherlock. A case is a problem being worked on until it is done: opened by a person, by hand or from a finding, analysed by an agent that suggests a fix with the Watson command to run and proposes changes, and completed with a root-cause analysis (RCA) that goes to Knowledge. ADR-105 to ADR-107 have the decisions. The server side is package `casework` (`server/internal/casework`, mounted by `internal/api/casework.go`); the screens are `web/src/components/cases/CaseList.tsx`, `CaseDrawer.tsx`, `NewCase.tsx` and `InboxCases.tsx`. The findings' actions (*New Case*, *Ignore*), the exceptions and the RCAs' pages in Knowledge are the FX section's; the Inbox's groups are the IB section's.

| ID | Requirement | Done when |
| --- | --- | --- |
| CW-01 | **A case** is numbered C-1, C-2… and has a title, a detail, a priority P1 to P4 (the reports' scale, IS-05), the cluster and, optionally, the object it is about (`namespace/kind/name` or `kind/name`), what it was opened from (a finding with its run, or by hand), who opened it and when, a status, its history (who did what and when, the first and the newest 60 kept), the agent's last analysis with its suggested fix, the proposed changes (Watson cards) it made, and its RCA once complete. Its statuses: *new*, *processing* while the agent works on it, *fixProposed* once it suggested a fix or proposed a change, and *complete* once a person gave its RCA. | Go tests of package `casework`. |
| CW-02 | **New Case** replaces *New report* on the bars of Cases › Inbox and Reports, and is on Cases › Cases: it opens the create-case form at `#/cases/cases/new` (title, what you know, cluster, priority, the object, checked as `namespace/kind/name` or `kind/name`), which opens the case and then its drawer. Only those who work the cases see it. *On behalf of…* stays for operators. | vitest; e2e `casework.spec.ts` and `cases.spec.ts`. |
| CW-03 | **A case from a finding** (`POST /api/v1/cases/cases {"fromFinding": "<id>"}`) takes the finding's title, detail and evidence, cluster, object and priority (its own, else from its severity: critical P1, warning P3, info P4), and names the finding, its run, job and check; the finding is then marked as made a case (`casework.Findings.LinkCase`). A finding with an open case already is refused; one that cannot be marked takes the case away again. | Go test `TestACaseFromAFinding` with a fake of the findings. |
| CW-04 | **The case analysis job.** Opening a case starts the Troubleshooter on its cluster, read only, as a run of the new event-started job *Case analysis* (kind `case-analysis`, event `case`, $3 a run and $15 a day by default), through the jobs' machinery: off until a SuperAdmin turns it on, budgeted, listed in Jobs › Runs with `case:C-12` as its reference, cancellable and audited like every run. *Analyse again* starts it once more on a new case or one with a fix proposed. Paused, nothing is analysed and the case's history says so. An analysis a replica left behind (it went down or lost the lead) is let go by the leader, and the case is new again. | Go tests `TestOpeningACaseAnalysesIt`, `TestAnAnalysisLeftBehindIsLetGo`; e2e. |
| CW-05 | **Changing an open case**: its title, detail and priority (`PUT /api/v1/cases/cases/{id}`), each change in its history and the audit log. A complete case is not changed; it is reopened first. | Go test; e2e: the priority changed from the drawer. |
| CW-06 | **What the analysis gives**: a summary for operators, the cause, the evidence, and a suggested fix (what to do, and at most five kubectl or helm commands Watson runs, checked as Watson parses them; others are left out with a note on the run). The changes it proposes become Watson cards with `source {kind: "case", id: "C-12", label: "Case C-12: <title>"}` and dedup key `case:C-12:<title>`, on the case's cluster; a fix suggested without a proposal becomes one. With a fix or a card, the case is *fixProposed*; otherwise *new* again, with the analysis kept. The case's and finding's text reach the agent between tags as untrusted data. An analysis skipped because the job was busy says so on the case (*The analysis was skipped: the case analysis job was busy with other cases*, 0.13.0; the seventh cluster test's N5): the day's skips of the job are one run, named after the first case skipped, and another case's run is neither named in its history nor listed among its runs. | Go tests `TestASkippedAnalysisNamesNoOtherCasesRun`, `TestOpeningACaseAnalysesIt`, `TestASuggestedFixBecomesACard`, `TestAFailedAnalysisSaysWhy`. |
| CW-07 | **Cases › Cases** lists the cases, open first, then by priority, then newest, with filters in the address (status, priority, cluster, Mine, words); each row shows its object with how long it has been running (`ObjectLink`), its status and how long it has been open. A case opens in its drawer at `#/cases/cases/<number>`: priority, status, how long open, the cluster and object, what it was opened from (a finding links to its run), the detail, the analysis with its run, the suggested fix with **Open in Watson** beside each command (Watson on the case's cluster with the command filled in, which the person runs as themselves), the proposed changes with their card's state now and a link to each, the RCA, and the history. A case being analysed is read again every 5 seconds. | vitest `CaseList.test.tsx`; e2e. |
| CW-08 | **Complete with an RCA.** *Complete…* opens a form: the cause (required), the fix applied, prevention and notes; *Draft with the agent* asks the case analysis job for a draft from the case's record (its history, analysis and proposed changes with their state), which the person edits; nothing of the draft is kept until saved. Saving gives the RCA to Knowledge (`casework.Lessons.AddRCA`) and then completes the case, naming the RCA's id there; if Knowledge refuses it, the case stays open and says why. Without Knowledge, the RCA is kept on the case. *Superseded by CF-44: an RCA is part of its case only.* | Go tests `TestChangeCompleteAndReopen`, `TestTheAgentDraftsAnRCA`; vitest; e2e. |
| CW-09 | **Reopen** a complete case: it is new again, its history kept, its RCA staying in Knowledge (the history names it). | Go test; e2e. |
| CW-10 | **The API**, `/api/v1/cases/cases`: `GET` (filters `status`, `open`, `waiting`, `cluster`, `priority`, `mine`, `q`; with the clusters, `mayChange` and how the cases name the person), `POST` (`fromFinding`, or `title`, `detail`, `cluster`, `resource`, `priority`), `GET`/`PUT /{id}` (by `C-12` or `12`), `POST /{id}/complete`, `/reopen`, `/rca-draft` and `/analyze`. Reading is for the approvers (`cases.approverRoles`, a SuperAdmin always), the console-wide roles (SuperAdmin, SuperViewer) and those who may edit jobs; opening, changing, completing, reopening and drafting are the approvers'. Every route runs on the leader (ADR-70). | Go test `TestCaseworkAccess`: a Viewer refused, a SuperViewer reads, an Admin works them, a Viewer reads once Viewers may edit jobs. |
| CW-11 | **In the Inbox**: the *Cases* group lists the cases waiting for a person (new, or with a fix proposed), P1 first, then the longest waiting, each with its object, status and how long it has waited, and *Open* or *Review*; *Mine* keeps the ones the person opened. Its count (`useCasesCount`) is what waits. | vitest. |
| CW-12 | **Audit.** `case.create`, `case.update`, `case.analyze` (started again, ended, let go), `case.draft`, `case.complete`, `case.reopen`, each with the case and its cluster; never the detail's or the RCA's text. | Go tests read the records. |
| CW-13 | **Storage and backup.** The cases are kept in a new ConfigMap, `<release>-casework` (`CASEWORK_CONFIGMAP`, `-casework-configmap`), key `cases.json`, at most 512 KiB: the oldest complete cases go first, then the history of old open ones; a new case that does not fit beside the open ones is refused. Text a person or an agent writes is scrubbed of credentials before it is kept. A backup part *Cases* (order 260) holds them whole; a restore adds the missing ones, keeps those here, numbers on after both, and a case being analysed then is new again. The simulator serves the ConfigMap and `make e2e-sim` names it. | Go tests `TestTheCasesFitTheirKey`, `TestBackupOfTheCases`, `TestTheCasesHaveRoomInTheirConfigMap`; `hack/check-chart.sh`. |
| CW-14 | **Proposed changes and their case refer to each other**: the case lists its cards, and each card's source names the case (`#/cases/cases/<number>`); a run of the case analysis job links to its case. | Go test; e2e: the card's source is the case. |

## Phase 12: Jobs

Asked for on November 5, 2026: agents that act on their own, with nobody at the screen, for read-only work: a schedule or an event starts them, they report and find, and any change they want waits for a person. Cases › Jobs › Schedule is where every such job is seen, run, paused and edited, the scheduled backup among them; Cases › Jobs › Runs keeps what each run made (FX-13). The issue service's investigator is one of these jobs. ADR-90 to ADR-93 have the decisions; the mockup is Triggers (Cases › Jobs) in the Phase 12 design.

| ID | Requirement | Done when |
| --- | --- | --- |
| JB-01 | **Kinds of jobs.** A registry of kinds (package `jobs`) that any package adds to with a label, an agent, what it makes, what starts it by default and a function that does one run. The console's own: **Nightly audit** (Auditor: new RBAC risks, volumes nearly full, practices not met; every day at 02:00), **Alert diagnosis** (Troubleshooter, on each new alert of warning and above), **Daily digest** (Summarizer: what changed in 24 hours, from the audit log and the runs; weekdays at 08:00) and **Report triage** (Troubleshooter, on each new report, package `investigate`). Each kind has a job of its own; a person may add more of a kind (*Add job…*) and remove those. Since KN-28, package `knowledge` registers a kind for each of Mycroft's sources, **Wiki sync: <source>**, changed with its source and taken off the registry when it is removed (`Registry.Unregister`); such a kind is `Single` (no *Add job…*) and `NoFindings` (its runs make no findings and say what they did as facts); an event may name the one kind it starts (`Event.JobKind`); a kind that calls its agent by itself counts the calls on the run with `RunEnv.Spend`, within the run's budget. | Go tests: the kinds' jobs are listed, an added job runs and is removed, a kind's own is not; a source's kind takes no added job, an event for one kind starts its job alone, a run counts what its kind spent and keeps its facts (`TestOneJobPerSourceKN28`). |
| JB-02 | **The scheduled backup is a job too.** *Backup to S3* is listed with its schedule, bucket, last run (size, kept N) and next run; *Run now* and *Pause*/*Resume* drive the backups' own scheduler (ADR-81), which keeps its schedule; its settings stay in Administration › Backup. | vitest: the row and the drawer say where it is set; Go: pausing saves the backups' settings off. |
| JB-03 | **Off until a SuperAdmin turns them on**, each with its own budget. Every change to a job is in the audit log (`job.update`) with its old and new values, and names who set the trigger; each run's record names the agent and that person. | Go test: jobs start paused; the record of a change says `state paused → on`, the schedule's old and new words, the clusters and budgets. |
| JB-04 | **Who may edit jobs**: a SuperAdmin, and the roles Administration › AI › Jobs adds (Admin, SuperViewer, Viewer). The runs and findings are for SuperAdmins and SuperViewers, who read across every namespace as agents on their own may, and for those roles. The settings are a SuperAdmin's (`job.settings`). | Go tests (package jobs and the API with local sign-in): a Viewer is refused, a SuperViewer reads, an Admin changes jobs once named. |
| JB-05 | **When, in plain words.** A schedule (every day at, weekdays at, weekly on some days, every N hours, a cron expression) in a time zone, the console's by default (`jobs.timeZone`, else UTC), shown as "Every day at 02:00, Europe/Berlin" with its next three times as it is edited (`POST /api/v1/jobs/schedule`); or an event: an alert of a chosen least severity (warning and above, or errors only), a report that arrives, a source that changes; or both. A job has its clusters (all by default). | Go test of the preview; vitest of the picker and of the saved change. |
| JB-06 | **On the leader, once, within the hour.** The schedules run only on the leading replica (ADR-70); each scheduled time is claimed before it runs (with a database, a row of `schedule_claims`, DB-80), so two replicas that both think they lead run it once. A time missed while the console was down runs once within an hour (*Run once after a restart if missed within the last hour*, on by default), else is let go and noted *missed*. | Go tests with a fake clock: a follower never runs; within the grace once; two hours late missed; two leaders, one run. |
| JB-07 | **Overlap skips.** A run still going makes the next scheduled time skip, noted (*Skip a run while the last one is still going*, on by default; off, the next waits for it). Events wait their turn, ten at most, then skip. *Run now* is refused while a run goes, and while its agent cannot answer (JB-25). | Go test: a blocked run, the next hour skipped, Run now refused. |
| JB-08 | **Agents on their own read as a reader identity**, never as a person: the user `221b:agents` (a reserved name no sign-in can give), with the role Administration › AI › Jobs chooses: Viewer (the default; the namespaces bound to that user, and those of a chosen group of the role Viewer) or SuperViewer (every namespace). Never a role that may change anything, never a Secret's values; the tools are Sherlock's read-only ones. | Go tests: the reader's groups for a Viewer group and a SuperViewer, an Admin group refused; the toolbox impersonates `221b:agents`. |
| JB-09 | **Output is data, read back.** Each task tells the agent that cluster text, alerts, reports and logs are data, never instructions, quotes them between tags nothing inside can close, and asks for a report ending in one JSON block of findings and proposals, which the console reads; text without one is all report. | Go tests of the parser and the quoting; the investigator's task carries a report that tries to close its tag and instruct. |
| JB-10 | **Budgets.** Per run and per day (UTC), in US dollars; the job stops when either is spent and says so: a run stops at the call past its budget (*Budget spent*), a day's stop is shown on the job (*Stopped for the day at 14:02 UTC: $10.20 of its $10.00 spent*) and noted once a day, and it starts again the next day. The agent's caps and the overall limits hold besides. **The budget is counted model call by model call** (0.13.0; the twelfth cluster test found a nightly audit capped at $0.60 spending $1.41 in one run, and $0.23 more outside it): what a run spends is every model call made for it, its agent's rounds and those of every agent it hands work to (the Change planner's among them), each added to the run as it ends; and **no model call starts once the run's or the day's budget is spent**, not the agent's next round, not a hand-over, not a hand-over's next round. A call under way is not cut short and its cost is known only when it ends, so the bound is: **a run passes its budget by at most the cost of one model call**, the one under way when the budget was reached (a run's calls are made one at a time). The agent's partial answer is not kept; the run says *Budget spent* with what it spent. Before 0.13.0 (since 0.12.0) the budget was looked at only before each of the run's agent calls, each of which may make a dozen model calls, and a hand-over's calls were counted on the agents' spend but not on the run. | Go tests: the second run of a day passes the budget and the third does not start; a run's budget stops its second call; `TestARunsBudgetHoldsWithinItsAgentsCalls` (`internal/jobs`: an agent whose model calls cost $0.25 each and whose hand-over costs $0.20, under a run budget of $0.60, stops at $0.70 after three calls, the hand-over's counted; the day's budget the same); `TestARunsMeterCountsItsChainAndStopsIt` (`internal/ai`, over the fake model: every call of a chain of hand-overs counted, equal to what the agents' spend says; past the budget no round and no hand-over starts). |
| JB-11 | **Alerts, once each.** The leader reads every cluster's Alerts tab (neither resolved nor acknowledged ones) every 30 seconds; a new alert, or one that became more severe, starts the alert jobs that take its severity and cluster. The alerts there when a job is turned on, or when a replica starts to lead, are only noted (*On the next alert*; *Run now* diagnoses up to three of those there are). A repeat of the same alert within 24 hours, on a new leader too, updates the run it started (*N repeats*) instead of running again. | Go test: the alerts there before are noted; a new one, one run in two looks; a repeat on another replica; a day on, a run; errors only ignores a warning. |
| JB-12 | **Proposals wait for a person.** A command an agent proposes (kubectl or helm, that Watson would run) becomes a proposal through the Proposer, with the agent, what started it (*Alert CrashLoopBackOff on shop/web-7f9 at 03:12*), who set the trigger, the cluster, the reasoning and evidence, the actions with impact and risk, a condition (the alert still firing), a key that makes a newer proposal replace an older, and an expiry a day on; the run keeps their ids. Anything else is left out, noted. Nothing a job does changes anything. | Go test: one proposal of three, its JSON in the shared shape, the refused and the non-kubectl command noted. |
| JB-13 | **Findings.** Each with a severity (critical, warning, info), a priority (FX-02), a kind of check, a title, detail, evidence, cluster and resource; open until someone makes a case of it (*New Case*, FX-07) or ignores it (*Ignore*: an accepted exception, FX-03, or a known issue, JR-10), each audited (`job.finding`), and listed in Cases › Inbox until then, by priority and then the oldest first; *Mine* keeps those of jobs whose trigger the person set. Findings acknowledged or dismissed before 0.12.0's cases stay as they were (FX-01). | Go test of the states and the audit; vitest of the Inbox's list and count. |
| JB-14 | **Runs kept.** Each run (the job, what started it and who set the trigger, the agent, the model, start and end, status and note, the report, findings, proposals' ids, tokens and cost) kept in the database, at most 400 (until 0.13 in the ConfigMap `<release>-runs`, and at most 720 KiB too; DB-80): the oldest go first, a run under way never. A run its replica left under way past its time is let go as *interrupted*. | Go tests: by count (`TestRunsAreKeptByCount`), the swept run. |
| JB-15 | **Cases › Jobs.** The list as the mockup draws it: the job with its agent and clusters, when in plain words with its zone, the budget, the last run (with a link to it and what it made), the next run (*On the next alert* for an event), the state (On, Paused, Stopped for the day, On but cannot run with why). A job opens in a drawer (`#/cases/jobs/<id>`) with *Run now*, *Pause*/*Turn on*, its settings (the schedule picker, the events, the clusters, the budgets, the two checkboxes) with *Save changes*, and its runs, each opening to its output and cost. An agent's drawer lists its jobs under *On its own*. | vitest: the list, Run now, Pause, a saved schedule, budget and clusters, an event's severity, the backup's row, a reader's view, the runs. |
| JB-16 | **Cases › Jobs › Runs** (FX-13, JR-03). Every run, newest first, filtered by status and *Jobs I set*: when it started and ended, the job and agent, what started it (with its repeats and a link to the report or alert), the status, what it made, its cost; one opens (`#/cases/jobs/runs/<id>`) to its report, findings (each with its priority, the object it is about, *New Case* and *Ignore*, or the case or exception it went to), what accepted exceptions left out, proposals and tokens; a run under way may be cancelled there (FX-12). | vitest of the list and of a run. |
| JB-17 | **API.** `GET/POST /api/v1/jobs`, `GET/PUT/DELETE /api/v1/jobs/{id}`, `POST /api/v1/jobs/{id}/run`, `GET /api/v1/jobs/{id}/runs`, `GET/PUT /api/v1/jobs/settings`, `POST /api/v1/jobs/schedule`, `GET /api/v1/runs[/{id}]`, `POST /api/v1/runs/{id}/cancel`, `GET /api/v1/findings`, `PUT /api/v1/findings/{id}/priority`, `POST /api/v1/findings/{id}/ignore` (and `/reopen`; FX-14); every path is the leader's (ADR-70). | Go test of each route and its refusals. |
| JB-18 | **Administration › AI › Jobs**, a SuperAdmin's: who may edit jobs, and what agents on their own read as. | vitest through the Settings page's section. |
| JB-19 | **Report investigation** (IS-16): the issue service's investigator is the report triage job. The Troubleshooter, read-only, on the clusters the report's service runs on (or those the job covers, three at most), is given the report's text and the reporter's messages as untrusted data, the services and the known issues open now, planned maintenance among them; it returns the likely service with its confidence, the cause, the evidence, a technical summary and a plain one naming nothing the reporter does not use, optionally a reply for an operator to send, a known issue to propose (a draft an operator accepts) and a fix (a proposal). A fix marked done is checked again: the original symptom gone or not. With the job paused or the Troubleshooter off, nothing is investigated and operators triage by hand. Report work has its own daily budget (the job's, $20 by default), apart from the agents' other work. | Go tests through the issue service with the fake Claude API: a paused job finds nothing; a report's finding, draft known issue, proposal, run and audit; the fix checked; the budget stops it. |
| JB-20 | **Audit.** Each run (`job.run`): the job, the agent, what started it, who set the trigger, the status, findings and proposals with their ids, the model, tokens and cost, never the report's text; each agent call (`ai.agent`) names the job and run as who asked. | Go tests read the records. |
| JB-21 | **Backup.** A part *Jobs* (order 220): each job's settings and state and who may edit jobs; a restore replaces them, counts their schedules from then, and refuses a reader role that may change anything. The runs and findings are left out (a history, possibly large; the next runs make new ones). | Go test of the round trip and the refusal. |
| JB-22 | **Chart.** The ConfigMap `<release>-runs`, created empty, in the Role's names, named in `RUNS_CONFIGMAP` (`-runs-configmap`); `jobs.timeZone` (empty: UTC) as `JOBS_TIME_ZONE`. | `hack/check-chart.sh`. |
| JB-23 | **An audit whose identity cannot read a cluster says so** (0.13.0; the sixth cluster test's O1), in place of reporting nothing found. Before a run of a kind whose agent's findings go into cases (the nightly audit, the alert diagnosis, the daily digest), the console asks Kubernetes, with SubjectAccessReviews it makes, whether the reader identity (JB-08: `221b:agents` with its groups) may list pods, Deployments or events across each cluster the run covers, or the pods of one of its namespaces (the first 100). Nodes and namespaces do not count: a Viewer reader lists them out of the box. A cluster it may read none of is not given to the agent: the run names it, *the agents' identity (221b:agents) cannot read any namespace of prod, so nothing there was looked at: set Administration › AI › Jobs › reader role, or bind a role to 221b:agents in the cluster*, and counts it among the clusters that did not answer (W-2: *Done, with errors*), outside the run's scope. When it can read none of them, the run does not ask the agent and ends `noAccess`, shown as *Cannot read the cluster* in red with that note on the run and on AIOps › Jobs › Runs, health *Failed*, audited as failed; its findings are not routed, so nothing counts as gone from it: no fix is verified, no validation rule counts it (`counted_runs`), no resolved known issue is left. An answer that does not come is no answer, and the run goes on. With sign-in off the console reads as itself and is never blind. | Go `TestAnAuditThatCannotReadSaysSoJB23` (jobs), `TestReaderBlindJB23` (api, the fake authorizer refusing); vitest `RunsTab.test.tsx` *says an audit could not read its cluster…*. |
| JB-24 | **A run says its agent, model and effort** (phase-13.1-ai, the owner, November 22, 2026). Jobs › Runs: each row reads, under the job's name, `Auditor · Claude Opus 5.5 · high` (the agent, the model it ended on, the highest effort a step ran at), then its clusters; since JB-26 the agent alone, the *Model* and *Effort* columns saying the rest. The run's page has *Agent*, *Model* with its provider (`Claude Opus 5.5 · Anthropic`) and *Effort* as facts; under Auto the effort reads `high, picked: a P0 case`, and a model moved up during the run `· moved up from Claude Sonnet 5.5 at step 2`. Kept on the run (`job_runs.ai_provider_name`, `effort`, `pick_method`, `pick_why`, `moved_up_step`, `moved_up_from_model`, `moved_up_why`, step `0052`), so an old run says what it ran with after the agent's settings change; a run from before 0.14.0 shows its model alone. | Go `TestARunKeepsWhatItsStepsRanWithAI48` (jobs); vitest `RunsTab.test.tsx` *says what each run ran with in the Model and Effort columns…* (JB-26), *shows the run's agent, model with its provider and effort as facts…*. |
| JB-25 | **A job whose agent is off says so, and is not run by hand** (phase-13.1-ai, the owner, November 23, 2026: *Run now* on the Daily digest said "Daily digest started." and the run failed 0.1 s later with "the Summarizer is off", which the Jobs tab did not show). *Run now*, *Rebuild now* and a start by hand from elsewhere (a knowledge source added or changed, KN-28) first ask the agents whether the job's agent can answer at all (`ai.Service.Available`, on no cluster in particular); one turned off is refused with a 409: *The Daily digest runs on the Summarizer, which is off: turn it on in Administration › AI › Agents.* (the job's name and its agent's), and one whose provider cannot answer with its own reason (*…which cannot answer now: the Summarizer's provider "x" is gone; pick another in Administration › AI › Agents.*). No run is recorded and the drawer shows the refusal where its actions' errors go, with no *started* toast. The jobs' view (`GET /api/v1/jobs`) carries `agentOff` ("the Summarizer is off"), on or paused; the Jobs tab shows it under the job's name in a warning tone with a link to the agent in Administration › AI › Agents, the state of a job on reads *On, cannot run*, and the drawer has a warning with the same link. Turning such a job on is allowed (the agent may be turned on next): the drawer warns before, and the toast after (*Daily digest resumed. The Summarizer is off: its runs fail until it is turned on…*). A last run that failed or was stopped (failed, interrupted, cancelled, budget spent, cannot read the cluster) shows its status and note (merged, as Runs shows it) on the row's *Last run*, linked to the run, and in a callout in the drawer. **Scheduled and event runs are not refused**: nobody is at the screen to read a refusal, so they run and fail as before, recorded with the note (*the Summarizer is off*) on Jobs › Runs and on the job's row. | Go `TestRunByHandIsRefusedWhileTheAgentIsOff`, `TestAScheduledRunOfAnAgentOffFailsWithTheNote` (jobs), `TestProvidersAndAgents` (ai, `Available`); vitest `JobsTab.test.tsx` *shows Run now’s refusal in the drawer, and no “started”*, *says on the row and in the drawer that the agent is off…*, *shows a failed last run’s reason on the row and in the drawer…*. |
| JB-26 | **Model and Effort on both tables of AIOps › Jobs** (phase-13.1-ai, the owner, November 23, 2026). *Schedule*: each job's agent's model and effort as set now: the model (`Claude Opus 5.5`; `Default (Claude Opus 5.5)` when the agent uses its provider's default; `Auto (Claude Sonnet 5.5 to Claude Opus 5.5)` when Auto picks it, from its floor model to the highest tier its provider offers), *on* its provider under it; the effort (Default, a level, or Auto with *at least medium* under it when a floor is set). They come on the jobs' list (`GET /api/v1/jobs`, each job's `setup`), so a person who may see the jobs but not Administration › AI sees them; a job without an agent reads —. *Runs*: what the run ran with (kept since step `0052`, JB-24): the model, *on* its provider and *moved up from* the model it started on under it; the effort, with *Auto:* and why when Auto picked it (a run's row carries `pickWhy` and `movedUp`); a run from before 0.14.0 its model alone and — for the effort. Both tables now sort by any column (a heading; Schedule's *Job* in the jobs' own order, Runs newest first by default), choose their columns (*Columns*) and export the rows in view (Excel, CSV, JSON: Model and Effort with the provider and what it moved up from as their own columns), as the console's other tables do. | Go `TestAnAgentsSetupForTheJobsList` (ai), `TestTheJobsListSaysEachAgentsModelAndEffort` (jobs); vitest `JobsTab.test.tsx` *shows them in their columns, sorts by them, hides them in the column chooser, and exports them*, `RunsTab.test.tsx` *says what each run ran with in the Model and Effort columns…*. |

## Phase 12: Findings, exceptions and RCAs

Asked for on November 6, 2026: findings get a priority; *Dismiss* becomes *Ignore*, which records an accepted exception in Knowledge that later runs respect, and *Acknowledge* becomes *New Case*; a case completed with an RCA records it in Knowledge; Jobs has two sub-tabs, Schedule and Runs; a running job can be cancelled. Packages `jobs` (findings, cancelling) and `lessons` (exceptions and RCAs); cases themselves are package `casework`'s (CW-). ADR-108 to ADR-110 have the decisions.

| ID | Requirement | Done when |
| --- | --- | --- |
| FX-01 | **A finding's states**: *open*; *case* (a case was made of it, named with a link); *ignored* (an exception was accepted for it, named with a link). Who set it and when are kept. A finding acknowledged or dismissed before cases and exceptions keeps that state, shown as *Acknowledged (before cases)* or *Dismissed (before exceptions)*, and is never made a case or an exception. | Go test of the states; vitest of how each reads. |
| FX-02 | **Priority** P1 to P5 since wave 5 (JR-08), the cases' scale: a finding starts at its severity's (critical P2, warning P4, info P5), and a person who may decide findings, or edit jobs, changes it (`PUT /api/v1/findings/{id}/priority`, audited `job.finding` with old and new). A case made of a finding takes its priority. | Go test; vitest of the picker. |
| FX-03 | **Ignore** (its *Accept as exception*, JR-09; or a known issue, JR-10) asks why (required) and, optionally, the last day it applies; it records an accepted exception (`exception.add`): the cluster, the resource, the kind of check, the finding's title and detail, where it came from (finding, run, job), the reason, who and when, and the expiry. The finding becomes *ignored* and names the exception. A finding already a case or ignored is refused. | Go tests (packages `jobs`, `lessons`); vitest of the dialog and its errors; e2e `findings.spec.ts`. |
| FX-04 | **Later runs respect exceptions.** An agent's task lists the exceptions active on its cluster, as data; and the console drops a finding the agent still returns when an active exception has the same cluster, resource and check. The check is the finding's `check` (a short id the agents now give, `rbac.cluster-admin-binding`) or, without one, its title made plain (lower case, numbers as `#`, words only). The run says how many were left out. Since 0.13.0 (the seventh cluster test's N3) an agent that leaves out a problem because an exception covers it says so in its output's `"covered"` (the exceptions' ids, read leniently: `EX-4`, `ex-4`, `4`, or `{"exception": "EX-4"}`; anything else is nothing), and that counts as the exception's match as a finding it drops does: once a run, only an active exception of the cluster the agent was asked about, never twice with a finding of the same run; the run says *the agent left out what EX-4 covered, counted as its match*. | Go tests: two exceptions leave two findings out, one expires, one is removed; `TestAnExceptionTheAgentSaysCoveredIsMatchedFX04` (casework), `TestWhatAnExceptionCoveredReachesTheRouterFX04` (jobs); e2e: the next nightly audit leaves the ignored finding out. |
| FX-05 | **Expiry and removal.** An exception past its last day (to its end, UTC) stops applying; one removed (`DELETE /api/v1/cases/exceptions/{id}`, `exception.remove`) stops applying at once and stays listed as removed with who removed it. Reopening an ignored finding leaves its exception until someone removes it. | Go tests; vitest; e2e. |
| FX-06 | **Who.** The findings, exceptions and RCAs are read by those who may approve proposed changes (`cases.approverRoles`, a SuperAdmin always), the console-wide roles and the job editors. *New Case*, *Ignore* and removing an exception are the approvers'; the priority and *Reopen* also the job editors'. The server decides; the pages show only what the person may do. | Go tests (packages `jobs`, `lessons` and the API with local sign-in). |
| FX-07 | **New Case** creates a case from the finding (`createCase({fromFinding})`, package `casework`), which reads it (`jobs.Service.Finding`) and links it (`jobs.Service.LinkCase`): the finding becomes *case* with the case's id, once; linking it to another case, or an ignored one, is refused. | Go test of the link; vitest of the button. |
| FX-08 | **RCAs.** Completing a case records its RCA (`lessons.Service.AddRCA`, `rca.add`): the case, its title, cluster and resource, the cause, the fix applied, the prevention and any more, who and when. *Superseded by CF-44: an RCA is part of its case only.* | Go test. |
| FX-09 | **RCAs in the wiki too.** When Mycroft's wiki has a store (git or S3), each RCA is also a page, `pages/rca/rca-<n>.md`, written straight into the approved pages (a person decided it already) and found by `ask_knowledge` at once. Without a wiki the console's records hold them; a page that cannot be written is noted on the record. Accepted exceptions are the console's records only and are no longer written to the wiki (MY-09); one written there before keeps its page, which says so once the exception is removed. Mycroft may not write pages of these kinds. *Superseded by CF-44: an RCA is part of its case only.* | Go tests (packages `knowledge`, `lessons`). |
| FX-10 | **Cases › Mycroft › Exceptions and RCAs** (MY-01). The exceptions, active first (expired and removed on asking), each with why, where, the check, who and when, its expiry, a link to the finding's run, and *Remove* for an approver; the RCAs, newest first, each with its case and wiki page. Each has an address: `#/cases/mycroft/exceptions/<id>`, `#/cases/mycroft/rcas/<id>`; the old `#/cases/knowledge/exceptions/<id>` and `#/cases/knowledge/rcas/<id>` open them. | vitest; e2e. |
| FX-11 | **Kept and backed up.** The keys `exceptions.json` (128 KiB) and `rcas.json` (288 KiB) of the casework ConfigMap: expired and removed exceptions go first, an active one never (a new one is refused while only active ones fill it); the oldest RCAs go first. A backup part *Exceptions and RCAs* (order 270) adds them where missing on a restore. | Go tests of the limits and the round trip. |
| FX-12 | **Cancel a run.** A run under way shows *Cancel run* to job editors and a SuperAdmin (`POST /api/v1/runs/{id}/cancel`, on the leader where runs run): its agent's call is cut short, what it found so far is kept, what it spent counts, and it ends *cancelled* with who cancelled it (`job.cancel`). A run marked running that no replica runs any more is marked cancelled at once. | Go test; vitest. |
| FX-13 | **Jobs › Schedule and Runs.** Cases › Jobs has two sub-tabs: *Schedule* (`#/cases/jobs`, a job at `#/cases/jobs/<id>`) and *Runs* (`#/cases/jobs/runs`, a run at `#/cases/jobs/runs/<id>`); Runs is no longer a tab of its own, and an old `#/cases/runs[/<id>]` link opens Jobs › Runs. | vitest of the routes; e2e. |
| FX-14 | **API.** `PUT /api/v1/findings/{id}/priority` `{priority}`, `POST /api/v1/findings/{id}/ignore` `{reason, expires?}` (a day, `YYYY-MM-DD`), `POST /api/v1/findings/{id}/reopen`, `POST /api/v1/runs/{id}/cancel`; `GET /api/v1/cases/exceptions[/{id}]`, `DELETE /api/v1/cases/exceptions/{id}`, `GET /api/v1/cases/rcas[/{id}]`. A run's answer says whether the person may cancel it and decide its findings (`mayCancel`, `mayDecide`). The old `acknowledge` and `dismiss` are gone. | Go tests of each route and its refusals. |

## Phase 12: Knowledge

Asked for on November 5, 2026: Mycroft keeps what the organization knows about its infrastructure in one wiki, written from the sources a SuperAdmin connects, reviewed by people before it is published, and read by every agent through `ask_knowledge`; and the issue service's known issues, with Mycroft's approved pages, are published to Confluence (removed in 0.12.1, KN-38; pages kept elsewhere are live sources since, KN-39 to KN-43). ADR-94 to ADR-98 have the decisions; the mockups are Knowledge (Administration › AI › Knowledge) and Cases in the Phase 12 design. Package `knowledge`, the Confluence client and publisher in package `confluence`; the records in the ConfigMap `<release>-knowledge`, every credential in the Secret `<release>-knowledge`.

| ID | Requirement | Done when |
| --- | --- | --- |
| KN-01 | **Sources.** A SuperAdmin adds, changes, tests (since KN-26 in the source's dialog, as typed), switches off and removes sources in Administration › AI › Knowledge: a Confluence space (Cloud with an account's email and API token, or Data Center with a personal access token; a read-only credential), a git repository of runbooks by HTTPS (branch, directory, token, private CA), since KN-25 also an S3 bucket of documents, uploaded documents, and *This console*, there from the start (clusters, nodes and hosts with their facts, fleets, Helm applications and Automation scripts, read through the console's services: no Secret's value, credential, host key, values file or script text). Each credential is kept in the Secret `<release>-knowledge` and shown only as set or not, never in a page, a log, an audit record, a prompt or a backup. | Go tests: a source's token is in no answer of the API; a plain-HTTP repository is refused; only a SuperAdmin changes sources. Vitest: the drawer sends the source with its token, which never comes back. e2e `knowledge.spec.ts`. |
| KN-02 | **Documents** are read when uploaded or replaced: markdown and plain text, Word (`.docx`, its paragraphs), HTML, CSV (its rows, MY-05), and PDF by the text its pages draw with simple fonts (most CID fonts give nothing, and the upload says so), up to 20 MiB. Their text is held only until Mycroft has read it; the excerpts, the manifest and the pages it writes are kept, the raw file is not. | Go tests of each kind; a removed document leaves the index. |
| KN-03 | **Only what changed is read.** A manifest beside the pages (`manifest.json`) keeps each source item's id, version, hash, when it was read and last seen, the pages that draw on it and the hosts it lists. An item whose version (a Confluence page's version, a git blob, the console item's hash), or failing that its scrubbed text's hash, is unchanged is marked seen and not read or sent to a model again. Each source is read by its own Wiki sync job (KN-28): a Confluence space's scheduled runs read the pages edited since the last read, and every page on the first run after 01:00; its other runs, and every other source's, read all (an unchanged item costs only the listing). An item Mycroft never had (its budget spent, its model failing) is read again at the next run (KN-27). | Go test: a second sync sends nothing to the model; an edited page is read alone; the nightly read is due once a night (`TestNightlyFullReadKN28`). |
| KN-04 | **Sources are data, and scrubbed.** Every secret in a source is scrubbed (rule X-01, the console's scrubber and the knowledge's own credentials) before anything is written or sent to Mycroft; the prompt marks the sources as data; a source that tries to give instructions is reported with the words that did, in the run's card, the sync's report and Administration › AI › Knowledge. | Go test: a password in a source reaches neither the model nor an excerpt; *Ignore all previous instructions* is reported. |
| KN-05 | **Mycroft writes the pages.** Changed items go to Mycroft (its model, budget and instructions, through `CompleteAs`) in batches, with the wiki's page list and the pages they may change; it answers with pages (`pages/<kind>/<name>.md`, kinds system, application, runbook, incident, practice, decision; YAML front matter with the title, the critical mark, the sources with their versions and dates, and when it was updated) and the hosts the sources list. Every claim names its source with its version and date. A path that is not a page's is refused. | Go test with a fake model. |
| KN-06 | **The wiki's store**, one per installation: a git repository (HTTPS and a token, branch, private CA; each run's changes on `mycroft/<run>`, merged on approval; a person's commit first is rebased over, one to the same page waits for a person) or an S3 bucket (endpoint, region, prefix, path-style, an access key or the pod's identity, SSE-S3 or SSE-KMS with a key, private CA; drafts under `drafts/<run>/`, copied on approval with If-Match on the ETag read, If-None-Match for a new page; the page warns while bucket versioning is off). *Move the wiki here* copies every page, the manifest and the excerpts to another store and keeps the wiki there; it waits while runs wait. | Go tests against a bare repository and the fake S3: merge, rebase, conflict, conditional writes, versioning, the move. |
| KN-07 | **The local copy.** Every replica keeps the approved files on its own disk (`-knowledge-dir`, an emptyDir in the chart), reads them at start and again on a push to the webhook or a poll (git every 15 minutes, S3 every 5, or as set), and builds its index from them: since 0.14.0 an index of the approved pages alone (the Wiki tab, Mycroft's related pages), the search being the index in the database the leader keeps from its copy (KN-53). Only the leader reads sources and writes the wiki. | Go test of the refresh; chart check of the volume. |
| KN-08 | **Reviewed before published.** Each sync's page changes wait as one item in Cases › Inbox with every page's diff, its sources and approvals; one person approves an ordinary page, two different people a critical one (best practices and runbooks always, and what Mycroft marks); an agent never counts. A page changed in the store since Mycroft read it is not overwritten and waits for a person. Reject discards a run. Admins and SuperAdmins review; the count shows on the Inbox. A page's diff wraps its lines (a page's paragraphs never widen the screen) and folds the page's front matter (sources, dates, already listed above it) into one line that opens. | Go test: one approval publishes an ordinary page, the same person twice is refused, a second publishes the critical one, a page changed meanwhile is in conflict. Vitest of the card. |
| KN-09 | **Agents read only approved pages.** The index holds the approved pages and the scrubbed excerpts of the sources; drafts are never in it, nor a page retired (KN-24). | Go test; `TestRetirePage`. |
| KN-10 | **Freshness shows.** A page whose sources were not seen for 30 days (a setting) is marked stale, in Cases › Mycroft › Wiki, in ask_knowledge's answer and on its Confluence page. | Go test of the index; vitest of the page. |
| KN-11 | **ask_knowledge** answers from the index in the database since 0.14.0 (KN-53; before, the replica's own, keyword search with BM25), by hybrid search (KN-54), 6 hits: approved pages (never a retired one, KN-24), each named with its path and date and said stale when it is, and excerpts of the sources with their versions, dates, links, sections and their sources' trust, each marked unreviewed, ranked together (KN-57); then the console's records the question matches, by id: the open known issues, the active fixes (CF-52) and, for who may read them, the active exceptions (KN-32).
