## Phase 12: Getting started

Asked for on November 5, 2026: onboarding as a checklist that works with or without AI, which Sherlock helps with once a provider works; no separate Welcome agent (the AI agents design, *Getting started*). ADR-103 and ADR-104 have the decisions; the mockup is Start (Administration › Settings › Getting started, with Sherlock answering "What's next?"). The checklist is worked out in package `api` (`setup.go`), the skill is a part of package `ai` (`setup.go`, `prompts/setup.md`) and one tool of package `sherlock`; [getting-started.md](getting-started.md) explains each step.

| ID | Requirement | Done when |
| --- | --- | --- |
| GS-01 | **The checklist.** Nine steps, in this order: sign-in (local users, or OIDC or LDAP); clusters; an AI provider and default model; agents on, with budgets; Mycroft's sources; jobs and notifications in Cases; fleets; SSH and hosts outside Kubernetes; a first backup, then a schedule to S3. `GET /api/v1/setup` works every step out from the console's services each time it is read (Sherlock's reads share a view for up to 5 seconds); no step is ever ticked by hand, and a step that stops being true opens again. A SuperAdmin's only: anyone else is refused. | Go test `TestGettingStarted`: the states follow the console (a fleet made, a backup downloaded tick their steps); a viewer gets 403. |
| GS-02 | **Each step says** its state (done, open, dismissed), what the console has now and what is missing, why it matters and comes where it does, where it is done (a console link), the field or button to use there, and the page of [getting-started.md](getting-started.md) that explains it. No step's words hold a secret: a credential shows only as set or not. | Go test of every step's fields; vitest of the list. |
| GS-03 | **When a step is done.** Sign-in: OIDC or LDAP, or local users the console keeps (from the chart, or added and kept in a Secret). Clusters: a connected cluster. Provider: the default provider has its credential and a default model. Agents: Sherlock on for a cluster, and an overall limit set. Knowledge: the wiki has a store and a source besides the console is on (a Confluence space, a repository, an uploaded document). Jobs: an agent's job is on and a notification channel is on with its address. Fleets: a cluster has one. SSH and hosts: a node or a host is paired. Backup: one was downloaded, or a backup to S3 worked. | Go tests; e2e `getting-started.spec.ts` against the simulator (sign-in through OIDC and the cluster done; the provider's step opens without a key and ticks itself once the key is saved). |
| GS-04 | **Dismissing.** A SuperAdmin dismisses a step that does not apply, or every open one (*Dismiss the rest*), and brings one back (`POST /api/v1/setup/dismiss`); a dismissed step that is done shows done. Dismissals are kept in the settings ConfigMap under `setup.json` (4 KiB), with who and when, and each change is audited as `setup.dismiss`, naming the step. | Go test of the record and the audit; e2e. |
| GS-05 | **Administration › Settings › Getting started** is the first section of Settings, its hint saying how many steps are left: the steps with a number each, *N of 9 done*, the next step highlighted with *Open <screen>*, *Open* and *Dismiss* on the other open ones, *Done* and *Dismissed* (with *Bring back*) on the rest. | Vitest `GettingStarted.test.tsx`; e2e. |
| GS-06 | **The home page** shows a SuperAdmin a card, until every step is done or dismissed: progress, the next step with a button to its screen, and a link to the whole list. | Vitest; e2e. |
| GS-07 | **Sherlock's setup skill** is on for a SuperAdmin while any step is open: one more part of Sherlock's fixed instructions (`prompts/setup.md`, after the core and before the house instructions) and one more tool, `setup_status`, read only, which returns the checklist in words. Other people's Sherlock and every other agent never have it; once every step is done or dismissed it leaves the instructions and the tool goes. The panel shows a *setup* tag while it is on (`setup` in `GET /api/v1/sherlock`). | Go tests in packages `ai` and `api` with the fake Claude API: the tool and the part are sent for a SuperAdmin with a step open, not for a viewer, not once all are dismissed. |
| GS-08 | **What's next and taking you there.** Asked what is next, Sherlock reads `setup_status` and says what is left, in order, and why, leading with the next step; it gives the step's link and names the field or button there, and opens the Nodes or Fleets tab itself with `view_open` when it may change the view. It never claims a step done that the checklist does not. | Base test case `base-sherlock-setup-1`; e2e: the answer names the next step and its link opens the step's screen. |
| GS-09 | **Explaining choices**: which model an agent needs (the Auditor a strong model with a long context, the Summarizer the cheapest, the Script reviewer another provider than the Automation author's), what acting on its own costs (each run charged to the agent's and the job's budgets), which role an agent on its own reads with (Viewer unless more is needed, never one that changes anything), citing [getting-started.md](getting-started.md) and Mycroft's best-practice pages through `ask_knowledge`. | Base test case `base-sherlock-setup-3`. |
| GS-10 | **Never touching a secret.** For a password, key, token, certificate or webhook address, Sherlock opens the screen and the person types it into its field; it never asks for one in the chat, never repeats one pasted there, and says to type it on the screen instead. | Base test case `base-sherlock-setup-2` (a token pasted is refused and never repeated); `TestTheSetupSkillsBaseCasesPass` runs the three. |
| GS-11 | **Mycroft's first import.** A source added in Administration › AI › Knowledge is read at once, in the background, on the leader, when the wiki has a store (`knowledge.Options.ReadNew`); its documents' hosts, with the labels they give them, and the groups of hosts they name, as fleets selecting those labels, are proposed as one card per document in Cases › Inbox (`host.add` with labels, `fleet.create`); hosts and fleets the console has are left out. Approved hosts are paired in the Add host flow. No card holds a credential or a host key. | Go tests `TestTheFirstImportProposesTheInventory` and `TestNoFirstReadWithoutAStore`. |
| GS-12 | **Backup.** A part *Getting started* (order 250): the dismissals and the first backup taken; a restore replaces them and drops a step that is not one. | Go test `TestGettingStartedBackupPart`. |
| GS-13 | **Prompt sources.** [prompt-sources.md](prompt-sources.md) records each source the agents' instructions drew on (repository, the commit or version read, its license at that commit, what we take); `hack/check-prompt-sources.sh`, run in CI with the chart's checks and by `make lint-go`, and `TestEveryPromptSourceIsRecorded` fail when a `source:` comment in a prompt file names no row, or a row whose commit is still to be recorded, or a row has no license. A prompt file's comments never reach a model. No network. | The Go test, with a fixture of each failure. |
| GS-14 | **The simulator** serves every ConfigMap the chart makes, `<release>-cases` and `<release>-knowledge` included, and `make e2e-sim` names them, so the browser tests run against the console as the chart sets it up. [issues.md](issues.md) says what is built: the AI investigation (`server/internal/investigate`, JB-19) and the Confluence publisher (KN-13 to KN-18). | e2e run with the full set of ConfigMaps. |

## Phase 12: Inbox and Sherlock in Cases

Asked for on November 6, 2026, with the Cases work items and the findings' exceptions (CW-, FX-): the Inbox as a list of groups with what waits first, the agents' proposals called proposed changes, each item linked to the object it is about with how long that has been running, two people never both approving the same change, and Sherlock (and Watson, for a case's suggested command) in the Cases area, knowing its pages. ADR-111 and ADR-112 have the decisions.

| ID | Requirement | Done when |
| --- | --- | --- |
| IB-01 | **The groups on the left** (wave 4; replaced by one table, IN-01, IN-04). The Inbox has a pane on the left listing All and the groups the person may see, in this order: Approvals (approvers), Proposed changes and Cases (approvers and the console-wide roles), Findings (the console-wide roles), Reports and Known issue drafts (operators), Knowledge drafts (the console-wide roles). Each shows its count of what waits for the person unreviewed, marked when more than none; the server decides again on every list. A group is chosen with `?kind=`; pressed again it goes back to All. | vitest `shows the groups in a pane on the left…`, `offers the kinds by role`; e2e `inbox.spec.ts`. |
| IB-02 | **The right side** (wave 4; replaced by IN-01) lists the group chosen, with its own parts (the approvals list, the proposed changes, the cases, the findings, the reports table, the drafts). Under All it lists the groups with something waiting, in the pane's order, and names the rest as links ("Nothing waits in …"). A group with nothing says so. Mine and Unassigned stay on its bar. | vitest `shownKinds`, `shows a group that waits for nothing as such`. |
| IB-03 | **The order** in every group: what waits first (not decided, not done), then by priority (P1 first; what has none after P4), then the oldest waiting first (`lib/cases.ts inboxOrder`). Reports put those past a response target first within a priority. | vitest `orders every group alike`, `keeps open reports…`. |
| IB-04 | **Proposed changes** is the name of the agents' proposals (Watson cards) everywhere a person reads it; the API keeps `proposals` and `cards`. A change made for a case (source kind `case`, id `C-<n>`) says so ("for a case") and links the case (`#/cases/cases/<n>`) on its row and in its drawer. | vitest `links a change made for a case…`. |
| IB-05 | **The count** (wave 4; IN-07 since) on the switch and the Inbox tab adds the cases that wait for the person (the Cases part's `useCasesCount`). | vitest of the counting. |
| IB-06 | **An object's link and age.** A finding, case or proposed change about an object (`namespace/kind/name` or `kind/name`; Watson's targets `deployment shop/web`) shows `ObjectLink`: a Kubernetes object links to its page in the Kubernetes area on its cluster (a pod, Service or node opened on its tab, another namespaced object on its namespace's page, a claim, volume or class on Storage, cluster roles on Access), a host to its row on Automation › Nodes. Its age, "running 3d 4h" (a pod from its start, anything else from its creation; "age" for objects that do not run), is read live through the endpoints its own drawer reads, once for every link that names it and again after 30 seconds, and moves on each minute; an object no longer there shows *gone*; a kind 221B does not read shows as text. | vitest `ObjectLink.test.tsx`; e2e: a pod's link and age. |
| IB-07 | **How long an item has waited** (the Waiting column since wave 5, IN-01) shows on it: reports and known-issue drafts in the Inbox (*Waiting*), proposed changes on their rows and drawer. | vitest; e2e: a report's wait. |
| IB-08 | **Two people cannot approve the same proposed change.** An approval or rejection of a card that is no longer waiting is refused with HTTP 409 and the card as it is now: "Already approved by <who> at <time>" (or rejected, expired, replaced by a newer one). The decision is checked again in the same conditional write that records it, so of two approvals at once one counts and the change runs once. On production, where a change needs two people, the second person's approval still completes it. The drawer shows the error, in the console's time format, and the card as it is, and the list is read again. | Go `TestASecondApprovalIsRefusedWithWhoAndWhen`, `TestDecidedTextNamesEachEnding`, the API test; vitest `refuses a second approval…`; e2e (a 409 served to the page). |
| IB-09 | **The same for the Inbox's other approvals.** A console change that waits for a second SuperAdmin (twoperson) is decided one at a time from the first read to the record, since an approval applies the change before it records it: two approvals at once apply it once, and the second gets 409 "Already approved by <who> at <time>" (withdrawn, when the person who asked rejected it), shown on its row. A Watson command approved twice says who approved it and when. | Go `TestTwoApprovalsAtOnceApplyOnce`, the API test; vitest `says who decided it first…`. |
| IB-10 | **Sherlock's Cases skill.** Asked from a page of Cases (the view says `"view":"cases"`), Sherlock's instructions add `prompts/cases.md` after its core and before the house instructions: every Cases page (Inbox groups, Cases, Findings, Reports, Known issues, Jobs › Schedule and Runs, Knowledge with exceptions and RCAs), what each button does and who may do what. It changes nothing in Cases itself; a change it suggests goes to Watson as a card. Other agents never get it. | Go `TestTheAIOpsSkillIsSherlocksInAIOps`, `TestFromAIOpsReadsTheView`; e2e. |
| IB-11 | **The item open goes with the question.** The view Sherlock is sent from Cases names the page, its filters and the item open: what a drawer says it shows (`useCasesItem`: kind, id, cluster, a line about it) or else what the address names (`#/cases/cases/<n>`, `?proposal=`, `?report=`, `#/cases/jobs/runs/<id>`…). | vitest `aiopsItem.test.ts`; e2e. |
| IB-12 | **The dock in Cases.** Sherlock, and Watson beside it, are in the dock over every Cases page, as on a cluster's tabs, and pages there may ask Sherlock. The dock's cluster is the open item's when it names a connected cluster (the address moves to that cluster), else the last cluster used. | e2e. |
| IB-13 | **Open in Watson.** `openWatson(cluster, command)` (`lib/watsonFill.ts`) opens Watson's pane on that cluster with the command in its box and the box focused; nothing runs until the person presses Enter, and it runs as them through Watson's rules. In Sherlock's own window the command goes there. Watson off on that cluster: a notice says so. | vitest `Open in Watson`; e2e. |

## Phase 12: Inbox as one table

Asked for at the owner's review of the deployed console on November 6, 2026 (wave 5): the Inbox like the Nodes and Namespaces pages, one table with no groups and what is pending first; every case in it; the top bar's bell for alerts in every area and a yellow *i* on the gear for what waits for the person, in place of the Inbox's notices in Watson's panel; and Sherlock's conversation scrolling in its pane. It supersedes IB-01, IB-02, IB-05 and IB-07 and the Inbox parts of CS-03 to CS-06. ADR-116 and ADR-117 have the decisions.

| ID | Requirement | Done when |
| --- | --- | --- |
| IN-01 | **One table.** Cases › Inbox is one flat table (`DataTable`, as on the Nodes and Namespaces pages: the same header that sorts by its column, Columns, Export, search with suggestions, a row that opens with a click or Enter), not grouped and with no pane on the left. Its columns: Kind (Case, Finding, Proposed change, Approval, Known issue draft, Knowledge draft), Priority, Title (with the item's id), Cluster, Object (`ObjectLink`, with its age), Status, Waiting (or how long ago it was done) and Assignee where the kind has one. | vitest `Inbox.test.tsx` `lists every kind in one table…`; e2e `inbox.spec.ts`. |
| IN-02 | **Every item is a row.** Cases (every one that is not done, whatever its status: one being analysed is listed, which the wave-4 Inbox left out; until the merge with the Cases package, today's reports as case rows), the jobs' findings, the agents' proposed changes, approvals, the known issues an agent proposed and Mycroft's knowledge drafts (wiki runs and Confluence edits). Each kind is shown to the roles that may see it (cases to approvers, the console-wide roles, operators and reporters; findings and proposed changes to approvers and the console-wide roles; approvals to approvers; known-issue drafts to operators; knowledge drafts to the console-wide roles); the server decides again, and a source that answers 4xx or 503 lists nothing, quietly. | vitest `offers the kinds by role`, `lists nothing for a source the person may not see`, `inboxRows.test.ts`. |
| IN-03 | **The order**, until someone sorts by another column: what is pending first, then by priority (P1 first, none after P5), then the longest waiting first; then what was done, newest first (`inboxOrder`, `inboxOrderKey`; the Waiting column's sort). The table keeps the sort chosen while the page is open. | vitest `sorts pending first…`; e2e: a P2 case above a P3 report. |
| IN-04 | **The filters on top**, all in the address: Pending or All (`?show=all` adds what was closed, completed or decided), Kind with how many of each are pending (`?kind=case`, `finding`, `proposal`, `approval`, `known`, `knowledge`; the old group names, as in `?kind=approvals`, still work), Everyone's, Mine or Unassigned (`?who=`; Unassigned is what nobody has for kinds with an assignee, and what waits for the rest), Priority P1 to P5 (`?priority=`), Cluster (`?cluster=`, when the rows name more than one) and the search (`?q=`). | vitest `narrows to a kind, Mine, Unassigned and a priority…`, `counts what is pending of each kind`; e2e. |
| IN-05 | **Late.** A row past its response or resolution target shows *late*, saying which target passed, and links Administration › AI › SLA, where the targets per priority are set. | vitest `says a case is late…`. |
| IN-06 | **A drawer for each row** at `#/cases/inbox/<kind>/<id>`: a case (`CaseDrawer`; `case/new` opens New Case), a finding (with its priority, object, run, evidence, New Case and Ignore), a proposed change (`ProposalDrawer`), an approval (who asked and decided, Review where it is decided, a console change decided in place with the second decision told who decided first, IB-09; a decided script version read in place), a known-issue draft (`KnownDrawer`) and a knowledge draft (its pages' diffs, or the Confluence edit). `report/<n>` opens the case carried over from report *n*. The filters stay in the address while a drawer is open and after it closes; the Inbox's old links (`?proposal=`, `?report=`, `#/cases/cases/<n>`, `#/cases/reports/<n>`) open the same drawers. Sherlock is told the item open (IB-11). | vitest `opens a row in its drawer…`, `opens the links it had before…`, `an approval in its drawer`, `aiopsItem.test.ts`; e2e. |
| IN-07 | **The top bar.** A bell left of *Live*, in every area (Kubernetes, Automation, Cases, Administration), red with the number of alerts going on in the cluster open, opens that cluster's Alerts tab, which stays as it was. The gear opens Administration, always; a yellow *i* beside it says how many approvals and proposed changes wait for this person to decide, and opens them in the Inbox. The count on Cases and on the Inbox tab is everything pending in the Inbox for the person, read with the Inbox's own lists. Since CF-80: the bell stands for that count and opens the Inbox, with no number; the yellow *i* is gone. Since CF-88 the count is on the Inbox tab only. Since 0.13.0 the count is not shown while it is not known (the database away, DB-15): **?** on the tab and on the bell. | vitest of the counting; e2e `the bell stands for what waits in the Inbox…`, `automation.spec.ts` `the bell says what waits for you…`. |
| IN-08 | **No Inbox notices in Watson's panel.** The panel lists its own commands and cards only; the agents' proposed changes wait in the Inbox, and the yellow *i* says so (since CF-80: the bell). | e2e: no `watson-proposals` with a change waiting on the cluster. |
| IN-09 | **The conversation scrolls in its pane.** However long Sherlock's conversation or Watson's log grows, it scrolls inside its pane, which keeps to the dock's height; the page's height never changes with it, at any width, and scrolling past the end of the pane does not scroll the page. | e2e `the conversation scrolls in Sherlock’s pane…` at 1440 and 800 wide. |
| IN-10 | **Sherlock's Cases skill** (`prompts/cases.md`) describes the tabs Inbox, Jobs and Mycroft, the Inbox's table, filters and addresses, the case's actions (Known issue, Fix, Complete with RCA, Close with a reason, Save Draft, Reopen), Ignore's two outcomes, priorities P1 to P5, the top bar's bell and badge, and who may do what. | Go `TestTheAIOpsSkillIsSherlocksInAIOps`. |
| IN-11 | **What a person asked for waits in their own Inbox** (0.13.0). A script's version in review that the person wrote, edited or submitted, a variable change they asked for, and a Watson command of theirs that takes a second person are rows of their own Inbox while they wait, where they were left out before. `GET /api/v1/admin/approvals` gives such a row to whoever asked, and to nobody else, with `own: true` and `waitingFor`, the words for who decides it: "another SuperAdmin" (Automation is a SuperAdmin's alone, and a session card is a SuperAdmin's to approve, TB-07), or "a second person" for any other Watson command under the two-person rule. The row says *Waiting for another SuperAdmin* in place of *Pending*, is listed under Pending and under Mine, and opens the approval's drawer, with *Open* in place of *Review* and nothing to approve or reject: a version in review cannot be withdrawn; a variable change is rejected, with a reason, by whoever asked on the fleet's Variables; a Watson card is discarded in Watson. **It is counted** (the owner, November 20, 2026: the Inbox counts everything in it): the Inbox tab's count and the bell stand for every row pending in the person's Inbox (CS-04, CF-80), such a row among them, as their own run requests and console changes already were; in its first build the row was listed and left out of the count. Anyone else who may approve it has the row as before, unmarked; whoever is no SuperAdmin is refused as before (403). The row carries what the approver's row carries (the names of what changes, never a value). A run with its own values and a console change, which whoever asked may withdraw on their row, stay as they were, counted (FU-05, AI-35). Approved, rejected or taken back, the row leaves what waits, and the count. | Go `TestASubmittersOwnRowWaitsForAnotherSuperAdminIN11`, `TestAWatsonCommandOfOnesOwnWaitsForASecondPersonIN11`; vitest *says who a row of the person’s own waits for, in place of Pending (IN-11)* in `inboxRows.test.ts`, *lists what the person submitted as waiting for another SuperAdmin, with nothing to decide (IN-11)* in `Inbox.test.tsx`, *counts what the person asked for and someone else decides, which makes the bell yellow (IN-11)* in `TopBarOrder.test.tsx`; e2e `automation.spec.ts` *Approvals lists what waits on top…* (the row, and the bell that counts it). |
| IN-12 | **A case the alert safety net opens is pending in the Inbox** (the owner, November 23, 2026: C-14 was left out of *Pending* and its count while open). Two P0 cases the console opened from alerts at the same moment, one with the alert's message as its detail, each analysed with no fix found, are both in the cases' list and in its pending list, and both are rows of the Inbox under Pending and in its count, under Everyone's and Unassigned; under Mine neither is, being nobody's and opened by the console. The cause of C-14's absence was not reproduced from the facts given (see the handover of November 23); these tests guard what the code does. | Go `TestTheSafetyNetsCasesAreListedC14` (casework, both stores); vitest `inboxRows.test.ts` *lists two P0 cases the console opened at the same moment, one with a detail, under Pending and in its count*. |
## Phase 12: Jobs and Helm repositories, round 2

Asked for at the owner's review of the deployed console on November 6, 2026 (wave 5): a run's status says what it found; a note said many times is said once; Jobs › Runs shows when each run started and ended and its health, as wide as Jobs › Schedule; a job's definition is shown plainly and its name and instructions may be changed; findings take the priorities P1 to P5; *Ignore* accepts a finding as an exception or makes a known issue of it; and applications install charts from Helm repositories and OCI registries, not only git. ADR-123 to ADR-125 have the decisions.

| ID | Requirement | Done when |
| --- | --- | --- |
| JR-01 | **A run's health.** Each run has a health: the worst severity among its findings (*critical*, *warning*, *info*), *none* when it found nothing, *failed* for one that failed or was interrupted; none while it runs or for a time let go (skipped, missed). The API gives it with the counts by severity (`health`, `severities`). A run done reads *Done · 2 critical* in red, *Done · 1 warning* in amber, and *Done* in green with info or nothing. | Go `TestRunHealth`, `TestRound2API`; vitest `shows when each run started and ended, its health…`; e2e `findings.spec.ts`. |
| JR-02 | **A note said once.** The same note said many times on a run is said once with how many: "4 proposed commands Watson would refuse were left out: …", "… (3 times)" for others. Runs kept before are shown the same way. | Go `TestNotesMerged`; vitest `says a note said many times once`. |
| JR-03 | **Jobs › Runs** has the columns Started, Ended (or *running*), Job, Started by, Status, Health (colored by the health), Result and Cost. | vitest; e2e. |
| JR-04 | **Schedule and Runs are as wide as each other**: the Jobs column takes the Cases page's width, whatever each table holds. | e2e (the two cards' widths). |
| JR-05 | **A job's definition, plainly.** A job's drawer says what it makes, its agent, the instructions it sends its agent, what the agent reads and what a run may make (each kind's `Task`, `Reads`, `Proposes`), and that each run puts what it is about (the cluster, the alert or report, the accepted exceptions) around the instructions and asks for its answer's format after them. A kind without instructions of its own (the knowledge syncs, the backups) says so. | Go `TestJobInstructionsAreEditable`; vitest `shows what a job does plainly…`. |
| JR-06 | **Its own name and instructions.** Any job, a kind's own too, takes a name and instructions of its own (at most 8,000 characters), shown pre-filled with the kind's built-in ones and marked *Built-in* or *Its own*, with *Reset to built-in*; the built-in text, or none, keeps the built-in. Runs send the job's instructions in place of the kind's: the nightly audit, alert diagnosis, daily digest, report triage (not its check of a fix) and case analysis (not its RCA drafts). The audit log says *instructions built-in → its own (n characters, digest)*, never the text. Schedule, clusters and budget are changed as before. | Go; vitest. |
| JR-07 | **Add another job of a kind.** *Add job* is *Add another <kind> job* in a job's drawer, with when one would want one: another schedule, other clusters, other instructions; it starts paused as a copy of the built-in. It is not offered, and the API refuses it, for kinds a report or a case starts (report triage, case analysis), which run their first job only. | Go; vitest `offers “Add another Nightly audit job”…`. |
| JR-08 | **Priorities P1 to P5** for findings, as for cases: P1 an outage, P2 blocking, P3 a service degraded, P4 preemptive, P5 a question. A new finding starts at its severity's: critical P2, warning P4, info P5; a person changes it to any of the five (`PUT /api/v1/findings/{id}/priority`). Findings kept with a priority keep its number; those kept without one take the new mapping. | Go `TestFindingKnownIssue`, `TestFindingsAreHandled`; vitest; e2e. |
| JR-09 | **Ignore: accept as exception.** *Ignore* opens a dialog with two outcomes; *Accept as exception* (the default) is FX-03's: a reason, an optional last day; Mycroft › Exceptions lists it, and the finding reads *Ignored as an exception* with its link. | vitest; e2e. |
| JR-10 | **Ignore: create known issue.** The second outcome makes a known issue in the issue service, pre-filled from the finding (its title, its detail as a symptom; a workaround optional), made by the person (`known` audited by the issue service, `job.finding` *→ known issue KI-n* by the jobs) with a first update naming the finding and its object; the finding becomes *known* (*Ignored as known issue*, linked to `#/cases/mycroft/known/<id>`) until reopened, and the known issue stays. Later runs still report the check: a known issue is not an exception. Offered only where the console keeps known issues (`mayKnown`); a finding already a case, ignored or known is refused; so is a case of a known one. `POST /api/v1/findings/{id}/known` `{title, symptoms?, workaround?, services?}`, for those who decide findings. | Go `TestFindingKnownIssue`, `TestRound2API`; vitest `ignores a finding as a known issue…`; e2e. |
| JR-11 | **Old states stay readable**: *open*, *case*, *ignored*, *known*, and *acknowledged*/*dismissed* of before cases. | vitest. |
| HR-01 | **Three kinds of source.** An application's chart comes from a git repository (as before), a **Helm repository** (an HTTPS repository with its `index.yaml`) or an **OCI registry** (`oci://registry/path/chart`), at a version. A source keeps its kind, the repository's name (Helm; an OCI registry's entry when one covers the address), the address, the chart's name and the version; the record keeps the archive's digest where git keeps the commit. A repository's chart takes the repository's credentials, never a token of its own. | Go `TestChartRefs`, the API test. |
| HR-02 | **Settings › Applications** lists the repositories the console knows: a name (lowercase, as helm lines use it), the kind, the address, the sign-in (none, a user and password, or a token, with an optional user name for registries that want one) and the last test. A SuperAdmin adds, changes and removes them (`helmrepo.save`, `helmrepo.remove`, audited without the password); those who manage applications read the list. The list is the key `helm-repositories.json` of the settings ConfigMap (16 KiB, at most 50); each password or token a key of the Secret `<release>-helm-repositories` the console writes, never shown again: the list says whether one is kept. A password belongs to its address: one moved elsewhere takes a new one or none. | Go `TestRepositoriesAreKept`, `TestHelmRepositoriesAndAnApplicationFromOne`; e2e `helm-repos.spec.ts`. |
| HR-03 | **Test**, before and after saving: a Helm repository is asked for its index (how many charts), an OCI registry at `/v2/`, with the credentials typed or kept; the answer says what failed (no answer, credentials needed or refused, not found). A saved repository keeps its last test (`helmrepo.test`). | Go; e2e. |
| HR-04 | **Fetching.** A Helm repository's chart is fetched from the address its index gives (relative to the repository, or absolute), with the repository's credentials only on the repository's own host; checked against the index's digest. An OCI registry's chart: its manifest, then the chart's layer (`application/vnd.cncf.helm.chart.content.v1.tar+gzip`), checked against the digest the manifest gives, signed in as the registry asks: a token given, or the credentials at the token service its challenge names (Bearer), or on the request (Basic); a version's `+` is the tag's `_`. Over HTTPS only, never to loopback, link-local or metadata addresses (HA-04), within the fetch limits (16 MB an archive, 32 MB an index, the files' count and size), two at once; the archive is unpacked in memory and read as a git checkout is. | Go `TestHelmRepositoryChart`, `TestOCIChart`, `TestChartArchiveLimits`. |
| HR-05 | **Charts and versions.** A Helm repository's charts (with their newest version, description) and a chart's versions, newest first, with the newest stable one, for the forms (`GET /api/v1/helm-repositories/{name}/charts`, `POST /api/v1/helm-repositories/versions`); a repository's index is read again after two minutes, or on a test or change. An OCI registry lists a chart's tags; it does not list its charts. | Go; e2e. |
| HR-06 | **The Helm tab.** *New application* asks where the chart comes from: Git repository, Helm repository (chosen from the list, the chart from its index or typed, the version from its versions or typed) or OCI registry (the chart's `oci://` address and its version). A release from a repository shows its chart, its version (saved for the next deploy) and the archive deployed by its digest; *Switch* and *Redeploy* offer its versions, and a newer version is announced as a newer tag is. | e2e `helm-repos.spec.ts`; vitest. |
| HR-07 | **Watson** installs and upgrades them: `helm install NAME REPO/CHART [--version V] -n NS [--app]` with a repository the console knows, and `helm install NAME oci://REGISTRY/PATH/CHART [--version V] …`; `helm upgrade` alike. A line names the chart once (`--git` with `--ref`, or a chart with `--version`); `--username`/`--password` stay refused. Without `--version` the newest stable version is deployed. A deploy brings the record up to date with the source, the version and the archive's digest. | Go `TestHelmInstallFromARepositoryAndARegistryAgainstTheSimulator`. |
| HR-08 | **A card deploys what it showed**: the archive's digest is its pin, as a commit is a git card's (P6-04); a line without `--version` deploys the version its card settled on; an archive changed in its repository since the card is refused with a fresh card to ask for. | Go `TestARepositoryDeployRunsTheArchiveItsCardShowed`. |
| HR-09 | **The API**: `GET /api/v1/helm-repositories`, `PUT`/`DELETE /api/v1/helm-repositories/{name}`, `POST /api/v1/helm-repositories/test` (as typed) and `/{name}/test` (as kept), `GET /api/v1/helm-repositories/{name}/charts`, `POST /api/v1/helm-repositories/versions`; the applications' `POST apps`, `PUT apps/{ns}/{name}`, `POST apps/refs` and `POST apps/inspect` take `kind`, `repository` and `chart`. A SuperAdmin changes, tests and removes; Admins and SuperAdmins read. | Go API test with local sign-in. |
| HR-10 | **Backups** hold the repositories (part *Helm repositories*, order 290), each password or token the placeholder; a restore adds the missing ones by name, one that signed in without its credential, and says to give it again. | Go `TestHelmRepositoriesBackup`. |
| HR-11 | **No third party in tests**: fakes of a Helm repository and an OCI registry with a token service, over TLS on the loopback (`internal/apps/repotest`). | The Go tests above. |
| HR-12 | **Egress**: no network policy rule changes; a new value, `helmRepositories.egressCIDRs` (empty, with `egressPorts: [443]`), adds a rule to the repositories' addresses only when set, for consoles whose outbound HTTPS is closed (`sherlock.egress` off). | `hack/check-chart.sh` *the network policy lets the console reach the Helm repositories only at helmRepositories.egressCIDRs*. |

## Phase 12: AI settings, round 2

Asked for on November 6, 2026, in the owner's review of the deployed console (items 3 to 9 and 16): the agents' instructions shown in full and editable, Draft, Test, Pilot and Live as steps that act, a provider dialog with a Test, a provider without a credential (the owner's in-house "A5000" serving qwen3.8-27b) usable once it passed a test, the model picked from what the endpoint lists, Sherlock fitting a model whose context is small, the Policies checkbox fitting the drawer, and the agents in the README. ADR-120 to ADR-122 have the decisions.

| ID | Requirement | Done when |
| --- | --- | --- |
| AS-01 | **The built-in text, pre-filled and editable.** An agent's drawer shows its built-in instructions in full, as the release ships them (its own part and the rules every agent keeps; Sherlock's with the view part while view changes are on), in a field a SuperAdmin may edit. Sherlock has a second part, its compact form (AS-07), on a tab beside its whole form. A saved edit replaces that part whole for that agent; the agents it may hand work to, the house instructions, the notes and the rule that text from the cluster is data are still added (the rule even when the edit leaves it out). *Reset to built-in* puts the shipped text back in the field; *Compare with built-in* shows the line diff. An edit equal to the built-in text is no edit. The edit goes Draft → Test → Pilot → Live with the house instructions and notes, in the same draft and versions; a restored version brings its edits back. When a release changes the built-in text under an edit (or, for Sherlock, view changes are turned on or off), the drawer says so and nothing replaces the edit. At most 6,000 words a part. | Go `TestTheBuiltInTextIsEditable`, `TestAIProviderDialogAndAnInHouseModel`; vitest `shows the built-in text pre-filled…`; e2e `an agent’s built-in text is pre-filled…`. |
| AS-02 | **Draft, Test, Pilot and Live are steps that act.** Each says in one line what it is and offers its action: *Back to draft*, *Run tests* (*Run tests again*), *Start pilot* (*Stop pilot* while one runs), *Make live*. An action that cannot be done now is disabled with the reason beside it: save your edits first, save a draft first, run the tests first (a pilot serves a tested draft), the tests are running, nothing waits to go live (with the version in force), waiting for another SuperAdmin's approval (with the request). The step the draft is at is marked. | vitest `offers each stage’s action, or says why…`; e2e. |
| AS-03 | **Back to draft** ends the draft's pilot, drops its test and gives it a new revision, so an approval asked for it no longer applies; it is tested again before it goes on. Audited. | Go `TestBackToDraft`; vitest `offers each stage’s action…`. |
| AS-04 | **The provider dialog has a Test**, before and after saving: it sends the settings in the dialog one short request (`POST /api/v1/ai/providers/try`) and says how it answered, and whether without a key. The saved credential goes with it only to the saved provider's own endpoint (same kind, endpoint, authentication and header). A test of a saved provider (its card's *Test*, or the dialog's *Save* after a test that passed, which tests the saved settings again) is kept with the provider. **A provider whose last test passed without a credential is usable without one**, with its connection settings as tested: its key reads *No key needed* (green), its agents are no longer refused with "has no credential", and Getting started counts it. Another endpoint, kind or authentication needs a new test. The test is the console's own record, never taken from a request. | Go `TestAProviderWithoutACredentialIsUsableOnceTested`, `TestAIProviderDialogAndAnInHouseModel`; vitest `tests the settings before saving…`; e2e `an in-house endpoint without a key…`. |
| AS-05 | **The model is picked from the endpoint's list.** The default model (provider dialog) and an agent's own model (its drawer) are a dropdown of the models the provider's endpoint lists, each with its context window when the endpoint gives it, and *Another model…* to type one: an OpenAI-compatible endpoint's `/models` (vLLM's `max_model_len`; `context_length`, `context_window` or `max_input_tokens` where others give them), Ollama's `/api/tags` where `/models` is missing, the Claude API's `/v1/models` with its key, Amazon Bedrock's foundation models (signed, not through a gateway). Vertex AI and a Bedrock gateway list none: the model is typed, with the usual suggestions. A saved provider's list loads as its dialog opens; *List the endpoint's models* lists again. Why nothing was listed is said. | Go `TestModelsAreListedFromTheEndpoint`; vitest; e2e. |
| AS-06 | **A context window** (tokens) for a provider's default model and for an agent's own model, filled from the endpoint's list when it says, else set by hand; 0 is not known. The listed Claude models have 200,000. Between 1,024 and 10,000,000. | Go `TestSherlockIsCompactOnASmallModel`. |
| AS-07 | **Sherlock's compact form.** When Sherlock's instructions and tool definitions in its whole form take more than half of its model's context window (or when a SuperAdmin chooses *Always*; *Never* keeps the whole form), it answers in its compact form: a short core (`prompts/sherlockCompact.md`, or its edit) with the house instructions and notes, the essential reads only (cluster overview, alerts, nodes, a node, pods, a pod, logs, events, a namespace's objects, an object, YAML, and `ask_knowledge`, of the tools it is given), no hand-over, no skills (setup, Cases) and no view actions. On a model whose context is known and under 64,000 tokens a reply is bounded at a quarter of it (at least 1,024), so the question fits. The agent's drawer says how many tokens its instructions and tools take of the model's context, in the form in force and whole, and whether the compact form is used. Measured with Qwen 3's tokenizer (November 2026): the whole form is 3,299 tokens of instructions and 6,752 of 38 tool definitions, 10,051 together (the cluster's block adds about 1,000, the Cases skill about 940); the compact form is 716 and 1,743 of 12 tool definitions, 2,459. The console's own estimate, which decides, is a little over: 10,259 whole, 2,468 compact; after the instructions were tightened (November 9, 2026), 10,097 whole (3,251 of instructions), 2,512 compact. | Go `TestSherlockIsCompactOnASmallModel`, `TestSherlocksRequestSize`, `TestAIProviderDialogAndAnInHouseModel` (what is sent); vitest; e2e. |
| AS-08 | **The agent drawer's checkboxes wrap and fit** at every width: the tools (*Policies (budgets, quotas, Pod Security, webhooks)*), the clusters, view control, the single-provider confirmation and the default model. | e2e: the Policies checkbox fits its column at 900 pixels. |
| AS-09 | **The README has a table of the agents**: each one's name, what it does, when it runs and what it may use. | README. |
## Phase 12: Mycroft tab

Asked for on November 6, 2026, in the owner's review of the deployed console: "Knowledge tab - Loading... - we need to be able to submit plain text, a url for crawling information, documents in different formats", and "Ignore can also create a known issue. It should not go to Knowledge. Maybe combine Known Issues and Knowledge under Mycroft?". The Knowledge and Known issues tabs become one tab, Mycroft, with the accepted exceptions and the RCAs beside them; the wiki gains Add; the "Loading…" that never ended is fixed. ADR-118 and ADR-119 have the decisions.

| ID | Requirement | Done when |
| --- | --- | --- |
| MY-01 | **Cases › Mycroft** has four sub-tabs: *Wiki* (`#/cases/mycroft/wiki`, the wiki's pages, drafts and sources; Add until MY-11, now in Administration › AI › Knowledge), *Known issues* (`#/cases/mycroft/known[/<id>]`, the old Known issues tab), *Exceptions* (`#/cases/mycroft/exceptions[/<id>]`, the accepted exceptions) and *RCAs* (`#/cases/mycroft/rcas[/<id>]`), the last two with their counts. The old addresses open the new places: `#/cases/knowledge[/…]` the Wiki, `#/cases/knowledge/exceptions/<id>` and `/rcas/<id>` the sub-tabs, `#/cases/known[/<id>]` Known issues. A role without exceptions and RCAs is told so in those sub-tabs. | vitest `MycroftTab` (in `LessonsViews.test.tsx`), router; e2e `knowledge.spec.ts`. |
| MY-02 | **The Wiki never loads for good.** The cause: with no approved page, `GET /api/v1/knowledge/pages` answered `"pages": null`, which the page took for "no answer yet", so it showed *Loading…* for good on every console with no wiki store (as the deployed one) or an empty wiki. The list is an empty array now, the page treats any answer, `null` included, or any failure as the end of the loading, and a failure is shown. With no store the Wiki says so ("No wiki is kept yet"), what a store holds, and offers *Set up the wiki* (Administration › AI › Knowledge) to a SuperAdmin; others are told a SuperAdmin sets it up. | Go `TestNoPagesIsAnEmptyList`; vitest `ends the loading on any answer…`, `says a failure…`; e2e. |
| MY-03 | **The Wiki's state** (`GET /api/v1/knowledge/wiki`, anyone who reads the wiki): where the wiki is kept (`git`, `s3` or none, never its address or keys), whether Mycroft has a model, the pages' and excerpts' counts, whether a sync runs, what the person may do (`mayAdd`, `mayReview`, `maySetup`), the sources (name, kind, items, last read, error, how many tried to give instructions; no address, account or credential), what was added in Mycroft, and the limits of Add. *Sources* lists them, with a link to Administration › AI › Knowledge for a SuperAdmin. | Go `TestNoPagesIsAnEmptyList`, `TestAddANote`; vitest `lists the sources…`. |
| MY-04 | **Add** (`POST /api/v1/knowledge/add`; since MY-11 in Administration › AI › Knowledge's *Added in Mycroft* card, `#/admin/ai/knowledge/added` (CU-18), no longer `#/cases/mycroft/wiki/add`): an Admin or a SuperAdmin adds a note, a web address or documents. Each becomes an item of the source *Added in Mycroft* (kind uploaded documents, there from the start): scrubbed of secrets (X-01, the knowledge's own credentials too), read by a sync of that source started at once, kept as excerpts the agents quote through `ask_knowledge` once read, and given to Mycroft, whose pages wait for review under *Drafts* like any other change of the wiki (KN-08). The raw text is held in memory only until it is read. It needs a wiki store (409 otherwise, saying where to set one) and the source switched on; audited `knowledge.add` with what was added and its size, never its text. | Go `TestAddANote`, `TestAddAudit`; vitest of the form; e2e. |
| MY-05 | **A note and documents.** A note is a title (up to 120 characters) and plain text or Markdown (up to 200 KiB); one of the same title replaces the one before. Documents are PDF, Word (`.docx`), Markdown, HTML, plain text and CSV (its rows, cells apart by ` \| `), each up to 20 MiB, several at once, each its own request; one of the same name replaces the one before. A kind the console does not read, or a file too large, is refused on the page before it is sent, and by the server. | Go `TestAddDocuments`; vitest `adds documents one by one…`. |
| MY-06 | **A web address** (http or https, no user or password in it) is read with the pages of the same site (scheme, host and port) it links to, breadth first, one request at a time: as far as asked (only the page, or 1 to 3 links away; 1 by default) and at most as many pages as asked (1 to 50; 20 by default), each page up to 2 MiB, 16 MiB in all, within 90 seconds. `robots.txt` is read first and obeyed (the group for `221B-Mycroft`, else `*`; Allow and Disallow, the longest match; `*` and `$`); a missing one allows everything, one that cannot be read (5xx, no answer) stops the crawl. A page's robots meta tag (`noindex`, `nofollow`) and a link's `rel="nofollow"` are obeyed; redirects stay on the site. HTML (its body, without scripts, styles and navigation), plain text, Markdown, PDF and Word are read; anything else is listed as left out, with why. The console never reads a loopback, link-local (the cloud's metadata) or multicast address, checked when it connects, and never through a proxy; the site must be reachable from the console's pod (with `networkPolicy.enabled`, through `knowledge.egressCIDRs` on `knowledge.egressPorts`). | Go `TestAddAWebAddress`, `TestCrawlRefusals`, `TestRobotsRules` (httptest, no network); vitest; e2e: a loopback address is refused. |
| MY-07 | **What was added** is listed under *Sources*, each with when it was read (or that it waits), the pages that draw on it and whether it tried to give instructions; an Admin or a SuperAdmin removes one (`DELETE /api/v1/knowledge/added/{id}`), which leaves the index; the pages drawn from it stay, and grow stale. The page reads the state again every few seconds while something added waits to be read. | Go `TestAddANote`; vitest; e2e. |
| MY-08 | **What a person adds goes through review.** A person's addition is a source, not a page: Mycroft keeps one page per subject and names its sources, and the wiki's review stays the one way into the approved pages (ADR-118). Without a model the excerpts are kept and the page says no pages are written. | Go `TestAddANote`. |
| MY-09 | **Exceptions are not in the wiki.** An accepted exception is the console's record only, listed under Mycroft › Exceptions and given to the agents in their task (FX-04); no wiki page is written for it any more. One written to the wiki before keeps its page, which says so once the exception is removed. RCAs are still written to the wiki when one is kept (FX-09). | Go `TestExceptions`, `TestOldExceptionPage`. |
| MY-10 | **The simulator keeps a wiki bucket**: `221b-sim -fake-s3-bucket sim-wiki` (the default) serves a fake S3 bucket path-style on its own address (region `us-east-1`, access key `sim-access-key`, secret `sim-secret-key`), so the browser tests add to a wiki with a store. | e2e `knowledge.spec.ts`. |
| MY-11 | **Add only in Settings.** (Since 0.12.1 notes and documents are added under Uploaded documents, MY-12, and a web address is a Web site source, KN-44.) A note (title and text), a web address (how far, how many pages, robots.txt obeyed) and documents (MY-04 to MY-06) are added in Administration › AI › Knowledge's *Added in Mycroft* card, which also lists what was added, each removable (MY-07); Cases › Mycroft › Wiki has no *Add* view. `#/cases/mycroft/wiki/add` (and `#/cases/knowledge/add`) opens `#/admin/ai/knowledge/added`, the card scrolled into view. Who adds stays an Admin or a SuperAdmin: an Admin, for whom the rest of Settings is closed, sees the card alone at that address with a line saying the rest is for SuperAdmins; anyone else is refused as for the rest of Settings. The Wiki's *Sources* › *Added in Mycroft* *Add…* button, its empty list and the empty wiki point to the card. | Vitest `has no Add view…` (KnowledgeTab), `has the Added in Mycroft card…` (KnowledgeSection), `AddedInMycroft.test.tsx` (the form, the list, the fallback, the old address), `shows an Admin Administration › AI › Knowledge’s Added in Mycroft card alone…` (Admin), router. e2e `knowledge.spec.ts` *Administration › AI › Knowledge › Added in Mycroft with a store…*, *Cases › Mycroft has Wiki…* (the old address). |


## Phase 12: Cases replace reports

Asked for on November 6, 2026, in the owner's review of the deployed console (wave 5, items 1, 10, 20, 21 and 24): *Why is the Reports tab still up? It was supposed to be replaced with Cases*; a case found under Cases › Cases and not in the Inbox; *New Case* refusing anything that is not `namespace/kind/name`; cases not about infrastructure at all (*disk space is 90%*); priorities P1 outage to P5 question; and where the SLA that makes a case *late* is set. One object, called a **Case** everywhere in the console, replaces the report and the wave-4 case: CU-01 to CU-14 supersede CS-05, CS-07 to CS-12 and CW-02, CW-07 and CW-11 where they differ, and the IS- requirements hold for a reported case. ADR-113 to ADR-115 have the decisions. The server side is the issue service (`server/internal/issues`, the case itself) and package `casework` (221B's work on it); the screens are `web/src/components/cases/CaseDrawer.tsx`, `NewCase.tsx` and `web/src/components/SLASettings.tsx`. The Inbox's table is the IN section's.

| ID | Requirement | Done when |
| --- | --- | --- |
| CU-01 | **A case is the one record of a problem**, whatever opened it: a person's report (the report page, the account menu, Sherlock's *Report this*, a system through the issue API's intake) has source *Report*; a finding's *New Case* source *Finding*; an alert's *New Case* source *Alert*; *New Case* by hand source *Manual*. One numbering, shown as `C-<n>`, kept by the issue service; a report's number is its case's. Every case has a title (a report's first line until someone gives it one), a priority, a status of the issue service (received, investigating, fixProposed, fixed, closed with an outcome), what it is about, an assignee, its conversation, its known issue and its SLA; 221B keeps beside it (package `casework`) what opened it in the console, its analyses, proposed changes, fix applied, RCA, history and each person's draft. The reporter's view (IS-22) never names its cluster or object. | Go tests `TestACaseOpenedInTheConsole`, `TestAReportIsACase`. |
| CU-02 | **New Case** (`#/cases/inbox/case/new`, the case drawer's address; prefilled by `?title=&cluster=&resource=&priority=`) takes a title, what you know, the cluster (or none), what it is about and a priority, and opens the case's drawer. An alert's **New Case** (on the Alerts tab, for those who work the cases) opens a case about the alert's object with its words. A case opened in the console counts as acknowledged and responded to when it is opened; the issue service neither matches it to known issues nor investigates it, the case analysis job does. | vitest `CaseDrawer.test.tsx`; Go tests; e2e `casework.spec.ts`. |
| CU-03 | **What a case is about is any words, or nothing** (the owner's items 1 and 10): a Kubernetes object (`namespace/kind/name`, `kind/name`), a host's disk, a path, a service. When the words name a Kubernetes object and the case names a cluster, the case keeps its parsed form (`object`) and links it with its age (`ObjectLink`); otherwise they are shown as written. A case without a cluster is not analysed and says so. | Go test `TestACaseOpenedByHandWhileTheJobIsPaused`; e2e: a case about a disk. |
| CU-04 | **Changing a case**: its title, priority, cluster, what it is about and its assignee (Nobody, Me, someone by user name), and the detail of a case opened in the console (a report's words stay the reporter's), each in its history and the audit log. | Go test `TestFixCompleteCloseAndReopen`. |
| CU-05 | **Priorities P1 outage, P2 blocking, P3 service degradation, P4 preemptive, P5 question**, everywhere: cases (reports kept with 1 to 4 keep their numbers), findings, the report page and the issue API, which takes 1 to 5. A case from a finding takes the finding's priority, else its severity's: critical P2, warning P4, info P5; from an alert, a critical one P1, a warning P3. People may change any priority. | Go tests `TestACaseFromAFinding`, `TestACaseFromAnAlert`. |
| CU-06 | **Administration › AI › SLA** (first under AIOps since 0.12.1, CU-15): the first-response and resolution targets per priority, P1 to P5 (P5 by default a first response within two days), for every case; moved out of Administration › AI › Issues, which links there. Read at `GET /api/v1/cases/sla` by those who see the cases, set by a SuperAdmin (`PUT`). A case past a target is **late**: the Inbox and the drawer say which target (first response, resolution) passed and its value, linking to Administration › AI › SLA. | Go tests `TestTheSLA`, `TestCaseworkAccess`; vitest. |
| CU-07 | **Close with a reason**: why (Duplicate, naming the case it repeats; Not a problem, a new outcome; Not infrastructure; Won't fix; Need info; Planned; Fixed) and an explanation, always required; a reported case's reporter reads it. Since CF-90, *Not a problem* is *No action needed*. | Go test; vitest; e2e. |
| CU-08 | **Pending** is a case open, not marked fixed (it then waits for its reporter, or the timer) and not waiting for the reporter's answer. The cases are listed pending first, P1 first, then the longest waiting (`waitingSince`); the others newest change first. `GET /api/v1/cases/cases` filters by `pending`, `open`, `status`, `source`, `cluster`, `priority`, `mine` (assigned to the person), `unassigned` and words, and lists every case the person may see, so every case reaches the Inbox (the wave-4 bug). | Go tests; vitest `InboxCases`. |
| CU-09 | **The case's actions**, in one drawer for every case: *Known issue…* links an open one or makes one from the case (title, symptom, workaround) and links it, or unlinks; *Fix…* records the fix applied (the case is fixed, and a reported one asks its reporter whether it works), beside the agent's suggested fix with *Open in Watson* and the proposed changes; *Complete with RCA…* (as CW-08; the case closes as fixed with its RCA in Knowledge); *Close…* (CU-07); *Reopen* on a fixed or closed case; a reply, *Ask for info* and an internal note on a reported case, notes only on one with no reporter; the investigator's drafted replies with Send, Edit and Discard; *Analyse* / *Analyse again*. | Go tests `TestAKnownIssueFromACase`, `TestFixCompleteCloseAndReopen`; vitest; e2e. |
| CU-10 | **Save Draft** keeps what the person is writing on the case (a reply or note, a fix, a close reason, an RCA) as their own draft until they send it, for them alone; it comes back when they open the case again, and sending a part takes that part away. A draft is scrubbed of credentials and audited without its text (`case.keep`). *Superseded by CF-59: a case has one draft, shared by everyone who works it, kept by Save.* | Go test `TestSaveDraft`; vitest; e2e. |
| CU-11 | **Who**: those who work the cases (who may approve proposed changes, `cases.approverRoles`, or triage reports, `issues.operatorRoles`) open and change every case; the console-wide roles and those who may edit jobs read them. The issue API keeps its paths and shapes for systems and the report page: a "report" there is a case, with the new optional fields `title`, `source`, `cluster`, `object`, `openedBy` and `legacy`, the priority 5 and the outcome `notAProblem` (docs/issues-api.yaml). With `issues.enabled: false` the report page and the issue API are off, and the cases keep their records in `<release>-issues` all the same. | Go test `TestCaseworkAccess`; `hack/check-chart.sh`. |
| CU-12 | **Carried over once.** On start, the leader carries the wave-4 cases (kept apart in `<release>-casework`) into the one numbering: the reports keep their numbers, each wave-4 case takes the next free one, with its status (new → received, processing → investigating, fixProposed, complete → closed as fixed), what opened it, its analyses, proposed changes, RCA and history, and the findings, RCAs and Watson cards that named it by its old id are renamed. It is idempotent (a case is found again by its old id), audited (`case.migrate`), and until it is done the cases take no writes (503, try again). Old addresses keep working: `#/cases/cases/<n>` opens the case a wave-4 case became, `#/cases/reports/<n>` the case of that report, `#/cases/known[/<id>]` and `#/cases/knowledge[/…]` Mycroft's. | Go tests `TestTheCasesOfBeforeAreCarriedOver`, `TestCarryAndWithdraw`; vitest; e2e. |
| CU-13 | **Audit**: the issue service's `issue.*` for what it keeps (a case opened, its status, triage, messages, known issues), and `case.create`, `case.update`, `case.analyze`, `case.draft`, `case.fix`, `case.known`, `case.complete`, `case.close`, `case.reopen`, `case.keep` and `case.migrate` for 221B's, each with the case and its cluster; never a detail's, draft's or RCA's text. | Go tests read the records. |
| CU-14 | **Storage and backup.** The case itself is in the issue service's reports key and its backup part; the work on it in `<release>-casework`'s `cases.json` (512 KiB): the work on the oldest closed case goes first, then the history of old open ones; a person's draft is never dropped, and a change that does not fit beside the open cases' work is refused. The backup part *Cases' work* (order 260) holds the work whole with the old ids; a restore adds what is missing, and a wave-4 backup's cases are carried over after it. | Go tests `TestTheWorkFitsItsKey`, `TestBackupOfTheWork`. |

## Phase 12: One flow for findings and cases

Asked for on November 7, 2026, in the owner's design of the case flow (the design document *Cases: one flow for findings and reports (wave 6 design)*, reviewed and approved line by line): every problem is one **Case**, whether an agent found it or a person reported it; a case goes *New* → *In progress* → closed with one outcome (*Fix*, *Close*, *Known issue* or *Exception*); an agent never opens the same case twice; a person sees similar cases before creating one; priorities run P0 to P4. CF-01 to CF-45 supersede the findings' own triage (FX-01 to FX-03, FX-07, JB-13, JR-09, JR-10), the Inbox's Findings kind and, where they differ, CU-01, CU-05 to CU-09, CU-12 and CU-14. ADR-126 to ADR-133 have the decisions. CF-46 to CF-49 (asked on November 8, 2026: "have Sherlock and Watson on the case drawer … the chat history has to stay with the case; the fix can be reused for future cases") troubleshoot a case in its drawer; ADR-134 has the decisions. The server side is the issue service (`server/internal/issues`), package `casework` (the routing of findings, the outcomes) and package `jobs` (the check catalogue, the object's normal form); the screens are `web/src/components/cases/CaseDrawer.tsx`, `NewCase.tsx`, the run detail and Administration › AI › Cases.

It solves these problems in the flow before it (0.12.0 dev8):

- One problem was two objects: a finding was triaged (*New Case* or *Ignore*), then the case was decided again (CF-01 to CF-03).
- A nightly run reported the same problems every night, known issues included, and nothing linked a repeat to its case, known issue or exception, so the same item came back to the Inbox until it was fixed (CF-05, CF-06).
- Muting known issues instead would have lost the proof that a fix worked and the warning when it returns (CF-06).
- *Close* took any reason, so nothing decided what happened when the problem was seen again: *Not a problem* on an agent's finding came back the next night, and the explanation was not shown to the next reporter (CF-03, CF-05).
- A person created a case without seeing the open case, known issue or recent close that already covered it (CF-12).
- A case could be closed as a duplicate of a closed case, attaching the reporter to work that had ended (CF-04).
- Agents named the same problem differently from night to night, so a repeat could open a new case and an exception could stop applying (CF-08 to CF-10).
- Exceptions could pile up, every one given to the agents (CF-10, CF-13, CF-19).
- Every case had an SLA, so a nightly audit's low-priority findings all went late (CF-16).
- A proposed change did not say which cases it resolved, and applying it changed no case: someone had to find the case and close it by hand, and nothing proved the change fixed it (CF-25 to CF-31).

| ID | Requirement | Done when |
| --- | --- | --- |
| CF-01 | **One object.** Every problem is a case (`C-<n>`): a person's report (the report page, the account menu, Sherlock's *Report this*, the issue API), *New Case*, a case from an alert, and every finding of an agent's run, which becomes a case or joins one. A case opened from a finding is *agent-found*; every other case is *human-created*. Findings are not triaged on their own any more: there is no *New Case* or *Ignore* on a finding. | Go `TestTheRoutingTable`, `TestFindingsAreKeyedAndRouted`; vitest of the run detail (`RunsTab.test.tsx`); e2e `findings.spec.ts`. |
| CF-02 | **States**: *New* (nobody has taken it), *In progress* (it has an owner; the analysis, the suggested fix and applying it happen here) and *Closed*, with exactly one outcome. A closed case is reopened by a person, with a reason (required). | Go `TestTheStatusesAndOutcomes`, `TestKnownIssueExceptionAndReopen`; vitest "reopens a closed case only with a reason". |
| CF-03 | **Outcomes**: *Fix* (what was done, and an RCA: required at P0 and P1, optional otherwise; the RCA is the case's, CF-44); *Duplicate* (of an original, CF-04); *Not a problem* (an explanation, shown if the same problem is reported again; human-created cases only); *No fix needed* (an explanation; one-off, transient or already gone); *Known issue* (link an existing one or create one); *Exception* (a reason, an optional last day, permanent otherwise; agent-found cases only, those who approve proposed changes). The server says which outcomes a case allows (`allowedOutcomes`) and refuses the others; the drawer offers only those. Since CF-90, *Not a problem* and *No fix needed* are one outcome, *No action needed*, for every case. | Go `TestOutcomesPerSource`, `TestFixAndItsRCA`; vitest "offers only the outcomes the server allows", "fixes with an RCA, required at P0 and P1". |
| CF-04 | **Duplicates only of an open original.** The original is the end of any chain of duplicates; a case is never its own original, directly or through a chain. An original that is New or In progress takes this case's reporter and history. A closed original refuses the close and offers what fits: closed as *Fix* → reopen it (the fix did not hold) or keep this case as a regression of it; as *Known issue* → close this case as the same known issue (if resolved, keep this case as a regression); as *Exception* → close as *Not a problem* explained with the exception's reason, or an approver removes the exception and this case stays open; as *Not a problem* → close the same way reusing its explanation, or reopen it; as *No fix needed* → reopen it, or keep this case linked as *same as*. The check runs when the original is picked (`GET …/duplicate-check`) and again at the close (409 with the offers). Since CF-90, an original closed as *No action needed* offers all three: close the same way reusing its explanation, reopen it, or *same as*. | Go `TestTheDuplicateCheck`, `TestCloseAndTheDuplicateRule`, `TestTheOffers`; vitest "offers what fits instead when the original is closed", "shows the offers again when the server refuses the duplicate at confirm (409)". |
| CF-05 | **A repeat never opens a second case.** The key of an agent-found case is its cluster, object and check. A finding with the key of an earlier case: joins it when New or In progress (*seen ×N*, first and last seen; nothing new in the Inbox); follows a duplicate to its original; is recorded on the known issue when closed as one (*still present*, with a count and last seen; nothing in the Inbox); is dropped when an active exception covers it (counted on the run and on the exception); reopens the case closed as *Fix* when it is the first run of that job since ("the fix did not hold"), and opens a new case marked *regression of C-n* later; opens a new case showing the earlier explanation, who wrote it and when, after *No fix needed* (since CF-90, *No action needed*). Without an earlier case it opens a new agent-found case. | Go `TestTheRoutingTable` (one case per row). |
| CF-06 | **Fixes and resolved known issues are verified.** A run of a job that does not see the key of a case closed as *Fix* since marks the fix verified, with the run. Since the review (CF-94), only a run that re-checked the case's object inside its scope and found the problem gone verifies it; a resolved known issue is done for a job only when every one of its keys is inside the run's scope, none seen again or left out past the cap. A run after a known issue is resolved that still sees one of its keys reopens the known issue (status investigating) with an update saying so, and its owner finds it pending in the Inbox. Resolving a known issue reopens none of its cases; their reporters are told it is resolved. | Go `TestTheRoutingTable` (verification), `TestAResolvedKnownIssueIsVerified`. |
| CF-07 | **Related and possible duplicates.** Agent repeats never match a human-created case (it has no check). An agent-found case on an object with an open human-created case lists that case as related; one with no key match but an open case on the same cluster and object with a similar title is marked *possible duplicate of C-n* for a person to confirm, never merged. | Go `TestTheRoutingTable`; vitest "shows an agent-found case's key, how often it was seen, its links, the previous close and no SLA". |
| CF-08 | **A fixed list of check ids per job kind**, given in the agent's task; an agent answers one of them or `other.<slug>`, and a finding with any other check is kept on the run as invalid, not routed. | Go `TestValidCheck`, `TestFindingsAreKeyedAndRouted`. |
| CF-09 | **The object's normal form**: `namespace/Kind/name`, or `Kind/name` for a cluster-scoped object, or nothing for the whole cluster; short names and kinds in any case resolve to the canonical kind (`deploy`, `crb`, `svc`, `cm`, …), and the object is looked up on the cluster when it answers (marked unverified otherwise, never refused for that). | Go `TestNormalizeObject`. |
| CF-10 | **The agent sees the keys in use**: an analysis run's task lists, as data, the open cases, the known issues still present and the active exceptions on its cluster with their keys (exceptions up to the *exceptions per agent* setting, the most recently matched first), and asks it to reuse a check id for the same problem. The console drops an excepted finding itself, so an exception beyond the cap still applies. | Go `TestKeysInUseW4`, `TestFindingsAreKeyedAndRouted`. |
| CF-11 | **A run's report** says what each finding did (opened, seen, known, excepted, reopened, regression, capped, invalid) with a link to its case, known issue or exception, the fixes it verified, and the check ids new on that cluster. A run opens at most *new cases per run* (Administration › AI › Cases); the rest are listed as capped. | Go `TestTheCapInvalidAndNewChecks`; vitest `RunsTab.test.tsx`. |
| CF-12 | **Before a person creates a case**, New Case shows beside the form, as they type (`POST /api/v1/cases/similar`, the issue service's match, no agent): an open case on the same object or close in words (*Add to C-n*: follow it and add a comment); a matching known issue with its workaround and expected fix (*Link to KI-n*: the case is created closed as that known issue); an exception on the same object, with its reason and who accepted it; a case closed as *Not a problem* or *No fix needed* within the look-back (*Open* or *Reopen* it); a case closed as *Fix* within the look-back with its RCA (*Create as regression of C-n*). Creating anyway is always possible. Sherlock's case creation shows the same matches; the report page shows only matching known issues and their workarounds, never other people's cases. | Go `TestNewCaseSimilarAndJoin`, `TestSimilarCases`; vitest "New Case shows what is like it"; report page `report.test.tsx`. |
| CF-13 | **Exceptions** cover one cluster, object and check; each keeps how often it matched and when it last did; Mycroft › Exceptions sorts by that and filters the stale ones (never matched, or not within *an exception is stale after*). When the same check on a cluster has been closed as *No fix needed* three times, its next case suggests an exception; none is ever made by itself. Exceptions are the console's records only, never wiki pages. | Go `TestExceptionMatchesAndStale`, `TestSuggestException`; vitest `LessonsViews.test.tsx`. |
| CF-14 | **Known issues** link the cases closed as them (many to one), and show *still present* with the count and last seen. | Go `TestAResolvedKnownIssueIsVerified`; vitest `Cases.test.tsx`. |
| CF-15 | **Priorities P0 to P4**: P0 Outage, P1 Blocking, P2 Service degradation, P3 Preemptive, P4 Question, everywhere (cases, the report page, the issue API, the SLA's targets). An agent's finding takes P1 when critical, P3 when warning, P4 when info, never P0; a case from an alert P0 when critical, P2 when warning. People may change any priority. | Go `TestTheCapInvalidAndNewChecks`; vitest "sets the priority P0 to P4, and the assignee". |
| CF-16 | **SLA**: human-created cases have the targets of Administration › AI › SLA at every priority; agent-found cases only at P0 and P1. Raising an agent-found case to P0 or P1 starts its clock then; lowering it stops the clock. A case without a clock shows *no SLA*, never *late*. | Go `TestTheSLAClock`, `TestTheSLA`; vitest of the Inbox (late only when the SLA applies). |
| CF-17 | **The Inbox** lists cases, approvals, proposed changes, knowledge drafts and the known issues pending for their owner; there is no Findings kind. A case is pending while New or In progress. A *Source* filter (People, Agents; `?source=`) joins the others. | vitest `Inbox.test.tsx`, `inboxRows.test.ts`; e2e `inbox.spec.ts`. |
| CF-18 | **The case drawer** shows an agent-found case's key, *seen ×N* with the last time, *regression of*, *possible duplicate of*, *same as*, related cases, the earlier explanation of a repeat, the exception suggestion and *no SLA*; its actions are the outcomes allowed (CF-03) and Reopen. | vitest `CaseDrawer.test.tsx`; e2e `casework.spec.ts`. |
| CF-19 | **Administration › AI › Cases** (SuperAdmin changes it; the console-wide roles read it): keep closed agent-found cases for (90 days), look back for recently closed cases on New Case (90 days), an exception is stale after no match for (90 days), exceptions given to an agent per run, per cluster (50), new cases one run may open (50); days 1 to 3650, counts 1 to 500. | Go `TestSettingsAndRetention`; vitest of Administration › AI › Cases. |
| CF-20 | **One ConfigMap per case** (`<release>-case-<n>`, labelled with the release and the case's number) holds the case and 221B's work on it, so a case is written alone and the cases are not bounded by one object's size; an index in the issue service's ConfigMap (a short line per case: number, status, outcome, priority, cluster, object, check, source, owner, times, seen) serves the lists, the routing and the matching. A repeat updates the index, and the case at most once a run. The chart's Role allows creating and deleting ConfigMaps in the release's namespace for this. A new case's object is written before the index that numbers it; when the index's save then meets another writer's (a conflict), the object just written is deleted before the change is tried again from the index read anew, so the retry takes the same number instead of failing on its own object ("object exists", five times); an object that cannot be deleted has its number skipped (phase-13.1-ai, November 23, 2026). | Go `TestEachCaseIsAnObjectOfItsOwn`, `TestACaseRetriedAfterAnIndexConflictKeepsItsNumber`; chart check "each case is a ConfigMap the console makes". |
| CF-21 | **Retention**: the leader deletes closed agent-found cases older than *keep closed agent-found cases for* (the index line and the ConfigMap), daily; human-created cases keep the issue service's retention of closed text. | Go `TestSettingsAndRetention`. |
| CF-22 | **Backups** save and restore the cases' ConfigMaps by their label. | Go `TestBackupOfTheCases`, `TestExportAndImport`. |
| CF-23 | **A clean start of the Cases area** before 0.12.0 ships: with `cases.cleanStart.token` set and different from the marker it last wrote, the leader deletes once the cases (their ConfigMaps and index) and known issues, 221B's work on them (analyses, drafts, history, exceptions, RCAs), the jobs' runs and findings, the proposed changes agents made for cases or runs, and Mycroft's pages, drafts and excerpts; it keeps the issue service's settings, services and tokens, the SLA targets (renumbered P0–P4 with their values), the jobs with their schedules and instructions, Watson's cards and approvals, Mycroft's sources and credentials, and everything outside Cases. With `cases.cleanStart.dryRun` it only logs what it would delete. Audited `cases.clean` with the counts. | Go `TestTheCleanStartTouchesOnlyTheCasesArea`. |
| CF-24 | **Audit**: each outcome, reopen, join, duplicate offer applied, exception made or removed, routing of a run (counts), retention and the clean start. | Go tests of each action (`TestTheHTTPContract`, `TestCaseworkAccess`). |
| CF-25 | **A proposed change names what it resolves**: a card carries `resolves`, one or more cases (`C-n`) and known issues (`KI-n`), at most 20, each once, with who linked each (`resolvesBy`: the agent or a person). The Inbox row, the proposal drawer and the run detail show *Resolves C-12, KI-3*, each a link (a case opens its drawer, a known issue Mycroft › Known issues). | Go `TestACardResolvesCasesAndKnownIssuesCF25`; vitest "a proposed change names what it resolves (CF-25)", "shows what each proposed change resolves, linked, …". |
| CF-26 | **The agent names findings, the console names cases.** In a job's answer each proposal's `fixes` lists the positions of findings in the same answer and ids from the keys in use (`C-n`, `KI-n`). After the run's findings are routed, a position becomes the case or known issue its finding went to (an excepted, invalid or capped finding resolves nothing); an id must exist, be open and be on the proposal's cluster (a known issue only needs to exist). A case analysis always resolves its own case, plus the open cases it names; a report's triage resolves its case. | Go `TestProposalsResolveWhatTheirFixesNameCF26CF27`, `TestFixesAreReadForgivinglyCF26`, `TestWhatAProposalMayResolveCF26`, `TestACaseAnalysisResolvesItsCaseCF26`. |
| CF-27 | **An agent's change must resolve something.** A proposal from a job run (every kind, the case analysis and report triage included) that resolves nothing is not filed: the run keeps it with the error *names no case it resolves* and a note, and the runs' list counts it apart (*34 findings, 1 proposed change not filed*), since only a filed change reaches the Inbox (the owner, November 22, 2026). A change a person asked for in a chat, and Mycroft's knowledge changes, may resolve nothing. | Go `TestProposalsResolveWhatTheirFixesNameCF26CF27`, `TestACardResolvesCasesAndKnownIssuesCF25`; vitest of the run detail (refused proposal). |
| CF-28 | **Links both ways, and a person can link.** A case lists every change that resolves it, from any source, with its state now (waiting, approved, applied, failed, rejected, expired, replaced, gone), who linked it and when it was applied. Who works cases may link a waiting, approved or applied change on the case's cluster to an open case (`POST …/cases/{id}/changes`), and unlink a link a person made (`DELETE …/changes/{card}`); an agent's link is never removed, only its change rejected (409). A change replaced by a newer one passes its links on. Linking an applied change records the fix at once (CF-29). Audit `cases.change-link`, `cases.change-unlink`. | Go `TestAPersonLinksAndUnlinksACaseCF28`, `TestAReplacedCardPassesItsResolvesOnCF28`, `TestLinkingAChangeToACaseCF28`; vitest "lists every change from the link, …", "links a change from a list of the case's cluster's cards …", "shows the server's refusal in the dialog …"; e2e `changes.spec.ts`. |
| CF-29 | **Applied: the fix is recorded and waits to be checked.** When a change reaches *applied*, each case it resolves records *Fix applied by change N* (by the approver), moves from New to In progress (owned by the approver when it has no owner) and, when agent-found, waits for the next run of the job that found it. A human-created case only records the fix; a person closes it. A known issue gets an update. A rejected, failed or expired change leaves a note and changes nothing else. The cards' store tells casework of each change of state, and the leader catches up on any it missed; each case acts on a change once. When the run finds it again, or re-checks it and finds it still there: "The fix did not hold", the check is cleared and the case stays In progress. | Go `TestAVerifiedFixClosesTheCaseBelowP1CF30`, `TestAVerifiedFixWaitsForItsRCAAtP1CF30`, `TestAFixThatDidNotHoldCF30`. |
| CF-31 | **Shown on the case and in the Inbox.** The case says *Fix applied by change N, waiting for the next run of job to check*, *Verified: add the RCA to close*, or *The fix did not hold*; the Inbox row carries a tag (*fix applied*, *fix verified*, *fix did not hold*; since CF-107 the case's stage: *Waiting for a validation run*, *Waiting for closure*, *Fix did not hold*). Reporters never see it. | vitest "waits for the next run of the job that found it, linked", "at P0 and P1, verified: …", "says when the fix did not hold", "badges a case row in the Inbox with its check, …". |
