The leader runs the queue one analysis at a time, highest priority then oldest, within the job's run and day budgets (the rest wait for the next day) and only while the job is on. A case leaves the queue when it closes, when a person presses *Analyse* (which still runs at once), or when its turn starts. | Go `TestNewAgentCasesAtP0ToP2AreQueuedCF32`, `TestTheQueueRunsOneAtATimeHighestFirstCF32`; vitest of the drawer ("Analysis queued"). |

| CF-47 | **Sherlock on a case.** A case has one conversation with Sherlock, on the case's cluster, shared by everyone who works the cases; the console-wide roles and those who may edit jobs, who read the cases, read it and do not ask. Each question runs as its asker (their tools and rights, as Sherlock does everywhere) and names them; the conversation starts from the case as data (its id, title, priority, type, cluster, object and detail, its latest analysis and the records like it, CF-43). It is kept in the case's object (bounded: the last 40 messages, each clipped and scrubbed, an answer's hand-overs with it, DB-58, within 96 KiB), so it survives a restart and a change of leader, and it streams as a person's own does. | CF-111 | **The keys in use are all of them, or none** (phase 12 second review R2-12, November 11, 2026; amends CF-10, CF-99). The keys a run gives its agent (the open cases, the fixes waiting to be re-checked, the known issues still present and the active exceptions on the cluster) are read whole or not at all: when the cases' index or the known issues cannot be read, as when the exceptions cannot (W-7), `KeysInUse` returns an error ("the cases could not be read: …", "the known issues could not be read: …") and gives no list. The run notes that its agent was not given the keys and why; the agent works without them, as it did when the exceptions could not be read, and is never given a list without the open cases or the fixes to re-check as if there were none. | Go `TestAFailedReadOfTheCasesGivesNoKeysR212` (casework), `TestKeysThatCannotBeReadAreSaidOnTheRunR212` (jobs). |
| CF-112 | **A case names its job, not its id** (phase 12 second review R2-15, November 11, 2026; follows U-11, U-12). A case's drawer names the job that found it by its label wherever it showed the job's id: the line under the title ("found by Auditor (Nightly audit)", "found by Nightly audit" when no agent is named) and *Opened from* ("an agent's finding in run-3 · Nightly audit"). The id stays as the hint of the name. The labels come from the jobs' list the drawer reads (a job's own label over its kind's); until it comes, and for whoever may not read it, the id stands. | vitest `CaseDrawer.test.tsx` *a case names its job, not its id (R2-15)*, `runNames.test.tsx` *a job by its name (R2-15)*. |
| CF-113 | **A job's schedule also in the browser's time** (phase 12 second review R2-16, the owner's choice, November 11, 2026). In AIOps › Jobs › Schedule, *When* stays in the console's zone, as the schedule is set ("Every day at 02:00", the zone under it), and where the browser's zone differs at that time the browser's time follows the zone: "UTC · 6:00pm PST" (the time of the zone's current day, in the console's time format, its hint saying it is the browser's). Where the two agree, nothing more is shown; a cron schedule, which has no one time, shows none. *Last run* and *Next run* stay in the browser's zone, as every time in the console. | vitest `JobsTab.test.tsx` *a job's schedule beside the browser's time (R2-16)*, `format.test.ts` *a time of day in another zone, by the browser's clock (R2-16)*. |
| CF-114 | **The dock steps aside for a drawer's own Sherlock** (phase 12 second review R2-8, November 11, 2026; amends CF-46). While a split drawer shows a Sherlock of its own that the person can ask in (a case's *Troubleshoot* for whoever works the cases, a waiting proposed change's for whoever may ask, Sherlock on for the cluster), the open dock is put away and the drawer reaches the bottom of the window: the dock no longer takes a third of its height or shows a second Sherlock and Watson under it. A drawer whose conversation cannot be asked in (read-only, decided, Sherlock off) keeps the dock, where the person asks (IB-10 to IB-13); a maximized dock is left as it is. The dock is back as it was when the drawer closes. | e2e `troubleshoot.spec.ts` *the open dock is put away while a case’s own Sherlock shows…*; `inbox.spec.ts`, `proposal-trouble.spec.ts`, `sherlock-ui.spec.ts`, `watson.spec.ts`. |
| CF-115 | **A waiting fix found again with no case is counted and says why** (phase 12 second review R2-10, November 11, 2026; amends CF-95). When a run whose scope holds a waiting fix's key finds that problem again, but the finding opens no case and joins none (an active exception covers it, the agent marked it expected, or it is below the job's lowest priority), the case is not reopened and its fix is not said not to hold: the run counts it among the fixes it did not re-check, the case counts the run (*Unchecked*), and its wait says why: "run-12 found it again and opened no case: exception E-3 covers it" (or "…: the agent marked it expected", "…: below the job's lowest priority"), in the case's drawer after how many runs did not re-check it. The reason goes with the wait: verified, reopened, closed without validation or a new fix applied. A finding that did reach the case (seen, reopened, the fix did not hold) is routed as before. | Go `TestAFindingThatOpensNoCaseLeavesTheFixWaitingR210` (casework: excepted, expected, below the lowest priority); vitest `CaseChanges.test.tsx` *says on a waiting case why the last run did not re-check it (R2-10)*, *says a verification and a wait in plain words*. |
| CF-116 | **A run routed twice counts once** (phase 12 second review R2-11, November 11, 2026; amends CF-01, CF-06, CF-13, CF-95). A run is routed again when its first routing went through but the run could not be saved with it (W-8). Routing it again changes nothing the first did: each count kept remembers the run that counted last (a case's *seen ×N* with the finding and how it was routed, a known issue's *still present*, a waiting fix's runs that did not re-check it, an exception's matches, the check ids new on a cluster), and the second routing counts nothing again and gives each finding the routing and case the first gave it (opened, seen, reopened, a regression, known, excepted), so the run is saved with them. Within one run a case is seen, and a known issue still present, once: a second finding of the same problem in the run joins the case without counting. The second routing does not list again the fixes the first verified; the cases keep the run that verified them. Mycroft's matches of an exception are not a run's and count each time. | Go `TestARunRoutedTwiceCountsOnceR211` (casework: the cases, seen counts, notes, known issue, exception, not-re-checked counts and the findings the same after a second routing), `TestExceptionMatchesAndStale` (lessons: the same run's matches once). |
| CF-117 | **One node failure is one case** (the owner, November 23, 2026: C-13 to C-19, one case for the node r620-worker and one P0 case for each of its six pods, all from one failure; amends CF-78). Before the alert safety net (CF-78) opens a case for a critical alert past its minutes, and after its covering check (CF-77) and CF-82's, it works the alert out to its node, deterministically and with no model: a node's alert to the node itself, a pod's to the node it runs on as the console's live view of the pods names it (the alert's `Node`); a claim's volume and a zone are not followed. An open case on the cluster whose correlation key is `node:<cluster>/<node>`, or, while the node is down (its alert among the alerts says Unknown or Not ready) or the pod is Unknown, one about `Node/<node>` (such as the Troubleshooter's C-13), takes the alert: it is attached as the case's evidence (a row of `case_signals` with the alert's cluster, key, name and object, when, by the alert safety net, *same node*), the case's history says *Alert <name> (<object>) attached: same node*, its seen count goes up one, and `case.attach` is audited; no case is opened. With no such case and the node down, the net opens ONE case about the node (from the node's alert, as CF-78's case: P0, an outage, `Node/<node>`), with the correlation key, and attaches every critical pod alert on that node that no case of its own covers; a later pod's alert on that node attaches to it. A node's alert is looked at before its pods' in a look. An alert attached covers it from then on (CF-77: the Alerts tab shows that case, and a new leader does not attach it twice or open a case for it). A pod failing on a healthy node keeps its own case though a case about its node is open; without the node's state (no node alert) and with no node case, each pod keeps its own case, as before. | Go `TestOneNodeFailureIsOneCaseCF117` (the owner's scenario: one case, six alerts attached, Pending 1, a seventh pod attached later), `TestAPodAlertOnANodeWithAnOpenCaseOpensNoneCF117`, `TestWithoutTheNodesStateEachPodKeepsItsCaseCF117` (casework, both stores); vitest `CaseDrawer.test.tsx` *lists the alerts attached to a node’s case (CF-117)*. |
| CF-118 | **The case analysis says which open cases share the cause** (the owner, November 23, 2026: C-15's analysis said *Same cause as open cases C-13 (node), C-14…* and nothing acted on it). The case analysis's answer has two more fields, read like the others: `same_cause_as` (a list of cases, `C-13`) and `root_case` (the one about the cause itself, or the case's own id); camelCase is read too. Only open cases on the case's cluster, other than itself, are kept, ten at most, and the root only when it is one of them or the case itself; they are kept with the analysis (`case_analyses.same_cause_as_case_keys`, `root_case_key`). The task's format asks for them, and what the console holds for the analysis (CF-43) lists the other open cases on the case's cluster, ten at most, newest first, which may share its cause. An open case whose analysis names another open case (the root when it is another case, else the first named) shows *Same cause. The analysis says this case has the same cause as C-13 …* with **Merge into C-13** for whoever may close it, one click that applies CF-119's merge; the root's own case suggests nothing. Only CF-117's node match attaches by itself: a merge the agent infers waits for a person. | Go `TestTheAnalysisNamesTheCasesWithTheSameCauseCF118`, `TestGoldenPrompts`, `TestTheAnalysisStartsFromWhatTheConsoleHoldsCF43` (casework); vitest `CaseDrawer.test.tsx` *shows the analysis’s "Merge into C-13" and merges with one click (CF-118)*, *offers no merge to one who may not close the case, and none without a suggestion*. |
| CF-119 | **Merge into case…** (the owner, November 23, 2026). Any open case may be merged into another open case, by hand from *Merge into case…* in the case's footer (the target's id and an optional reason) or from CF-118's suggestion: `POST /api/v1/aiops/cases/{id}/merge` with `into` (`C-13`) and `explanation`. The case closes as a duplicate of the target with *Merged into C-13* (and the reason) as its explanation and `mergedInto` pointing at it (`cases.merged_into_case_id`, its number beside); its alerts move to the target, and so does its own alert when an alert opened it, each attached there *merged from C-n* by the person and counted seen; both histories say so (the target's: *C-n (title) was merged into this case: its N alerts moved here; its history and its conversation with Sherlock stay on C-n*); it keeps its history, its messages and its conversation with Sherlock. Closed, it leaves the Inbox's Pending list and count and its SLA stops: the target's stand. It is a data operation of the console (`case.merge` in the audit log), not a command on a cluster and not through Watson; whoever may close a case may merge it (403 otherwise). Refused (400): into itself, into a closed case, a closed case; a case that is not there is 404. A merged case reopened is merged no more. | Go `TestMergeIntoACaseCF119` (casework, both stores); vitest `CaseDrawer.test.tsx` *merges into any open case by hand, from Merge into case… (CF-119)*. |

## Phase 12.1: the agents' and jobs' instructions

Asked for on November 12, 2026, after the 0.12.0 release: a review of every prompt an agent or a job is sent against open-source projects under Apache-2.0 with comparable prompts, and the changes it proposed, on the branch `phase-12.1-maint`. [phase-12.1-prompts.md](phase-12.1-prompts.md) has the review, the sources and what differs from the proposal; [prompt-sources.md](prompt-sources.md) records the sources. No text is copied from any of them.

| ID | Requirement | Acceptance |
|---|---|---|
| PM-C1 | **Evidence.** Every agent says only what it read: a cause it could not confirm is *likely*, with the read that would confirm it; an object is affected only on its own evidence; it says what it checked and found healthy and what it did not check (`common.md`; one line in `sherlockCompact.md`, PM-C7). | The golden cores hold the line; `TestTheRulesEveryAgentKeeps`. |
| PM-C2 | **Reads.** Every agent is told how many rounds of reads one answer gets (the mark `{{rounds}}`, `sherlock.DefaultMaxRounds`), to ask in one round for the reads that do not depend on each other, never to repeat a read, and to read up to three pods of one workload. | `TestEveryMarkOfAPromptFileIsFilled`. |
| PM-C3 | **Knowledge first.** Before a diagnosis or a change plan an agent asks the knowledge base once whether the problem is known, fixed before or accepted, unless its task gives it those records; they are records, checked on the cluster. | The golden cores hold the line. |
| PM-C4 | **Another agent's answer is evidence**, read as a tool's is. | The hand-over line of `common.md`. |
| PM-C5 | **The last round answers.** On the last round the engine allows, the last message also says *This is your last round: answer now…*, in a chat and in an agent's run alike; the history never keeps the note. | `TestTheLastRoundIsSaid`. |
| PM-C6 | **A job's answer without a block is said, and asked for once.** When the answer of a nightly audit, an alert diagnosis or a daily digest has no JSON block the console can read, the run notes it and the agent is asked once for the block alone, which is read with the first answer's report; still none, the run keeps its report, has no findings from it and says so. | `TestAnAnswerWithoutABlockIsAskedForOnce`. |
| PM-A1 | **When it started, and what changed before.** Sherlock and the Troubleshooter find when a failure started and compare it with the changes just before; a change after it began did not cause it. | The golden cores. |
| PM-A2 | **The Troubleshooter: cause, not symptom**, one chain for each cause; a pod its controller replaced is not harmed; Ready is not healthy. Its tools gain `list_policies`, `list_helm_releases`, `get_helm_release` and `automation_runs`, which its instructions and the case analysis already sent it to. | `TestAnAgentHasTheToolsItsInstructionsName`. |
| PM-A3 | **The Change planner** lists what a request leaves open as questions, gives every plan its way back and says how to tell it worked: *Undo* and *Check* after the commands. A job's proposal says both in `impact`. | The golden core and the output formats. |
| PM-A4 | **The Auditor** reports one finding for one problem, leaves system namespaces out, and reports a rule only on evidence a tool showed. Its tools gain `list_volumes` and `trace_ingress`. | As PM-A2. |
| PM-A5 | **The Host doctor** goes from the Kubernetes sign to the part of the host it points at, orders checks from harmless to intrusive with what a healthy reading looks like, and says how old the node's facts are. | The golden core. |
| PM-A6, PM-A7, PM-J9 | **Automation's script rules, said once.** `automation.ScriptRules` and `automation.ReviewRules` are in the Automation screen's tasks and, through marks, in the Automation author's and the Script reviewer's cores, so a script drafted or reviewed through a hand-over keeps them. New rules: a copy before a file changes, a check after, timeouts, a stopped service started again, versions compared part by part; the review looks at a failure half way, waits, input, empty variables and secrets in output, and ends with one of three verdicts. | `TestEveryMarkOfAPromptFileIsFilled`; the golden files `agent.automationAuthor`, `agent.scriptReviewer`, `automation.draft`, `automation.review`. |
| PM-A8, PM-J4 | **The Summarizer and the daily digest** open with one line of the overall state; the digest raises a finding only for what no case tracks. | The golden files. |
| PM-J1a | **Severities defined** in the jobs' output format: critical, warning, info. | `TestTheOutputFormatSaysWhatItsWordsMean`. |
| PM-J1b | **A finding names the object that owns the problem**: the workload when every pod of it has it, since a pod's generated name changes and its case would not be found again. | As PM-J1a. |
| PM-J1c | **A fix is verified only by a read.** `"still"` is false only when the agent read the object and the problem is gone; `null` when it could not read it. `"still": null` is read as no answer: before, it was read as false, which verified the fix. | `TestAStillOfNullIsNoAnswer`. |
| PM-J1d | **Confirmed or likely.** A finding and a case's analysis carry `confidence`; the run's detail tags a likely finding, the case says *Likely cause*, and a likely cause's suggested fix does not become a proposed change by itself. | `TestAFindingsConfidence`, `TestALikelyCauseProposesNothingByItself`; the web tests of the case drawer and the runs. |
| PM-J2 | **The alert diagnosis** first decides what the alert is (a problem now, one that recovered, the system working as intended) and names one cause once for the alerts that fired together. | The golden file `job.alert-diagnosis`. |
| PM-J3 | **The nightly audit** also asks for what `review_cluster` reports under reliability and platform. | The golden file `job.nightly-audit`. |
| PM-J5 | **A case's analysis says what it ruled out**, shown in the drawer and given to the next analysis; sent back, the analysis is told not to repeat itself and is given what people wrote on the case. | `TestALikelyCauseProposesNothingByItself`; the golden file `case.analysis-again`. |
| PM-J6 | **An RCA's draft** also gives the timeline, how the problem was detected and what it affected; they fill the RCA's free text, a line each, when that is empty. | `TestAnRCADraftsFreeText`. |
| PM-J7 | **Report triage** asks the one question that would settle the service rather than guess; **the check of a fix** answers fixed only on a read. | The golden files `report.triage`, `report.verify`. |
| PM-J8 | **A runbook page** has the same parts in the same order. | The golden file `wiki.sync`. |
| PM-R1, PM-R2 | **Validation rules.** Ten more for the alert diagnosis and fifteen more for the nightly audit, and three RBAC rules reworded to what a tool can show (the list is in [phase-12.1-prompts.md](phase-12.1-prompts.md#validation-rules)). A rule a SuperAdmin added under an id that now ships becomes that built-in rule with their words as its edit. | `TestTheBuiltInRulesAgree`, `TestTheMergedListOfValidationRules`. |
| PM-S1 | **Prompt sources**: three rows with their commit, HolmesGPT's repository and license corrected, nine rows added. | `hack/check-prompt-sources.sh`. |
| PM-U1 | **Sherlock says it is still working.** While an answer runs (or a question is being sent), a banner above the question box (`sherlock-working`, a polite status) shows a spinner, what Sherlock is doing (from its latest answer, first match: *The X is working* for a running hand-over, a running read's label, *Writing the answer* once text streams, else *Thinking*, or *Sending*), the number of reads when there are any, the time since the answer started as m:ss (ticking, hidden from screen readers) and Stop, moved there from the panel's header. It also shows above a shared case conversation's read-only or unavailable note, without Stop for who may only read. The box's placeholder reads *Sherlock is working. Stop to ask something else.* The banner goes when the run ends; the dots in the answer stay. Out of sight: a pulsing dot on the dock's Sherlock toggle while Watson is in front (and on *Assistants* while a session tab is), and the page title starts with ● while it works, restored when it ends or the panel closes. Web only. | vitest `SherlockWorking.test.tsx`. |
| PM-D1 | **Sherlock is the front desk, and nothing else while the owners can be asked.** Its fixed text is its identity, its place in the console, how it answers and the person's screen (`sherlock.FrontDesk`). The troubleshooting manual, the two reports' parts, the node checks and the commands of a change are *own work* (`sherlock.OwnWork`), added to its instructions for each kind of work whose owner (Troubleshooter, Auditor, Host doctor, Change planner) is not in its list of agents, and only for those. With every owner in the list its instructions hold no manual and no report recipe; with no agents at all (`sherlock.BuiltIn`) they hold all four. | Go `TestSherlocksOwnWorkFollowsItsRoster`; golden `agent.sherlock`, `sherlock.own-work`, `sherlock.built-in-without-agents`. |
| PM-D2 | **Every kind of work has one owner**, said once to every agent (`common.md`, *Working with the other agents*): an agent's own work is its purpose, the list of agents says whose the rest is (PM-D6), and Sherlock owns the conversation, the screen and the answer. An agent stops where its work ends and names the part that is another's; it does that part itself only when the owner is not in its list or could not be asked, and says so. An agent's findings are passed on as they stand, and the cluster is not read again for them. No agent file tells its agent to do another's work: the Troubleshooter says what must change in words and gives no commands, the Auditor leaves fixes to the Change planner and failures to the Troubleshooter, the Change planner asks for a missing diagnosis rather than make one, the Script reviewer does not rewrite, the Summarizer does not diagnose. | Go `TestEveryKindOfWorkHasOneOwner`; goldens of every agent. |
| PM-D3 | **The manual and the reports are said once**, by whoever does the work: the six layers and the correlation rule (`sherlock.TroubleshootingManual`) and the troubleshooting report's parts (`sherlock.TroubleshootingReport`) in the Troubleshooter's instructions, the deficiency review's (`sherlock.DeficiencyReview`) in the Auditor's, filled from the marks `{{sherlock.TroubleshootingManual}}`, `{{sherlock.TroubleshootingReport}}` and `{{sherlock.DeficiencyReview}}`; Sherlock holds them only as own work (PM-D1). | Go `TestSherlockHandsTroubleshootingToTheTroubleshooter`; golden `agent.troubleshooter`, `agent.auditor`. |
| PM-D4 | **Only the agent asked first hands work over.** A handed task begins *Handed to you by <agent>.* (`sherlock.HandedBy`), and the rules tell an agent whose task begins so to do its own part and hand none of it on: the agent that asked brings the others in. The engine's limits (three deep, never the same agent twice in a chain) stay as the guard. In a job, the job's agent hands every change of the run to the Change planner in one hand-over. | Go `TestEveryKindOfWorkHasOneOwner`, `TestHandOver*`. |
| PM-D5 | **A hand-over carries what its agent needs.** The task holds up to 4,000 bytes (1,000 before, cut in the middle of a character at worst) and the context up to 12,000 (`MaxHandOverTask`, `MaxHandOverContext`), each cut at a character. In a conversation the engine adds the person's question and what their window showed, so the agent asked reads the question itself and not a retelling. The agent is told to answer complete, as the person should read it, rather than briefly. | Go `TestAHandOversTextIsCutAtACharacter`; golden `sherlock.agent-tools`. |
| PM-D6 | **An agent's instructions stay short.** What every agent is told is said once and in few words: the owners of the work are named by the list of agents and by no second list; the rules on working together are five lines; only an agent that may give Watson's commands (the Change planner, and the Troubleshooter and the Auditor when no Change planner can be asked) is told what Watson runs, and Sherlock only when the commands are its own work (PM-D1). Sherlock's text does not repeat what its view tools' descriptions say (the tabs' filters, what `view_aiops` and `view_admin` open). With every agent on, the assembled instructions are at most 9,500 bytes for Sherlock, 10,000 for the Troubleshooter (which holds the manual) and 6,500 for each other agent, and the shared rules at most 2,800. | Go `TestInstructionsStayShort`, `TestPromptsNameEveryCommandWatsonRuns`; goldens of every agent. |
| PM-D7 | **Instructions hold only what an agent learns nowhere else.** What a tool's description says (the tabs and their filters, the console's records, the knowledge base, how search and highlight work), what the engine says (the list of agents, the rule on text from the cluster, which it appends to any instructions without it, and the last round's note) and what the model knows of Kubernetes is not said again. Sherlock's instructions no longer include the rules every agent keeps: its own text says the ones it needs. The rules every agent keeps are ten lines. With every agent on, Sherlock's assembled instructions are at most 4,000 bytes, every other agent's at most 4,500, and the rules every agent keeps at most 1,200. | Go `TestInstructionsStayShort`, `TestPromptsNameEveryCommandWatsonRuns`; goldens of every agent. |

## Phase 12.1: Sherlock reads the console's records

Asked for on November 13, 2026, on the branch `phase-12.1-maint`: Sherlock and the agents read more of the console itself, so that they answer from an Automation run's output, a Watson command, the audit log or a case rather than say they cannot see it. Every new tool is a read of the API of the screen it mirrors, called as the person asking (A-2), so it answers only what that person may open there.

| ID | Requirement | Acceptance |
|---|---|---|
| SR-01 | **An Automation run's output**: `get_automation_run` reads one run (`GET /api/v1/automation/runs/{id}`): its script and version, fleet, who started it, state and note, and each node's state, exit code, error and the last lines of its output, 60 by default and at most 200 a node, at most 20 nodes with those whose part failed first, or one node by name. The values each node got and the run's overrides are left out. A SuperAdmin's, as Automation is. | `TestAutomationRunOutputIsScrubbedAndBounded`. |
| SR-02 | **One rule for the console's records**: each tool of SR-01 to SR-08 calls the console's own API in-process as the person (`tb.get`), so a person the screen refuses is told *Not allowed*; text people or nodes wrote (output, script text, messages, reports, details) is scrubbed of credentials as `pod_logs` is (P5-03) and cut to a bound that the result says. | `TestSherlockReadsTheConsolesRecordsAsThePerson`; `TestSherlockToolsOnlyRead`. |
| SR-03 | **Watson's history**: `watson_history` lists the commands the person's own Watson panel lists on the cluster (those they asked for or approved, and changes waiting for them as the second person), newest first, with how each ended and the last lines of its output (10 by default, at most 50), filtered by text and status. The panel's list is also served once, unstreamed, at `GET …/watson/history?lines=N` (at most 200 lines a command). | `TestWatsonHistoryIsThePersons` (the route), `TestWatsonHistoryIsThePersonsAndScrubbed` (the tool). |
| SR-04 | **The audit log**: `audit_log` reads Administration › Audit log (`GET /api/v1/admin/audit`) with its filters (words, action or the actions under it, outcome) and at most 200 events; a SuperAdmin's, as that page is. | `TestAuditLogIsASuperAdmins`. |
| SR-05 | **Automation's scripts**: `list_scripts` lists them as the Scripts tab does, with their parameters (secret ones marked); `get_script` reads one version (the latest by default) with its text, header, checks' findings, approvals and review, and the script's versions. A SuperAdmin's. | `TestScriptsAndVariablesKeepSecretValues`. |
| SR-06 | **Automation's variables**: `automation_variables` reads the Variables tab of the cluster (`GET …/automation/fleets`), by fleet or by node. The value of a parameter any script declares secret, and any value that names a Secret (`secret:namespace/name/key`), shows only that it is set (X-01). A SuperAdmin's. | As SR-05. |
| SR-07 | **Cases**: `list_cases` lists AIOps › Inbox's cases (`GET /api/v1/aiops/cases`) by status, open, text, priority and cluster; `get_case` reads one with its analysis, related cases, proposed changes, fix and verification, and its last 20 messages. For those the Inbox lets read the cases (CW-10). | `TestCasesAndJobRuns`. |
| SR-08 | **The jobs' runs**: `list_job_runs` lists AIOps › Jobs › Runs (`GET /api/v1/runs`) by job, status and reference; `get_job_run` reads one run's report (cut at 8,000 characters), health and findings (at most 50) with what each became. For those who may see AIOps › Jobs (JB-15). | As SR-07. |
| SR-09 | **Sherlock points at a case or a run**: the view action `view_aiops` opens the Inbox (pending or all, with its search), a case's drawer by its id, Jobs › Runs or a run's drawer by its id, in the cluster the person is on. It takes nothing else, and what the page shows comes through its API as for the person's own click (P6U-07). | `uiControl.test.ts` (*Sherlock points at a case or a run in AIOps*); `TestSherlockUIActionsList`. |
| SR-10 | **The agents that use them**: Sherlock has every tool of SR-01 to SR-08; the Troubleshooter `get_automation_run`, `watson_history`, `audit_log`, `list_cases` and `get_case`; the Host doctor `get_automation_run`; the Automation author `get_automation_run`, `list_scripts`, `get_script` and `automation_variables`; the Summarizer `list_job_runs`, `get_job_run` and `list_cases`. Their instructions name the tools they are sent to. An administrator who gave an agent fewer tools keeps that choice. | `TestAnAgentHasTheToolsItsInstructionsName`; `TestEveryAgentToolExists`. |
| SR-11 | **Sherlock's core names the records** it may read and `view_aiops`. | The golden files `agent.sherlock`, `sherlock.core-view-changes-off`, `sherlock.view-actions`, `sherlock.toolbox`. |
| SR-12 | **Sherlock opens Administration and Settings** (amends RB-48 and P6U-12): the view action `view_admin` opens the home page (from the console's own cluster), the cluster's connection page, Administration's Roles, Permissions, Users, Toolbox, Logs (Sessions, Audit log) and Backup, and every section of Settings (Getting started, General, Providers, Agents, Knowledge, Watson with its notification channels, Applications, Issues with Confluence, SLA, Cases, Jobs), each in the cluster the person is on, as its own link addresses it. It opens a role's, a person's, a provider's or an agent's drawer by its id, and sets the search of Roles, Permissions or Users. It only navigates: nothing on the page is edited, saved, toggled or pressed. Search, sort and highlight work on the tables of Administration's pages too. A provider's and an agent's drawer open from their address (`#/admin/ai/agents/<id>` since CU-18). | `uiControl.test.ts` (*Sherlock opens Administration and Settings pages*); `TestViewAdminDescribesThePagesTheBrowserOpens` with `testdata/ui_admin.json`; `sherlock-ui.spec.ts` (*opens a Settings page with an agent's drawer*). |
| SR-13 | **As the person's role opens them**: a page the person's role does not open is refused in the browser with the reason (Roles and Permissions for everyone signed in, Knowledge's Added in Mycroft card for an Admin, the rest for SuperAdmins; Permissions alone with sign-in off), from the same rule the Administration area offers its pages by (`lib/adminAccess.ts`), and the model is told to say so. | `uiControl.test.ts` (*refuses a page the person's role does not open*). |
| SR-14 | **Never away from unsaved changes**: while anything in the console window holds edits not saved yet (a Settings section's Save bar, the agent, provider, job and script drawers, an agent's instructions, notification channels, a release's values or source), registered in `lib/unsaved.ts`, the window applies no view change: the result says the view was left because of unsaved changes, and the audit record says *deferred*. A page the role does not open is still refused at once. | `uiControl.test.ts` (*leaves the view while a form holds unsaved changes*); `TestSherlockKeepsWhatTheWindowLeft`. |
| SR-15 | **Show in 221B**: a view change the window left undone (unsaved changes, the person moved the view during the answer, or the window did not answer in time) is kept under the answer, at most 10, and offered as a **Show in 221B** button that applies them in order when pressed, through the same checks. With unsaved changes still open it asks first (*Leave them and show* or *Stay*). With view changes turned off (P6U-14) there is no change and no button. | `sherlock-ui.spec.ts` (*with unsaved changes open, the view stays and Show in 221B applies it*); `uiControl.test.ts` (*applies what was left in order*). |
| SR-16 | **Every answer shows its page**: Sherlock's core asks it, with every answer about something that lives on a 221B page (objects, alerts, runs, cases, roles, people, agents, settings), to open that page and set its filters, search, sort or details to show exactly what the answer is about, and to leave the view for an answer about nothing on a page; the rules on checking the rows, an empty table, at most four changes and not widening a filter stay. | The golden files `agent.sherlock` and `sherlock.view-actions`. |
| SR-17 | **Escape closes Sherlock**: with the focus in Sherlock's pane in the docked panel, Escape closes the panel to its round icon, which takes the focus; maximized, Escape first restores it to the bottom of the page. A panel or drawer open on the page stays open, and a question being typed is kept for when Sherlock opens again. In the *Leave them and show* question Escape is *Stay*. In Watson or a session's terminal Escape is theirs. | e2e *Escape in Sherlock closes it to its icon, and maximized it first restores it (SR-17)*, *Sherlock is on the Administration pages too…*. |
| SR-18 | **The screen moves when the answer is done**: a view change Sherlock makes during an answer is not made then. The asking window checks it (its input, the tab, path and filters on the page the answer's earlier changes will open, a page the role does not open, the tables while no earlier change has moved the page) and the answer holds it; the model is told it is held and is made once the answer is complete. When the answer ends done, the server sends the held changes again, in order, and the window makes them, under the usual rules: unsaved changes, a view the person moved during the answer, or a window that does not answer leave them for **Show in 221B** (SR-15). An answer stopped or failed makes none and keeps them for the button. Reads (`view_state`) run at once. The audit record says *held*, then how each ended. Sherlock's core now asks it to decide the view from its reads, since a change no longer returns the view it makes (amends P6U-05 and the row check of SR-16). | Go `TestSherlockMakesViewChangesWhenTheAnswerIsDone`, `TestSherlockHoldsOnlyWhatTheWindowAccepts`, `TestSherlockUIActionsRunInTheAskingWindow`; vitest `uiControl.test.ts` (*a change held for the end of the answer*, *holds a change asked for during the answer*), `uiWindow.test.ts`. |
| SR-19 | **Any question, any view, in every Sherlock**: the docked panel, Sherlock's own window, a case's shared conversation and a proposed change's take any question, about the case or proposal or not, and move the main 221B window as the dock does. Sherlock's own window drives the main window that opened it or, when that one is gone, a main window on its cluster, as its links do. On a case or a proposal the question goes with the asker's window and view; the view actions go to the asker's session only, and only that session may post their results (`POST /api/v1/aiops/cases/{id}/sherlock/ui/{uid}`, `…/proposals/{id}/sherlock/ui/{uid}`). The others who share the conversation see the answer, not the asker's Undo or Show in 221B. Role checks on pages stay as SR-13. | Go `TestCaseSherlockChangesTheAskersViewOnly`, `TestTroubleshootingAProposalCF61CF62` (the result route); vitest `uiWindow.test.ts` (*view actions from a shared conversation*), `CaseTroubleshoot.test.tsx` (*offers an answer's Undo and Show in 221B only to the person who asked it here*); golden `skill.aiops`. |
| SR-20 | **Sherlock hands troubleshooting to the Troubleshooter**: a question about why something fails, is unhealthy, NotReady, Pending, crashing or restarting, an alert's cause, or a request to troubleshoot (the Troubleshoot buttons' too) goes to the Troubleshooter with `hand_over`, with the symptom, the objects and what Sherlock knows; the engine adds the person's question and view (PM-D5). Sherlock passes on what came back as it stands (PM-D2). When the Troubleshooter is off, does not work on the cluster or has no usable provider it is not in Sherlock's list of agents, and only then do Sherlock's instructions hold the troubleshooting manual and the report's parts (PM-D1); its compact form, which hands nothing over, keeps its own short text. Reworded by PM-D1 to PM-D5. | Go `TestSherlockHandsTroubleshootingToTheTroubleshooter`, `TestSherlocksOwnWorkFollowsItsRoster`; golden `agent.sherlock`, `sherlock.own-work`, `sherlock.core-view-changes-off`. |

Asked on November 13, 2026, on the same branch: *when a new docker image is available, add a notice on the Helm drawer page* ([helm-apps.md](helm-apps.md#newer-images), ADR-154).

| ID | Requirement | Acceptance |
|---|---|---|
| HA-21 | **Newer images are looked for.** For each image the newest deployed revision of a release runs (kept from its manifest with the release's summary), the leader asks the image's registry over the OCI distribution API for its tags, anonymously (a Bearer challenge's token is asked for without credentials; no pull secret is read), and picks the newest tag of the running tag's form: the same prefix, as many numbers, the same suffix but for its last number (`1.2.3-alpine` is compared with `1.2.9-alpine`, `1.25.3-debian-12-r4` with `1.25.4-debian-12-r0`, never with `-debian-11-`); pre-releases only when a pre-release runs; `latest`, hashes and dates are not versions. A tag that is not a version but is pinned by digest is newer when the tag points at another digest now; `latest` without a digest, and a digest alone, are not checked. Answers are kept six hours per repository, a failure one hour; four registries are asked at once, twenty seconds each; a request never waits for a registry; a standby asks none. | Go `TestNewestPicksTheNewerTagOfTheSameForm`, `TestPreReleasesAreComparedWhenOneRuns`, `TestTagsThroughAnAnonymousToken`, `TestACheckDoesNotBlockAndIsKept`, `TestATagThatMovedIsNewer`, `TestAPrivateRepositoryIsUnknown`, `TestAnUnreachableRegistryIsUnknownAndAskedAgainLater`, `TestAStandbyAsksNothing` (imagecheck), `TestAReleaseKeepsItsImages` (kube). |
| HA-22 | **The notice.** `GET helm/newer-images` lists, for each release the person may see (Secrets in its namespace), the images with a newer one. The release's panel opens with *A newer image is available: ghcr.io/acme/api:1.2.4 (running 1.2.3)*, the others after it, and a link to the Images tab, whose Tag column says *1.2.4 available*, *checking…* or, quietly with the reason in its tip, *could not check*. The table's App version cell carries a *newer image* mark, and the export a Newer images column. A registry that cannot be reached shows nothing anywhere but that quiet note. | Go `TestNewerImagesOfTheReleases` (api); vitest `NewerImages.test.tsx`. |
| HA-23 | **A setting.** Administration › Settings › General › Newer images, *Check* (the default) or *Off*, for everyone; off, no registry is asked and nothing is shown. | `TestNewerImagesOfTheReleases`; `settings_test.go`. |

### Searching Mycroft, and a wiki of hundreds of pages

Asked for on November 13, 2026: *Need ability to search in the knowledge base and across known issues and exceptions* and *Need to support Wiki with 100s of entries*. The rows continue the knowledge rows (KN-01 to KN-34).

| ID | Requirement | Acceptance |
|---|---|---|
| KN-35 | **One search across Mycroft.** A box beside AIOps › Mycroft's sub-tabs searches the wiki's approved pages (never a retired one), the sources' excerpts (each source once, by its best excerpt), the known issues, the fixes and the accepted exceptions together (`GET /api/v1/aiops/mycroft/search?q=&kind=&limit=`), ranked in one list by the wiki index's BM25 (KN-11), the records scored among the index's documents by their id, title and text; equal scores list pages, known issues, fixes, exceptions, then excerpts. Each kind is searched as its own screen lets the person read it: pages and excerpts for who reads the wiki; known issues as the issue service lists them to them (drafts, resolved and inactive ones for an operator only); fixes for who reads the cases; exceptions only for who may read them in Mycroft › Exceptions, as `ask_knowledge` decides (KN-32), a kind the person may not read being neither listed nor counted. Records are scrubbed (X-01) before they are searched. Each hit shows its kind, its id or path, its title, status, date and freshness, and a one-line snippet around its first matched word with the words matched marked; chips narrow the list to one kind and count each kind. The search runs after a pause in typing; ↓ ↑ move through the hits, Enter opens one (a page, a known issue, a fix or an exception in its sub-tab; an excerpt's source in a new tab), Escape clears the box. While the box holds a query its results take the sub-tab's place; opening a hit or picking a sub-tab clears it. | Go `TestFindRanksEveryKindTogether`, `TestSnippetIsAroundTheFirstMatch` (knowledge), `TestMycroftSearchHonoursEachKindsReaders`, `TestMycroftSearchNothingToSearch` (api); vitest `MycroftSearch.test.tsx`, `searches every sub-tab from one box…` in `MycroftPage.test.tsx`. |
| KN-36 | **A wiki of hundreds of pages.** The page list (`GET /api/v1/knowledge/pages`, without bodies) filters as it is typed by every word of a page's title or path, sorts by title or by last update, shows the stale pages alone, and groups the pages by kind in groups that fold: with more than 60 pages only the group of the page open starts unfolded, each group shows its first 50 pages until *Show all*, and the list scrolls on its own; the retired pages keep their filter (KN-24). The full-text search is KN-35's, which replaces the Wiki's own search box. `ask_knowledge` still finds the page a question is about among the first three with 500 pages, each query in well under 50 ms. | Go `TestTheIndexScalesToHundredsOfPages` (500 pages: index built in about 40 ms, a query about 1.3 ms on the build machine); vitest `filters the page list by title or path…`, `keeps hundreds of pages usable…` in `KnowledgeTab.test.tsx`. |
| KN-37 | **The ConfigMap store's room is said.** The temporary ConfigMap wiki store (CF-57) keeps every page, excerpt and staged change in one key of at most 900 KiB (`knowledge.DefaultWikiBytes`, below a ConfigMap's 1 MiB): a few hundred pages of a few KiB, fewer with excerpts. `GET /api/v1/knowledge/wiki` gives its use (`usage`: bytes used and the limit) when the wiki is kept there; from 80% the Wiki says it is nearly full, from 97% that it is full and that new pages and changes are refused, and points to a git repository or an S3 bucket, with *Move the wiki to Git or S3* for a SuperAdmin. The limit is unchanged: a ConfigMap cannot hold more. | vitest `says when the ConfigMap store is nearly full…` in `KnowledgeTab.test.tsx`. |

### Requests from use (FU)

Asked for on November 13, 2026, on the same branch: changes to screens people use every day. FU-04 and FU-05 were asked for the same day, for the Run dialog's parameters, and FU-06 after them, for how a bound variable and a constant read; FU-07 then, for what an empty box sends.

| ID | Requirement | Acceptance |
|---|---|---|
| FU-01 | **An agent's model is a dropdown of its provider's models.** The agent drawer lists the models of the provider picked as it opens and again whenever another provider is picked, each with its context window when the endpoint says; while it lists, the dropdown says so. A model saved before that the list lacks stays chosen, marked *not listed by the provider*. A model is typed by hand only when the provider's models cannot be listed, and the drawer says why. The provider dialog keeps *Another model…*. | `AgentsSection.test.tsx`: *lists the provider's models as the drawer opens, again when the provider changes…*. |
| FU-02 | **The Inbox says who raised each row, in a From column**: the reporter of a reported case, the person who opened one by hand, the agent that found one with its job, the alert one was opened from; the agent and job of a proposed change (or the chat it came from); who asked for an approval or a page request; Mycroft for wiki changes; the housekeeping for a suggested rule; Confluence for an edited page; for a known issue its source, or the operator who wrote it. The column sorts, is in the Columns menu and the export (*From*), the search finds its names, and a *From* filter (`?from=`) narrows to one. A known issue keeps who wrote it (`createdBy`, operators only). | `inboxRows.test.ts`, `Inbox.test.tsx` (*says who raised each row in the From column…*); `TestAKnownIssueKeepsWhoWroteItFU02`. |
| FU-03 | **Run parameters are typed with suggestions, by their kind.** Under its name each parameter says its kind (*Variable* or *Secret*, the two kinds a `# 221b:param` line declares), whether it is required, and the script's description. While one is typed the field offers, as a combobox (arrows, Enter, Escape): for a variable, the value that applies to the first node of the run and its fleet, the values fleets and nodes set for it and this script's earlier runs' values; for a secret, `secret:namespace/name/key` for every key of the Secrets the person may list (`GET …/automation/secret-refs`: names and keys, never a value, X-01), and why none are offered when they cannot be listed. The script drawer's header names each parameter's kind too. | `paramSuggest.test.ts`, `AutomationTab.test.tsx` (*says each parameter's kind…*, *offers a secret parameter Secrets by name and key only…*); `TestTheRunDialogIsOfferedSecretNamesAndKeysOnlyFU03`. |
| FU-04 | **A run parameter is filled, searched and bound by name.** In the Run dialog a variable parameter's box shows the value every node of the run gets, when they all get the same one; where they differ it stays empty and says *varies by node (N values)*. Left so, nothing is sent and each node keeps inheriting its own value; the box follows the fleet and node choice, and never overwrites what was typed. Typed, the box searches every variable a fleet or a node of the cluster sets, and this script's earlier runs' values, by name and by value, case aside; each suggestion shows the variable's name, its value on the run's nodes (or *varies: N values*) and where it is set; a variable marked sensitive, named like a secret (PASSWORD, TOKEN, SECRET, KEY…) or holding a Secret's reference or a key shows its name alone, and its values are not searched. Picking one binds the parameter to it: the run value `var:NAME` gives each node its own value of NAME when the run starts (ADR-156), shown as a *variable NAME* chip with its values, which clears; the nodes without it are named, a required parameter is then refused for them (*required parameters have no value: node: P (bound to variable NAME, not set here)*), and a variable no node of the run has is refused whether or not the parameter is required. Anything typed is a constant, the same on every node, and never binds: a typed text that starts with `var:` is sent as `\var:…`, and the server drops one backslash. A run keeps `var:NAME` in its own values and each node's resolved value; its details say *from variable NAME*. A secret parameter is unchanged. | Go `TestABindingGivesEachNodeItsOwnValueOfTheVariable`, `TestABindingSomeNodesLackIsMissingWhereRequiredAndEmptyWhereNot`, `TestABindingNoNodeHasStopsTheRun`, `TestAConstantNeverBinds`, `TestABindingNoTargetHasIsRefused`, `TestARequiredParameterBoundToAMissingVariableIsRefused`; vitest `paramSuggest.test.ts`, `AutomationTab.test.tsx` (*fills it when every node gets the same…*, *leaves a parameter empty where nodes differ…*, *binds to another variable…*). |
| FU-05 | **On production a run may give its own values, weighed as a variable change.** A real run's own values are weighed as if the fleet's values changed for that run alone: for the script's version and the nodes it reaches, a value that differs from what a node inherits, or a binding to another variable, is a change; the console's rule (sensitive on any of the nodes' fleets, or read by a condition) and then Sherlock decide whether it needs approval, as on the Variables page (AU-20). One that needs none starts at once, and the run's basis says so. One that does becomes a *run request* that waits: it is listed in AIOps › Inbox as *Run with its own values*, with the values and what they touch; a SuperAdmin other than the one who asked approves it, which starts it at once as the person who asked, with the version, nodes, rollout, run profile, hooks and values they gave, every check made again and the values resolved then (when a check fails it keeps waiting and the answer says why); anyone, the asker included, rejects it with a reason. The run records who approved its values. Every step is in the audit log (`automation.run`). The dialog says before Run is pressed which values would wait and why (`POST …/automation/runs/weigh`, the rule alone, nothing recorded), and Run becomes *Ask for approval*. A run without values of its own, and a dry run, are as before. | Go `TestOnProductionValuesThatNeedNoApprovalStartAtOnce`, `TestOnProductionValuesThatNeedApprovalWaitForASecondAdmin`, `TestAWaitingRunIsRejectedWithAReason`, `TestAnApprovedRunIsCheckedAgainWhenItStarts`, `TestAWaitingRunIsDecidedBySomeoneElse`; vitest *says on production which values wait for approval, and asks for it*. |
| FU-05a | **A run request expires, and a local asker is checked again.** A run request nobody decides within the wait set in Administration › Settings › Watson › Approvals (*An Automation run whose own values need approval waits for up to*, 5 minutes to 7 days, an hour by default; `watson.waitingRunMinutes`, changes audited as `watson.settings`) expires: it is marked *Expired* at the time it expired, in AIOps › Inbox › Approvals and the run requests list, it can no longer be approved or rejected (*run request N expired after 1 hour; ask again*), and the expiry is in the audit log (`automation.run`, failed). Approving a request asked for by a local account first checks that the account still exists, is enabled and is a SuperAdmin as the rules stand now; when not, it is refused (409, audited as denied) and keeps waiting until it is rejected or expires. A directory or provider person cannot be read again without their session; the expiry bounds them. Owner decision, 2026-11-18. | Go `TestAWaitingRunExpires` (api and automation), `TestAnApprovalChecksTheLocalAskerAgain`, `TestLocalSuperAdminIsReadAsItStandsNow`, `TestTheRound2SettingsValidate`; vitest *shows the wait as saved, an hour by default, and saves a new one with the approvals*, *keeps a saved wait that is not one of the choices*. |
| FU-06 | **A run parameter starts bound to the variable of its name, and a variable reads apart from a constant.** In the Run dialog a variable parameter whose name a fleet or a node sets for at least one node of the run (All nodes, for a script on the cluster) starts bound to that variable as the dialog opens and as the nodes change: its field holds the chip *variable NAME · N values* (*· its value* when every node has the same one; *value hidden* for a sensitive or secret-looking variable), whose tooltip gives each node's value (or, hidden, where it is set), with ✕ to clear it. That is what each node inherits anyway, so it is not sent: the run's own values stay empty for it and on production nothing is weighed; the nodes without it are named as for a binding picked (FU-04). Only a choice is sent: another variable picked (`var:NAME`), or a constant typed. A change of nodes never undoes a choice. Clearing the chip leaves an empty box, focused, for a constant; a value typed shows a *constant* badge at the field's right (a secret parameter's, *secret ref*). Typing a variable's exact name binds only when it is picked from the list. The chip's text is the field's description, ✕ has its label, the badge is in the field's description. A run's details and a run request in AIOps › Inbox show a binding as the same chip and a constant with the same badge. | vitest `AutomationTab.test.tsx` (*says each parameter's kind, binds it to the variable of its name…*, *stays bound where nodes differ…*, *binds automatically where only some nodes have the variable…*, *binds to another variable…*, *starts a run of the approved version…*), `paramSuggest.test.ts` (*says what a bound variable's chip shows…*), `Inbox.test.tsx`; e2e *a run parameter binds to a variable picked by name, and the run says so*. |
| FU-07 | **An empty parameter says what it sends.** In the Run dialog a variable parameter's empty box holds a chip that says it: *empty string ""* once the person cleared a chip or a value, which the run sends as a value, `NAME=""` on every node, over what nodes and fleets set (its ✕ goes back to the variable of its name, or to not set); *not set* when nothing was chosen and no node inherits a value, so the script gets no `NAME` at all. Environment variables have no null: unset is the nearest. For a required parameter an empty string is no value: the dialog refuses to run (*NAME (an empty string)*) and so does the server. A secret parameter is never sent empty. A run's details and a run request in AIOps › Inbox show a run's empty value as the same *empty string* chip. | Go `TestAnEmptyRunValueIsTheEmptyStringAndNoValueWhenRequired`; e2e *a run parameter binds to a variable picked by name, and the run says so*. |

### Email about cases (EM)

Asked for on November 13, 2026: *Add configuration for email and if configured and passed test send email on the case changes, opening, include a link and ability to see all cases that person is involved in.* ADR-158.

| ID | Requirement | Acceptance |
|---|---|---|
| EM-01 | **Settings › Email** (General's Email section since 0.12.1, EM-11), a SuperAdmin's (`GET`, `PUT /api/v1/settings/email`): the SMTP server (a host name or an IP address, no scheme or port), the port (1 to 65535; 587, 465 or 25 by default), the security (*STARTTLS*, which the server must offer, never falling back to the clear; *TLS* from the start; *none*, only for a relay that needs no sign-in, so it takes no user name or password), the user name, the password and the From address (one address). Every field is one line. The settings are kept in the casework ConfigMap (`cases-mail.json`, 4 KiB); the password in the Secret `<release>-cases` (`mail-password`, in memory with `connections.store=memory`), written and never read back: the page shows *set* or not, and *Forget the password*. No server and no From address turns email off and drops the password. The links start with the console's address of Administration › Settings › Watson's notifications, which the page shows, or says is missing. | Go `TestTheSettingsAreChecked`, `TestSettingsEmailIsASuperAdmins` (an Admin is refused; the password is in no answer); vitest `EmailSettings.test.tsx`. |
| EM-02 | **Email is on only when configured and tested.** *Send test email* sends one email with the settings as saved to the address typed, the SuperAdmin's own by default (`POST /api/v1/settings/email/test`). Email is on only while the last test passed at the settings as they stand: a change of the server, port, security, user name, From address or password turns it off until a test passes again; saving the same settings keeps it on. A test of settings another replica changed meanwhile counts for nothing. | Go `TestEmailIsOnOnlyAfterATestOfTheSettingsAsTheyStand`, `TestATestCountsOnlyForItsSettings`. |
| EM-03 | **The page says the state**: *Not configured*; *Configured, not tested*; *On: tested <when> by <who>, to <address>*; or *The last test … failed: <reason>*, the reason in one line without the password; and how the last email went on the replica. | vitest *says each state…*. |
| EM-04 | **Audited.** Each change of the settings (`mail.settings`, naming the fields changed and *password set* or *removed*, never the password) and each test (`mail.test`, with its outcome and reason). | Go: the audit log has both, and no password. |
| EM-05 | **Who is involved in a case** is read from what the case records (`issues.Involved`): its reporter and the person who filed it for them, who opened it in the console, its assignee, who follows it (joined it with *Add to C-n*, or reported a case closed as its duplicate), who wrote in its conversation (a reply, a question, an internal note, a comment, or sent a reply someone drafted; not a draft), who closed it while it is closed, and who approved a change that fixed it (recorded on the case, `approvers`, when the change is applied). Never a system account, a token or an agent. A person's address is the one the case keeps for them, or their local account's or the directory's (`auth.EmailOf`); a person with none is skipped. | Go `TestWhoIsInvolvedInACase`, `TestTheApproversOfAFixAreInvolved`. |
| EM-06 | **What is emailed**: a case opened (by any way in: the report page, the issue API, New Case, an alert), its status, assignee or priority changed, it closed (fixed, no action, a duplicate, a known issue, an exception) or reopened, a change applied to fix it, and a person's message in its conversation (the reporter's, a reply, a question, an internal note, a comment). Not its title, detail or what it is about, a draft, the agents' analysis or other steps. Each person hears of the changes others made, never of their own; whoever opens a case gets its first email as a receipt. An internal note reaches only the people who work the case (its assignee, who opened, closed or approved, who wrote as an operator), never its reporter. | Go `TestThePeopleInvolvedAreEmailed`. |
| EM-07 | **One email per case and minute, sent in the background.** The changes to one case within a minute of the first are one email to each person, sent from the leader after that minute, never in the request; a send the server refuses is tried again after 30 seconds, 2 and 10 minutes, then audited as failed (`case.mail`, naming the people, not their addresses). Nothing is sent while email is off, and a change made then is not sent later. | Go `TestEmailIsSentOnlyWhenOnAndAgainWhenRefused`. |
| EM-08 | **The email** is plain text with a simple HTML part: `[221B] C-12 opened: <title>` or `… changed: …`, the case's priority, status and assignee, one line per change naming who made it, a link to the case (`<console>/#/aiops/inbox/case/12`) and to every case the person is involved in (`<console>/#/aiops/inbox?show=all&who=involved`), and why they get it. ## What Mycroft draws on (0.12.1)

Asked for on November 17, 2026, in the owner's review of Administration › AI › Knowledge: the Sources table holds only the sources that are read again or looked up (a web address is a source of its own, *Web site*); the uploaded documents and notes are one list of their own; a page of AIOps › Mycroft lists every item the agents can draw on, where a SuperAdmin excludes what should not be read; removing or excluding something says which wiki pages drew on it.

| --- | --- | --- |
| KN-44 | **A web site is a source** (kind `web`, *Web site* in Administration › AI › Knowledge › Add source, Ingest): a name, a start address (http or https), a link depth (0 to 3) and a page limit (1 to 200, 20 by default). Its read crawls the site as an address added in Mycroft was (MY-06): the same site only, breadth first, robots.txt and the pages' `noindex`/`nofollow` respected, loopback, link-local and multicast addresses refused, 5 minutes and 32 MiB a read. It signs in as a live source does: none, a bearer token, or a user with a password or API token (https only), the credential in the Secret `<release>-knowledge`, sent to the site only and never shown; a private CA may be given. It has Turn off, Edit, Remove, its dialog's Test (the pages it would read, KN-26) and its Wiki sync job (`wiki-sync-<id>`, nightly, off until turned on, with Run now, KN-28). Each page is an item by its path and query, its version its text's hash: an unchanged page is not sent to Mycroft again; a page gone leaves the index at the next full read; a read that could not fetch a page, or stopped at its limit, marks nothing gone. Adding a web address in Mycroft (`POST add`, `kind: url`) is refused, pointing here. | Go `TestAWebSiteIsReadAsASource`, `TestWebSiteRefusals`, `TestAWebSiteSignsIn`; vitest of the source drawer's Web site fields. |
| KN-45 | **Exclusions.** A SuperAdmin excludes an item of a web site, a Confluence space, a git repository or an S3 bucket from AIOps › Mycroft › Sources (*Exclude this*, or *Exclude everything under …*: a web path glob such as `/blog/*`, a repository's folder `docs/old/`, a bucket's prefix `archive/`, a Confluence page and its children), with an optional reason (`POST /api/v1/knowledge/sources/{id}/exclusions`). Its excerpts are emptied at once (the manifest marks it gone, its excerpt is emptied, the index is read again) and it joins the source's *Excluded* list with who, when and why, kept with the source's settings (an Edit of the source keeps it). Every read leaves out what is excluded; a web site's crawl does not fetch an excluded page nor follow the links found only on it. *Include again* (`DELETE …/exclusions?pattern=`) takes it off the list; it is read at the next read (the job's next run or Run now). Uploaded documents and notes are removed, not excluded. Nothing is deleted: the page's excerpts stay, quoted and listed under its web site, until the site's first read reads the page again; then the old item is gone, its excerpt emptied, and the wiki's pages that drew on it count the new one. | KN-57 | **A source's trust level** (decided by the owner on November 23, 2026). High, normal or low, set per source by a SuperAdmin on its row of Administration › AI › Knowledge's *Sources* table (since 0.14.0; the *Trust* column, with the product, the version and *Only the versions the clusters run* under the level in the same cell, saved together; the sources of uploads, which are not rows, have their trust under the table; before, a table of the *Search* card) (`PUT /api/v1/knowledge/sources/{id}/index` with `trust`, `product`, `productVersion`, `followsClusterVersions`; `trust` empty is its kind's default: the organisation's own, Confluence spaces, git repositories, uploaded documents and notes, the console's records and the fixes, high; web sites and S3 buckets, where vendor documentation is read, normal), audited `knowledge.trust` with what changed and what it was. It weights its passages' rank in every search (high ×1.5, normal ×1, low ×0.5; an approved page ×2), and it is how Mycroft chooses what to write pages from (KN-59). `ask_knowledge` ranks pages and excerpts together and marks each excerpt *Unreviewed*, with its section and its source's trust, and its preamble asks the agent to prefer a page and to say when it relies on an unreviewed excerpt. | `TestTrustVersionsAndSize`, `TestAskKnowledgeCitesPagesWithTheirDates`; vitest of the Sources table (`KnowledgeSection.test.tsx`) and of `SourceIndexCell`. |
| KN-58 | **No cap on the index: its size per source, and a warning at 80 % of the database's volume** (decided by the owner on November 23, 2026). The Search card lists each source (live ones aside) with what the index holds of it (its trust and what it is about are set in the Sources table since 0.14.0, KN-57): documents, passages and bytes (its passages' text, words and embeddings as stored, without the indexes), and the approved pages apart. It says the database's size (`pg_database_size`) against its volume's, which the chart gives the console (`DB_VOLUME_SIZE`, `-db-volume-size`): the bundled database's `storage.size`, or an external database's `database.external.volumeSize` when set; and warns when the database holds 80 % of it or more. Without a volume's size it says so and gives no warning. | `TestTrustVersionsAndSize`; vitest of the Search card; `hack/check-chart.sh`. |
| KN-59 | **Mycroft writes pages for what matters** (phase-13.1-ai, part B). Every changed item a sync reads is excerpted and indexed; Mycroft is given, to write or update pages from, only an item of a source of high trust (KN-57: by default Confluence spaces, git repositories, uploaded documents and notes, the console's records and the fixes; a SuperAdmin raises or lowers any source; an uploaded document of its own trust by that, KN-61) and a correction (KN-23). The items of the other sources (web sites and S3 buckets by default: vendor documentation) are indexed for the search alone, so Mycroft's cost and the Inbox's approvals no longer grow with them; the sync's report says how many (*N of the changed items were indexed for the search alone*), and the hosts and groups of hosts Mycroft proposes come from the items it was given. **On request**: AIOps › Mycroft › Sources › an item › *Write a page from it* (`POST /api/v1/knowledge/items/page` with `source` and `id`; an Admin's or a SuperAdmin's) has Mycroft write or update pages from that item's excerpts (a long document's sections), whatever its source's trust, as a run that waits for review in AIOps › Inbox; it takes the sync's turn (409 while one runs), records the pages in the manifest, is kept in the syncs' history, and is audited `knowledge.page.request`. A source added with its index settings is audited `knowledge.trust` too. | `TestMycroftWritesPagesForWhatMatters`; vitest of AIOps › Mycroft › Sources. |
| KN-60 | **The search is measured** (phase-13.1-ai, part B; the owner's questions are his to run). `POST /api/v1/knowledge/evaluate` (an Admin's or a SuperAdmin's, at most 500 questions, nothing kept) takes questions, each with the passage that answers it (its source by name or id, or `wiki` for an approved page; the item's path or address, or its end; optionally a section's heading), asks each of keyword, fuzzy and semantic search and of the three ranked together for 6 hits, as `ask_knowledge` does, and gives each question's rank of the passage in each (0: not among them), what the hybrid search found, and for each way how many questions found their passage and the share; it says when the semantic search did not run. `hack/knowledge-eval.py` (`make knowledge-eval CONSOLE=… QUESTIONS=…`) sends a file of questions (JSON Lines, or tab-separated question, source, path and heading) to a console, signed in with a browser's session cookie (`CONSOLE_COOKIE`) or a local account (`CONSOLE_USER`, its password on stdin), and prints the report in Markdown (`--json` keeps it whole). | `TestTheSearchIsMeasured`; the script run against a fake console. |
| KN-61 | **A trust level per uploaded document** (asked by the owner on November 23, 2026). Every upload lands in one source (*Added in Mycroft*; older ones in *Uploaded documents*), so a source's trust (KN-57) was every upload's. An uploaded document has a trust level of its own, its source's by default: a SuperAdmin chooses it at the upload in Administration › AI › Knowledge › *Uploaded documents* (*Trust*: *Source's (High)*, High, Normal, Low; `POST /api/v1/knowledge/add` and `POST /api/v1/knowledge/sources/{id}/documents` with `trust`, `""` the source's; an Admin's upload with `trust` is refused) and changes it on the document's row in AIOps › Mycroft › Sources, whose items of a source of uploaded documents have a *Trust* column (`PUT /api/v1/knowledge/sources/{id}/documents/{name}/trust` with `trust`; a SuperAdmin's, as a source's trust is; an Admin sees it in words). A change is audited `knowledge.trust` with what it was (*Added in Mycroft › vendor.pdf: trust low, was high (its source's)*); the upload's audit says the trust chosen. The search weighs the document's passages, its sections' too, by its own level when it has one (`trustWeight`, and a hit's `trust`), and Mycroft writes pages from it by itself only when that is high (KN-59): at once for the search, from its next read for Mycroft (*Write a page from it* asks meanwhile). A note takes its source's trust. The level is kept on the document's entry of the wiki's manifest (`trust`), as the retirements are, so it travels with the wiki to another store and into a backup, and no schema step is needed; a sync that runs while it is changed keeps the change. An Admin's upload chooses none: a document uploaded again, in either source of uploaded documents, keeps its own, so an Admin cannot raise one a SuperAdmin lowered; a document removed loses its own. | `TestADocumentHasItsOwnTrust`, `TestADocumentsTrustWeighsItsPassages`, `TestASyncKeepsTheTrustSetWhileItRan`; vitest `UploadedDocuments.test.tsx`, `MycroftSources.test.tsx`. |
| MY-12 | **Uploaded documents.** After Sources, Administration › AI › Knowledge has *Uploaded documents* with tabs *Notes* and *Documents*: *Add note* (a title and a text, stored as a Markdown document) and *Upload* (the formats and size as before), each list newest first, 25 and *Show more*, a filter by title or who added it, with its title, who added it, when, its state (*reading*, *read*, or *error* with why) and *Remove*. It lists the notes and documents of both built-in sources together (`GET /api/v1/knowledge/uploads`): nothing is moved, so every page keeps the source it cites; new ones go to *Added in Mycroft*. A note of the same title, or a document of the same name, replaces the one before (a document in either source). Removing (`DELETE /api/v1/knowledge/uploads/{source}/{name}`) is final: it leaves the index at once, and a sync that was running then keeps it removed when it writes the manifest it read at its start (0.13.0; the seventh cluster test's N4), as it keeps retirements and exclusions. Admins add and remove from the same section on their Administration page (`#/admin/settings/knowledge`), SuperAdmins in Administration › AI › Knowledge; a built-in source switched off is said there, with *Turn on* for a SuperAdmin. The old *Uploaded documents* and *Added in Mycroft* cards are gone. | Go `TestUploadedDocumentsAndNotes`, `TestADocumentRemovedDuringASyncStaysRemoved`; vitest `UploadedDocuments.test.tsx`; e2e *a note is added, listed under Notes and removed*. |

A SuperAdmin excludes and includes again there (KN-45); an uploaded document or note is removed there too. It links to Administration › AI › Knowledge for changes to the sources, and replaces the Wiki's *Sources* view: `#/aiops/mycroft/wiki/sources` leads here.
