Pages are never changed by the removal or the exclusion itself. | Go `TestUploadedDocumentsAndNotes`; vitest of the remove and exclude dialogs. |

## Hardening and Helm safety (0.12.1)

| ID | Requirement | Acceptance |
|---|---|---|
| SEC-30 | When requests carry `X-Forwarded-For`, `Forwarded` or `X-Real-IP` from a peer not listed in `auth.trustedProxies`, the console keeps not believing the header, but logs one warning naming the peer (then at most one an hour) and shows SuperAdmins a notice in Administration naming the peer, saying that the sign-in limit per address and the audit log see the proxy's address and that the proxy should be listed under `auth.trustedProxies`. The chart's notes warn when an Ingress is enabled and `auth.trustedProxies` is empty. | A forwarded request from an untrusted peer makes `/api/v1/me` report the peer to a SuperAdmin only, and Administration shows the notice. One from a trusted proxy does not. Repeated requests within an hour log one warning. `helm template` with `ingress.enabled` and no trusted proxies renders the warning in NOTES, and the install still succeeds. |
| HA-40 | Adopting a release as an application seeds the app's values from the release's current user-supplied values; values sent with the first save are deep-merged over them (theirs win). | PUT of a source for an installed release with no record saves `release.Config` (merged with typed values); the record has `adoptedRevision`; the audit has `values_from_revision`. |
| HA-41 | App values reach the page with credentials masked; a masked placeholder saved back keeps the stored value, never the literal placeholder. | GET/PUT answers contain no secret and list `valuesMasked`; a save with an unknown placeholder is a 400 and changes nothing. |
| HA-42 | Every Helm upgrade card lists, by key only, the release values it would remove or change (at most 20 and "and N more"); removing any gives the card a warning tone. | Card impact has "Removes N values …" / "Changes N values …" with no values; `plan.tone=warn`. |
| HA-43 | Cards for the console's own release (`SELF_RELEASE`) say "This is the release that runs this console: if it fails, the console goes down." with a danger tone, and the panel shows a callout; an upgrade of it that removes values is refused at approval (durable cards too). | `plan.alert` and `plan.tone=danger`; Approve refuses with rule "own release"; the panel shows `app-self`. |
| SEC-20 | A diff of an application against its source renders the chart without reading the cluster (`lookup` finds nothing) and shows no credential in any object: values under credential-looking names, environment variables named like credentials, and values of the manifest's Secrets are masked wherever they appear; Secrets show sizes only. Comparing with the source and listing its refs need the role, plus list and create Secrets in the namespace. | A chart whose ConfigMap copies a Secret through `lookup` shows no part of the value in the diff, plain or base64; a person who may list but not create Secrets gets 403 from `diff?to=source` and `refs`. |
| SEC-21 | Every render of a chart before approval (card impact, Watson dry run, Apps diff, source check on save, the check before a deploy) runs in a child process of the same binary, with an address-space ceiling, a CPU-time ceiling and a 60 s kill. At most two run at once, output is capped at 32 MB, and the loop budget still applies. | A template that doubles a string 40 times is refused with "used more memory or time than allowed" and the console keeps serving; an endless template is killed at the timeout and its slot is free right after; an ordinary chart renders the same as before. |
| SEC-01 | A proposed change asked for in a chat reads its targets and impact as the requester; only its fingerprint is read with the console's account. | A namespace-limited Admin's card for a Secret's annotation outside their namespace shows no value; the fingerprint equals the console's. |
| SEC-02 | from="Sherlock" makes a durable card only for a command in one of Sherlock's answers in the caller's own conversation on that cluster. | A from="Sherlock" command with no such answer makes no proposal and is labelled Typed. |
| SEC-03 | Watson's helm install/upgrade with --git or a repository chart, dry runs included, needs the role and Kubernetes rights to manage applications in the namespace. | A Viewer's `helm install --git … --dry-run=client` is refused; an Admin is refused in a namespace they may not manage. |
| SEC-04 | Network-level fetch failures read "the repository could not be fetched"; the cause is logged; authentication and not-found messages stay. | A refused port and a TLS failure give identical messages; a 401 and a 404 keep theirs. |
| SEC-10 | `apps.allowedHosts` (default empty = no limit) lists hosts, `*.domain` and an optional `:port` for git, Helm and OCI sources. | The chart renders APPS_ALLOWED_HOSTS; a bad entry stops the start. |
| SEC-11 | With `apps.allowedHosts` set, other hosts are refused when saved and at connect time, redirects included. | Saving an unlisted host is refused; a git or Helm fetch to it and a redirect to it are refused; wildcard and port rules work. |

## Credentials (0.12.1)

Agreed with the owner on November 17, 2026: *Agree. You will need to update all related pages — Helm Setup, Knowledge, probably others.* A credential is who the console is when it signs in somewhere, and where that applies; the connection's details (bucket, region, branch, path, chart, space) stay with what uses it. Model providers' API keys, the SMTP password, the notification channels' webhooks and the issue webhook are not credentials of this page yet.

| ID | Requirement | Done when |
| --- | --- | --- |
| CR-01 | **Administration › Credentials** (`#/admin/credentials`, a tab of Administration of its own since CU-18; `#/admin/settings/credentials` leads there; not *Secrets*, which is the cluster's view), with a one-line description, lists every credential: name, kind, endpoint, description, how it is sent, its expiry (a pill), *Used by*, its last test and who changed it when. A SuperAdmin adds, changes, tests and removes one in a drawer with its own Save. Sherlock's `view_admin` opens it as page `credentials`. | vitest `CredentialsSettings.test.tsx`, `uiControl.test.ts`; Go `TestViewAdminDescribesThePagesTheBrowserOpens`, the golden prompts; e2e `credentials.spec.ts`. |
| CR-02 | **Kinds**: *Token*; *User + password/token*; *Access key* (key ID, secret, optional session token, optional region); *SSH key* (private key, optional passphrase, and the server's host key, required: never trusted on first use; written like the secret and never read back, the page showing its type, SHA-256 fingerprint and the key cut short, *ssh-ed25519 AAAAC3NzaC1l…Qx7v9a2E*; a change without it keeps it, a new SSH key or one of another kind made one needs it); *GitHub App* (app ID, installation ID, private key: the console signs a JWT with it and asks GitHub's API for the installation's token, kept until five minutes before it expires; `api.github.com` for github.com, `https://HOST/api/v3` for GitHub Enterprise Server); *Client certificate* (certificate and key, mTLS); *OAuth2 client credentials* (token URL, client ID and secret, scope: the bearer is fetched and kept until shortly before it expires); *None / pod identity*. Any kind may have a private CA. | Go `TestGitHubAppMintsAnInstallationToken`, `TestOAuth2ClientCredentials`, `TestAnSSHKeyNeedsItsHostKey`, `TestAnAccessKeySignsTheBucket`, `TestTheHostKeyIsShownByItsFingerprint`; vitest *shows an SSH key’s host key by its fingerprint…*, *needs the host key of a new SSH key*. |
| CR-03 | **Send as** (a token, a GitHub App's or an OAuth2 client's): Bearer; Basic with a user (x-access-token for GitHub, oauth2 for GitLab); a custom header with an optional prefix (X-API-Key, PRIVATE-TOKEN, `Authorization: token …`); a query parameter, with a warning on the page that it lands in logs; a cookie. A user and password is always Basic. | Go `TestEachWayOfSending`; vitest *adds a token sent in a custom header…*. |
| CR-04 | **The endpoint scopes every use.** An endpoint is an address or the start of addresses (`https://github.com/org`, `github.com/org`, `oci://ghcr.io/org`, `ssh://git@host/org`, `git@host:org`; a bucket's is its store's address and the bucket, `https://s3.amazonaws.com/BUCKET`, not `s3://`); a credential is sent only to an address on the same host and port, under its path, and with an endpoint without a scheme never over plain http. Neither an endpoint nor an address under it has a `.` or `..` segment or an encoded `/` or `\` (`%2e%2e`, `%2F`, `%5C`). Every use resolves the credential for its address on the server and is refused outside it; every HTTP request it signs is checked again: a crawl's links, a redirect (whose copied headers are dropped) and a Helm repository's files are signed only under the endpoint, a live source's client certificate goes only to the addresses it covers, and a credential sent as a query parameter is never in an address the console keeps, shows or gives a model. Two exceptions, named on the page: an OCI registry's token service named by the registry's own challenge gets the registry's credential, as Helm does; and an OAuth2 client's secret goes to its token URL (https only, the console's guarded connections, no redirect followed), whose host the list shows beside the endpoint. | Go `TestTheEndpointScopesEveryUse`, `TestDotSegmentsAndEncodedSeparatorsAreUnderNoEndpoint`, `TestABucketsEndpointIsItsStoresAddress`, `TestTheClientSecretGoesToTheTokenURLOnly`, `TestRepositoriesAreKept`, `TestAHelmCredentialSignsOnlyUnderItsEndpoint`, `TestASourceReadsWithItsCredentialCR11`, `TestAQueryCredentialIsNeverInAnAddress`, `TestARedirectIsSignedOnlyUnderTheEndpoint`, `TestALiveSourcesClientCertificateGoesOnlyUnderItsEndpoint`; vitest *says where an OAuth2 client’s secret goes besides its endpoint*. |
| CR-05 | **The secret part is written once.** It is kept in the Secret `<release>-credentials` (in memory when the console keeps its connections in memory), never in an answer, a log, the audit log or a prompt; the page shows which parts are set, and a drawer's secret fields start empty (*kept; type to replace*). A secret replaced is what every use sends from then on. The Secret is kept under 900 KiB (Kubernetes holds 1 MiB in one): a change that would grow it past that is refused with why; a removed credential's key leaves it. The Secret also holds the list of the credentials, everything but their secrets, under the key `index`; with a database that list is rows of `credentials` and the Secret holds the secrets alone (DB-60, DB-61). | Go `TestASecretNeverLeavesTheService`, `TestCredentialsAPIForSuperAdminsAndOthers`, `TestTheCredentialsSecretStaysUnderItsBound`; vitest *edits without sending the kept secret…*. |
| CR-06 | **Who does what.** SuperAdmins create, edit, test and remove, and see what uses each (*Used by*, here and in Settings › Repositories: it names applications in namespaces others may not open); anyone signed in reads the names, kinds and endpoints (the pickers', unchanged), without *Used by*. A credential in use is not removed: the refusal (409) names what uses it. Changes are audited (`credential.save`, `credential.remove`, `credential.test`) with the kind, endpoint and how it is sent, never a value. | Go `TestCredentialsAPIForSuperAdminsAndOthers`, `TestGitRepositoriesInSettingsRepositories`, `TestACredentialInUseIsNotRemoved`; vitest *shows others the names, kinds and endpoints, without Used by*, *shows others the repositories without Used by*; e2e `credentials.spec.ts`. |
| CR-07 | **Test** against the endpoint, or an address, repository (`name.git`, its `info/refs`), bucket or SSH host under it, typed in the test box: it says how the credential was sent (never its value), what the far end answered, and after a 401 the way of sending its `WWW-Authenticate` asks for. An access key lists one key of the bucket (SigV4); an SSH key checks the host key and signs in. The result is kept as the last test. | Go `TestEachWayOfSending`, `TestAnAccessKeySignsTheBucket`. |
| CR-08 | **Expiry.** A credential may have an expiry date: from 14 days before it, the row has a pill (*Expires in 5 days*, *Expired*), the page a line naming how many, and Settings' list says it under Credentials. | vitest *lists the credentials with their uses, expiry…*, *says an expiry in words*. |
| CR-09 | **Each consumer takes what it supports**: a web site and a live source any way of sending, and a client certificate; Confluence Cloud Basic (the account's email and an API token), Data Center Bearer; git over HTTPS Basic or Bearer; a bucket an access key (SigV4) or the pod's identity; a Helm repository or an OCI registry a user and password, or a token sent as Basic or Bearer. Git over SSH (an SSH key) waits for SSH sources. | Go `TestCredentialsAPIForSuperAdminsAndOthers` (choices), `TestASourceReadsWithItsCredentialCR11`. |
| CR-10 | **A picker** in each consumer offers, by name, the credentials that serve it and cover its address (`GET /api/v1/credentials/choices?use=&address=`): the only one is preselected while nothing is chosen; among several the person must choose (Save waits); *None (public)* is always there. A consumer keeps the credential's id, never its secret. | vitest `CredentialsSettings.test.tsx` (CredentialPicker), `AppDialogCredential.test.tsx`, `HelmReposSettings.test.tsx`, `KnowledgeSection.test.tsx`. |
| CR-11 | **The consumers name a credential**: the Helm tab's new application and a release's Setup card (its git repository; `credential` replaces `token` and `clearToken` in `POST`, `PUT /api/v1/apps…`, `apps/refs` and `apps/inspect`; moved to a repository its credential does not cover, the credential is let go and `credentialRemoved` says so); Settings › Applications' repositories (name, kind, address and credential, in place of their own user, password or token); Administration › AI › Knowledge's Confluence spaces, git repositories, buckets, web sites and live sources (in place of their own token, access key and sign-in). Watson's typed `helm install|upgrade --git URL` uses `--credential NAME` when given, else the application's own when a record is kept for that repository, else the one credential that covers it, and refuses when several do, asking which. | Go `TestMovingToAnotherRepositoryLetsItsCredentialGo`, `TestHelmRepositoriesAndAnApplicationFromOne`, `TestAGitSourcesCredentialIsChosenNotGuessed`; e2e `helm-repos.spec.ts`, `knowledge.spec.ts`, `credentials.spec.ts`. |
| CR-12 | **The upgrade moves the secrets kept before.** On the leader, at start and every quarter of an hour after (so a backup of before 0.12.1 restored moves too), each application's repository token, each Helm repository's password or token and each knowledge source's token or access key becomes a credential: one per distinct secret, way of sending and endpoint (an application's or a git source's endpoint is its repository's host and owner, `https://github.com/org`; a repository's, its address; a bucket's, `ENDPOINT/BUCKET`; a site's, its scheme and host); identical ones are merged; each named after its endpoint and what it came from (*github.com/ucesys (from app 221b/k8s-console)*), its description naming every use, or the first and how many more, within the bounds a change keeps. The consumer is switched to it and marked so (`credMigrated`), never taken again; its old secret stays where it was, no longer read. A secret that cannot be a credential (a live source's addresses on several sites) is left as it was and said. Administration › Credentials lists what was made, *Created from existing settings*, until dismissed. | Go `TestTheUpgradeMergesAndRunsOnce`, `TestTheUpgradeMovesApplicationsAndRepositories`, `TestTheUpgradeMovesTheSourcesCredentialsCR12`, `TestALegacyRepositoryIsSwitched`, `TestTheUpgradesNamesAndDescriptionsAreBounded`. |
| CR-13 | **Backups** hold the credentials (part `credentials`, before the applications, repositories and sources that name them): each secret sealed with the install's backup key, or a placeholder, never in clear. A restore adds the credentials missing by id (one whose name another credential has comes back under its id as *NAME (restored)*, *NAME (restored 2)* when that is taken too, said in the restore's notes; the copy at the upgrade to 0.13 says *NAME (copied at upgrade)* instead, DB-61; so what the backup restores with it still names it); one whose secret the key does not open comes back without it, listed to give again, and refuses to be used until it is. A backup of before 0.12.1 restores the consumers' own secrets as then, which the upgrade then moves. | Go `TestTheCredentialsBackup`, `TestARestoredCredentialKeepsItsIDUnderAnotherName`, `TestHelmRepositoriesBackupWithTheKey`. |
| CR-14 | **No per-application token any more.** The Helm tab's dialogs and Setup card have no token field; a record of before 0.12.1 keeps reading its own token until the upgrade moves it, and says so. | vitest `AppDialogRepository.test.tsx`; e2e `helm-repos.spec.ts`. |
| CR-15 | **Test shows an untrusted certificate.** A Test of an `https://` address (a credential's in Administration › Credentials; a source's in Administration › AI › Knowledge, Confluence, git, S3, web site and live source alike; the wiki store's; a repository's in Administration › Settings › Repositories) whose certificate is not trusted shows what it presented: subject, issuer (*self-signed* when so), validity, the names it is for and its SHA-256 fingerprint, and *Trust this certificate* when it fails only because its CA is not trusted (an unknown CA, or self-signed). A certificate of a trusted CA that fails for its name or dates is shown but not offered. The same Test of a pinned certificate shows it, and warns when it has expired. | Go `TestUntrustedIsShownAndTrustable`, `TestPrivateCAUnchangedAndNameMismatchNotTrustable`, `TestTrustThisCertificate`, `TestTrustingASourcesCertificate`, `TestTrustingTheStoresCertificate`, `TestTrustingARepositorysCertificate`; vitest `CertificateTrust.test.tsx`, `CredentialsSettings.test.tsx` *shows an untrusted certificate, trusts it and forgets it*. |
| CR-16 | **Trust this certificate** (SuperAdmins; `POST /api/v1/credentials/{id}/trust`, `POST /api/v1/knowledge/sources/trust`, `POST /api/v1/knowledge/store/trust`, `POST /api/v1/helm-repositories/{name}/trust`, each with the `sha256` the Test showed) looks at the address again and pins the certificate only when it presents that one now and it fails only for its CA: a fingerprint the server did not just see is refused (409). The pin (`pinnedCert`: fingerprint, subject, issuer, expiry, who, when) is kept with the credential, or with the source, the repository or the wiki store when it names none (a source without a credential is saved first); an edit keeps it while the address's host stays the same, and the page cannot set one. Audited (`credential.trust`, `knowledge.trust`, `helmrepo.trust`) with who, the address and the fingerprint; a refusal too. | Go `TestTrustThisCertificate`, `TestASourcesCredentialKeepsThePin`, `TestTrustingACertificateThroughTheAPI`, `TestTrustingARepositorysCertificateThroughTheAPI`; vitest `HelmReposSettings.test.tsx`, `KnowledgeSection.test.tsx` *trusts the certificate a saved source presents*. |
| CR-17 | **What a pin accepts.** Every HTTPS client the console builds for these (credential tests and token minting, Confluence, S3, web sites and live sources, git fetches and pushes of sources, the wiki and applications, Helm repositories and OCI registries) takes its TLS from one helper (`certpin`): normal verification first (the system's CAs and the private CA, the name, the dates); when it fails, the connection is accepted only when the leaf certificate is exactly the pinned one, by its SHA-256 fingerprint, whatever its chain, names or dates (an expired pinned certificate is accepted, and its Test warns). An address given by IP sends no name, so there only the pinned certificate is accepted. There is no switch that skips verification; the private CA works as before, without a pin. | Go `TestPinAcceptsExactlyThatCertificate`, `TestExpiredPinnedCertificateIsAcceptedAndShownExpired`, `TestPinnedByNameStillVerifiesNormally`, `TestContextTransport`, `TestAGitFetchCarriesThePin`, `TestThePrivateCAIsUnchangedByPinning`. |
| CR-18 | **A changed certificate fails visibly**: any other certificate is refused with *the certificate changed (was …, now …)*, in the Test and in the reads' errors, and the Test shows the new one with *Trust this certificate*. Never silent. | Go `TestPinAcceptsExactlyThatCertificate`, `TestTrustThisCertificate`, `TestTrustingASourcesCertificate`; vitest `CertificateTrust.test.tsx` *says a pinned certificate changed*. |
| CR-19 | **Pinned certificate note.** The credential's drawer, the source's drawer, the repository's editor and the wiki store's card show the pinned certificate (its fingerprint, what keeps it, who trusted it when) with *Forget* for a SuperAdmin (`DELETE …/trust`), after which the address is verified normally again; forgetting a source's or repository's pin forgets its credential's when the pin is the credential's. ## Phase 13: Storage in PostgreSQL

Agreed with the owner on November 18, 2026. The ConfigMaps do not last: each holds 1 MiB, is read and written whole, and every record set has a cap of its own (#46). The console keeps its records in PostgreSQL instead, in a proper data model ([docs/data-model.md](data-model.md): 73 tables, eleven domains, three tiers, as the model was agreed; the schema after step `0038` has 121 tables in 12 domains, the count the page gives at its last step, as of November 20, 2026), not one table of keys and values. The owner's words on the pieces: *"lets go with Patroni as an option … single instance with no ha by default, ha is controlled by an option in values"*; *"we also need an ability to configure external postgresql"*; and, of the code, *"creating an abstraction layer for accessing rdbms"*: a repository per domain. The move was made a domain at a time. **Since 0.13.0 the database is the only store** (DB-69, the owner, November 20, 2026: *"we don't need an ability to run with configmaps"*): every console has one, so where a requirement below says *with a database* it always holds now, and what a row says of a console without one describes a release before 0.13, which the one-time copies read from. How the database is run is in [docs/database.md](database.md).

| ID | Requirement | Done when |
| --- | --- | --- |
| DB-01 | **The database is named by flags, and there is always one** (DB-69). `-db-host` (`DB_HOST`) and the other `-db-*` flags name the PostgreSQL the console keeps its records in; a console given no host does not start, and says what to set (DB-02). Package `internal/db` is the only one that imports the driver. *Until November 20, 2026 the database was optional, and with no host every record stayed in its ConfigMap.* | Go `TestConfigValidate`, `TestAConsoleGivenNoDatabaseDoesNotStart`. |
| DB-02 | **A console given no database does not start** (DB-69): it stops at once, before it has made anything that keeps records, with a sentence that names `-db-host` and the chart's `database.mode`. **A console given a database waits for it at start**: it tries for two minutes. When the database has answered, the schema is brought up to date and the copies are made before anything listens. When it has not, the console exits with why and the pod starts again: a console does not start without its database, and there is no option that lets it (the owner, November 20, 2026; DB-67 is withdrawn). Nothing listens while the start waits, and a start that gave up has written nothing to the cluster and made no table. A database whose settings cannot work is refused at once. | `cmd/221b/database.go`; `TestAConsoleGivenNoDatabaseDoesNotStart`, `TestAConsoleNeverStartsWithoutItsDatabase` (a database that refuses connections, and one that swallows what it is sent), `TestTheStartAfterOneThatGaveUpIsAFirstStart`, `TestAStartThatGaveUpLeavesTheRecordsAsTheyWere`, `TestAReplicaWithItsDatabaseIsUnaffectedByOneThatDoesNotStart`; kind test to add (handover). |
| DB-03 | **PostgreSQL 16 or later**, checked at start; `track_commit_timestamp` off is a warning. | `db.Open`; `db.MinServerVersion`. |
| DB-04 | **The password is read from a file at every connection**, so a rotated Secret needs no restart; it is never in a flag, a log or the connection string. | `db.Config.PasswordFile`; Go `dbtest` with `TEST_DATABASE_PASSWORD`. |
| DB-05 | **TLS as libpq means it**: `verify-full` (the default), `verify-ca`, `require`, `disable`; a CA file; a client certificate and key together. | Go `TestConfigValidate`. |
| DB-06 | **The sessions that must stay one session use a direct address**: the listener, the advisory locks and the migrations go to `-db-listener-host` when the main address is a pooler in transaction mode. | `db.Config.ListenerHost`; `DB.direct`. |
| DB-07 | **The schema is brought up to date at start**, from steps embedded in the binary, numbered without gaps, each applied once in a transaction behind one advisory lock, with its checksum kept; a step applied from a different file is refused, with both checksums and what to do; no step is edited once written down (`testdata/steps.sum`), a change being a new step; a schema newer than the build's is left alone. | Go `TestMigrate`, `TestMigrateTogether`, `TestMigrationsAreNumberedAndNamed`, `TestAppliedStepsAreNotEdited`, `TestADatabaseFromAnotherBuildOfTheSchemaIsRefused`. |
| DB-08 | **The schema is the data model**: its 120 tables (the model's 73, the scripts' 3 lists, a variable change's impacts, a run's approvers, a run request's impacts, a provider's headers, the 5 of the agents' instructions and tests, the 3 of a job run's facts and proposals, the 10 of the checks, rule suggestions, what the runs found, the suggestions dismissed and the runs counted, the 5 of a proposed change's evidence, plans, results, decisions and commands, the 2 of the conversations Sherlock keeps, which replace the model's 1, the 11 of Mycroft's sources, syncs and runs of page changes, and a net 6 more of Cases), every key named for its table, no bare `id`, the keys between domains, the view `script_run_host_values`. | Go `TestMigrate` (73 tables, no `id` column). |
| DB-09 | **Keys are UUIDv7** made by the console, never a sequence, except the model's natural keys. | Go `TestNewIDSortsByTime`. |
| DB-10 | **Ledgers are only added to**: an `UPDATE` of `audit_events` or `ai_spend` is refused by the database; retention may delete. | Go `TestLedgersAreAppendOnly`. |
| DB-11 | **A sweep runs on one replica at a time**, behind an advisory lock of its name that a dead replica never keeps. | Go `TestSweepRunsOnOneReplica`. |
| DB-12 | **A change is announced and looked for anyway.** `NOTIFY` on commit reaches every replica's listener; each also looks every poll (30 seconds) and when its listener reconnects. A rolled back notice is not delivered. | Go `TestNotifyAndPoll`. |
| DB-13 | **A scheduled time is claimed by one replica**, by inserting its row. | Go `TestClaimOnce`, `TestRepoRuns`. |
| DB-14 | **While the database is away** a call returns `db.ErrUnavailable`, the health says since when and why, the pool reconnects by itself, and what waited is told when it is back. The console stays ready. **Nothing a request does waits for a database that is away** (the ninth cluster test's F3 and F4: with the database's pods gone, where a connection is not refused but times out, `/metrics` gave no answer in 90 seconds, the home page none in 120, and each page that needs the database took 5 to 19 seconds to say so, at every click). The rule, in one place (`internal/db/away.go`): (1) every call looks at the health first, and while it says *away* returns `db.ErrUnavailable` at once, without taking a connection: a read, a transaction, a snapshot, a lock, a sweep, the claims, the schema's version, a copy's record. (2) **Only a probe changes the health**: a connection of the console's own, made, signed in and closed, given 2 seconds; it does not go through the pool, since a pool whose connections are all waiting says nothing of the database. (3) A call that does not reach the database (a connection refused or broken, the call's own time run out), a call that has waited 2 seconds (and every 2 seconds for as long as it waits), the listener's connection lost, and the check made every 10 seconds while nobody uses the console, each **ask for a probe**; none of them marks the database away by itself. So **a slow query on a database that answers** costs that query its own time and one probe, which is answered: the health does not change and no other call is refused or held; a probe's answer stands for 1 second (3 for a call that is merely slow), so a run of slow queries makes no run of probes. A server that takes no more connections (`53300`) and a name that does not resolve are no verdict: the connections the console holds may work as ever, and are left. A server that refuses the console's sign-in (a password changed under it, a database that is not there) is away, to the console, and the health says so without the server's sentence, which names the user. (4) When a probe fails, the database is away: **every connection the console has open is closed and every one being made is given up**, so the calls that were already waiting return at once, whatever time they were given, and those given none; no connection is made until the database is back. (5) While it is away the probe is made again every second, by the console and not by a request, and the first that is answered brings the database back: the calls go through, the pools make new connections, the listener listens again at once (and has every subscriber look again), and what waited is told (`db.OnAvailable`: the sessions ended meanwhile; the audit log's queue and the sweeps try again on their own timers). **What it costs:** a database that refuses connections is found away in milliseconds, usually by the listener before any request; one that swallows what it is sent holds **the first requests to meet it for up to 4 seconds** (2 until a waiting call asks for the probe, 2 for the probe), once, and nothing after; it is back within 1 to 3 seconds of answering. | Go, package `db`: `TestNothingWaitsForADatabaseThatIsAway` (a real PostgreSQL behind a relay, cut and made to swallow: six calls given no time of their own return in 2 ms and in 4.0 s; then every kind of call, with a time and without, returns in under 100 ms, measured in microseconds; back 0.5 s after the relay; the listener hears another console's notice again; what waits is told once), `TestASlowQueryDoesNotTakeTheDatabaseAway`, `TestAConnectionCutByTheServerIsNotAnOutage`, `TestHealthFollowsTheDatabase`, `TestTheHealthNeverShowsTheDriversText`; `cmd/221b`: `TestARunningConsoleDoesNotWaitForADatabaseThatIsAway` (DB-15). |
| DB-15 | **The database's state is shown**: `GET /api/v1/database` (configured, available, since; for a SuperAdmin the error, the schema's version and the domains moved) and the metrics `b221_database_up`, `b221_database_schema_version`, `b221_database_connections` and `b221_database_connections_max`, to anyone signed in. **An answer that says the database is unavailable is a 503**, whatever status the handler gave the failure (500, 502, 400), **and carries the header `X-221b-Database: unavailable`**, which is how the browser app tells it from any other refusal (a part that is off answers 503 too, without it). It is known by the error, not by its words: an error writer that has the error answers it first (`db.Refuse`: the issue service's, the cases', the jobs', the fixes', the exceptions' and Mycroft's, a proposed change's, a host's and an account's), so that no rewording on the way out changes it (the tenth cluster test's N1: the issue service wrote the sentence with a capital and a full stop, and `/api/issues/v1/me` and `/settings` answered 500; a proposed change's own routes did the same). Every route registered with the console's API, a service's own among them, passes through one writer, which makes a marked answer a 503 and, for a handler that passes on only an error's text, still holds an error's status until its text is written and goes by `db.ErrUnavailable`'s own sentence in it, marking the answer then. **With the database away every GET route answers 200 (read live, or from memory) or this 503**; none answers 500, 502 or 504. **The sentence is the console's and nothing else** (the ninth cluster test's F4: it carried the driver's text, with the database's user, its name and its Service's address, to the person and into the audit record of a refused change): `the database is unavailable; sign-in and the pages read live from the clusters still work`, after what the page was doing (`reading the credentials: …`). The error a call returns reads as that sentence alone (`db.ErrUnavailable`); the driver's own words are logged, once when a call first fails to reach the database and once when the probe finds it away (the address is in the log, a password never is), and why the database is away is said on `GET /api/v1/database` in the console's words (it refuses connections, it does not answer, it refuses the console's sign-in, or the server's own message for a server that is starting or shutting down). A connection the server refuses to make (a password changed under the console) reads the same way. **A page that needs the database says so in under 2 seconds** once the outage is found (DB-14), where each click took 5 to 19 seconds. **The app says it once, on every page**: a banner under the top bar, *The database is unavailable*, while `GET /api/v1/database` says so (a console that has a database and cannot reach it): sign-in and the pages read live from the clusters still work; what the console keeps in the database (the Inbox, cases and reports, jobs and their runs, Mycroft, Automation, applications, credentials, the audit log and the settings) cannot be read or changed until it is back; since when, and for a SuperAdmin why, in the console's words. It is a polite status like the banners about a cluster, with nothing to focus or dismiss, and goes by itself. The app reads the state with its other shared lists, every half minute, every 10 seconds while the database is away, and at once when any answer carries the header, so the banner is there within the half minute on a page that asks nothing and at the first refused call otherwise, and gone within 10 seconds of the database's return. **The Inbox does not say that nothing is pending when it cannot know** (the tenth cluster test's F3): while any of its lists is refused with that header it says *The Inbox cannot be read while the database is unavailable: what waits for you is not known*, once, above whatever rows it last read and in place of the table's empty state; the Inbox tab and the Inbox's Pending filter show a **?** in place of their count and the bell a **?** with the name *What waits for you is not known: the database is unavailable* (never 0, never no mark, never the last number: a tab without a count and a plain bell both mean that nothing waits); and its lists are read again as soon as the state says the database answers. **`/metrics` asks the database nothing**: every figure is read from memory, so the scrape that says `b221_database_up 0` is answered. The two figures kept elsewhere, `b221_sessions` and Sherlock's spend (`b221_sherlock_spend_today_usd`, `b221_sherlock_spend_month_usd`, `b221_sherlock_spend_unsaved`), are what the console last read: the sessions as last listed (each minute by the sweep), the spend as the ledger last had it, and both asked for again behind a scrape, at most every 15 seconds, never in it. **Bounds, with the database taken away under a running console** (DB-14), by a cut and by a database that swallows what it is sent: `/metrics` answers in under 2 seconds at every moment and says `b221_database_up 0` once the outage is found (at once for a cut, within 4 seconds otherwise, and within 14 with nobody using the console); `GET /api/v1/home` answers in under 3 seconds with every cluster's live figures (Sherlock is then marked unavailable for the cluster, which is all of the home page the database decides); the first request to meet the outage may wait up to 4 seconds, once; and within a few seconds of the database's return every route answers as before, without a restart. **The log says an outage once** (0.13.0; the ninth cluster test logged about 200 lines a minute): what fails at every look while the database is away (the AI configuration's reads, Sherlock's spend, the saved settings, the audit log's saves, each route answered 503 for it, and since the twelfth cluster test Getting started's record, the jobs, the hosts, the proposals' sweep, the Wiki sync jobs' sources, the cases' catch-up with the proposed changes and, since the thirteenth cluster test, the jobs' sweep of the runs a replica left under way) is said at its first failure and then not again until it works, which is said once (*the AI configuration is read again*, *requests are answered again* with the route, *Getting started's record is read again*, *the runs are swept again*); any other error is said each time (`db.Quiet`). | `internal/api/database.go`, `internal/api/server.go` (`statusWriter`), `cmd/221b/database.go`, `cmd/221b/metrics.go`; `TestAnAnswerThatSaysTheDatabaseIsUnavailableIs503`; `TestARunningConsoleDoesNotWaitForADatabaseThatIsAway` (the whole console, as the chart starts it, on a real PostgreSQL behind a relay; measured: the first request 1 ms and 4.0 s, then the home page, a scrape and each page that needs the database in 1 ms; | DB-55 | **A record set leaves its ConfigMap once it is copied** (decided by the owner on November 19, 2026, after the sixth cluster test's F1: a ConfigMap kept as it was at the copy let a console from before the move show changes approved and run since as waiting, and run them again). After a record set's one-time copy has committed and is recorded in `imports`, it leaves where it was kept: since DB-72 (November 20) **a ConfigMap of several record sets is deleted whole once every record set it held is recorded**, and a case's own ConfigMap whole once the cases' copy is (until DB-72 the key was deleted and the object stayed, or was emptied in a store that cannot delete a key). What an earlier build copied and left is deleted at the next start. A copy that fails, or a record set with no row in `imports`, leaves its ConfigMap as it was, every key in it, and the next start tries the copy again. A deletion that fails is logged, does not stop the start, and is tried again at the next. The record sets that only grow (the audit events, the Toolbox's session records) are copied as before, what is newer than the last copy, before their ConfigMap is deleted. | DB-65 | **Sign-in does not need the session store** (to-do 13 of the handover; decided for the owner on November 20, 2026, for him to confirm, that every way of signing in gets it, that it lasts 30 minutes, and that a password reset does not end it early). **The session store** is the table `auth_sessions` when the console has a database and keeps its connections in Secrets (DB-66), and the session Secrets otherwise. **When.** A sign-in that cannot write its session because the session store is *away* gives the person a session kept in its cookie instead. Away is: the store did not answer (for the Secrets, the API server not reached, the call's time run out, or its answer 429 or 5xx; for the database, `db.ErrUnavailable`). A store that answers and refuses (forbidden, a quota, unauthorized, a conflict, invalid, too large) is not away and makes no such session: the sign-in then behaves as it did. One function decides it (`auth.StoreAway`), and a store says it of its own errors. **The cookie.** `221b_session` then holds `s1.<payload>.<mac>` in place of the session's ID: the payload is who the person is as the sign-in's source gave it (subject, user name, email, name, groups, how they signed in, their name in the directory), a random id of the session, and when it was issued and when it ends, as deflated JSON in URL-safe base64; the MAC is HMAC-SHA-256 over `s1.` and the payload, compared in constant time before the payload is opened. A session's ID has no dot, so neither kind of value is ever read as the other. What the person may do is not in it: roles, the SuperAdmin flag and the names the clusters see are worked out again on every request, from the configuration and the account as they are then. **The key** is 32 random bytes, 64 hexadecimal characters under `key` in the Secret `<release>-session-key`, which the console makes at start when there is none (a conditional write, so replicas starting together end with one) and never changes; one that cannot be read as a key is left as it is. A replica reads it again, at most every 30 seconds, when a signature does not verify and before it signs, so a key put back by hand reaches every replica. With `connections.store=memory` the key is the process's own. It is never logged, shown, exported, or put in a backup or the database, and neither is a cookie's value. **How long.** `auth.outageSession` (`-session-outage`, 30 minutes; 1 minute to 24 hours), from sign-in, not renewed by use; the cookie's Max-Age is the same. **Checking.** Any replica takes a signed session whose MAC verifies and whose time has not run out, with the key and the clock alone; the store is asked one thing, and only while it answers: whether it holds the session's ending (below). A value that is not shaped like one costs nothing; one whose MAC does not verify costs at most that one read of the key's Secret in 30 seconds. The session is then one of that replica's memory, marked as not stored, and every check a stored session gets applies: a local account disabled or deleted is refused at once on every replica (within the 5 seconds a replica takes to read the accounts again), a directory person's groups are read again as they age (RB-23) and the session ends when none is listed, and a session of a way of signing in the console does not have is refused. **Ending one early** is possible only where it is known: sign-out clears the cookie and the replica that served it refuses the session until it would have ended; ending a person's sessions (a password reset, *Sign out everywhere*) ends the ones the replica holds. While the store is away another replica takes a copy of the cookie until it ends: there is nowhere to write that it ended. The lifetime is what bounds that, **and the exchange does not lift it** (the ninth cluster test's F1, decided for the owner on November 20, 2026: a session signed out during the outage came back, through the exchange on a replica that did not remember the sign-out, as a stored session of seven days). **The ending is kept in the store** as soon as the store answers: a record under the key of the stored session the signed one is exchanged for, in that session's place, that says the session was ended (`method` is `ended`; whose it was, when it was signed in and until when the ending is kept, which is when the signed session would have expired; no group, no role, and a last activity at the beginning of time, so a console that does not know the mark reads a session that ran out long ago). It is a row of `auth_sessions` with a database and a Secret without one, written by the sign-out or the ending when the store answers and otherwise by the replica that remembers it when the store is back (`db.OnAvailable`, the sweep); no schema step. A replica shown a signed cookie looks for it (a read it holds for 5 seconds, 2 when there is none, and drops when the write is announced), refuses the cookie when it is there, and exchanges nothing; a stored session that may have stood for a signed one (its whole life no longer than the longest a signed session can be set to last, 24 hours) leaves the same record when it is signed out or ended, so the signed cookie it came from is not exchanged again; a write of last activity never replaces the record; nothing lists it, counts it or ends it as a session; and the sweep removes it once the signed session would have expired. What is left of the bound: an ending no replica that lasts remembers (the store away, and the replica that served the sign-out restarted before the store was back) and a session ended on a replica that had never seen it are not known anywhere, and such a cookie is taken until the signed session expires, 30 minutes from sign-in at most, exchanged or not. Whoever can read the key's Secret can sign a session for anyone, which reading the session Secrets never allowed: the console's namespace is its own. **When the store is back** the session's next request, on any replica, starts a stored session for the same person, signed in the same way at the same time, and sets the usual cookie. **The exchange never gives more than the signed session had**: the stored session expires when the signed one would have (`expires_at` is the cookie's `exp`), nothing moves that later (not use, not the write-back of last activity, not the sweep), its cookie's Max-Age is what is left of it, and at that time the person signs in again. The stored session's ID is the key's MAC over the signed session's id, so every replica shown the signed cookie, before or after its exchange, comes to the one stored session and never starts a second. The exchange is an audit event (`session.exchange`: who, and when the session ends; never a cookie). A stream does not exchange; what the page asks by itself does. If the write fails the signed session stands until it ends, and it is tried again. **Size.** A signed session's cookie is at most 3,584 characters (browsers keep about 4 KiB a cookie). A person in so many groups that theirs would be longer is refused, told that a local account can sign in, and no cookie is set. **What the person is told.** After such a sign-in the sign-in page says that the session store was unavailable, how long the session lasts and at what time they will be asked to sign in again (whether or not the store is back before then), with a button on to the page asked for; the audit record of the sign-in says it too. **What a store that is away must not break** (fixed with this): the store's list can fail (`SharedStore.List` returns an error), and a list that failed says of no session that it ended, so the sweep of sessions forgets nothing, the Users page and the metric count what the replica holds, and a session that cannot be asked about counts as alive for Sherlock's sweep of conversations; a sign-out or an account's sessions ended while the store cannot be told are refused on that replica and removed from the store when it can be reached; and a store found away is left alone for 10 seconds, then tried by one request at a time, so one that swallows what it is sent does not hold every click for the length of a timeout. **An exchanged session is what the person is now** (the review of November 23, 2026, finding 1): the stored session the exchange gives, or finds already made, is checked as every session is before the request goes on, so a role taken away since the exchange is gone from a request that shows the signed cookie again, and a disabled or deleted account is refused. A key is filled when its row is written, by the id; a case made after what names it, a known issue, an exception or a service likewise, give their key to what named them before. The step keys what the database holds. **Not keyed**: an exception's finding, run and job, and a case's own finding, run and job: a job's run is written again whole with a new row at each save, its findings with it, and a job has a row only once it is changed. **Unique**: `findings.finding_key` (the case work looks findings up by it) but the empty id, and `cases.case_number` but 0 (a case's object is looked up by its name, its number). The step does not fail on duplicates: a finding's repeated id gets `~2`, `~3` after the oldest's; a repeated case number is set to 0 but on the case whose object its number names. `rcas.rca_key` is not declared unique: nothing looks an RCA up by it, and the RCAs written since wave 6e have none. **Names**: a test case's id in the agents' test runs is `test_case_id` (it was `case_key`, a case's id elsewhere); `case_work.case_number` is dropped, the number read from the case's row. `cases.service_key` keeps its name, as the service's id kept beside the new key, as `known_issue_services.service_key` is. | Go `TestStep0045KeysWhatTheIDsName` (keys filled where the rows are there and not elsewhere, emptied on delete; duplicates marked and numbered 0; the names gone), `TestAKnownIssueKeepsItsRow`, the services', jobs', changes', knowledge's and agents' tests over the database (`TEST_STORE=database`), `TestTheDataModelIsTheSchema`. |
